## Current State (as of 2026-07-02, B11 — REPL closure wrapper uses the canonical ptr ABI)

- **`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` — `emit_repl_fn_with_closure_slot` now calls the canonical `clo_wrap_define`.** A REPL-defined top-level `fn` (e.g. typed bare at the prompt: `fn mk() do 5 end`) is stored as a first-class closure in a persistent slot so later fragments can reference it by bare name — each REPL fragment gets a fresh `ctx`, so `ctx.top_fns`/`ctx.compiled_fns` from the defining fragment are invisible to subsequent ones, and `emit_prev_slot_bridges` is what makes the cross-fragment reference resolve at all, as a bridged local variable rather than a fresh top-level call. That bridged closure is dispatched through `ECallPtr`, which always reads the callee's result as `ptr` regardless of its concrete return type (every other closure-dispatch wrapper in this file honors that ABI via `clo_wrap_define`: scalars tagged `(n<<1)|1`, doubles bitcast into the ptr slot, heap pointers passed through — see its doc comment). `emit_repl_fn_with_closure_slot` instead built its own wrapper inline, declaring the RAW concrete return type (`i64` for an `Int`-returning fn) — commit e709bee9 made every OTHER wrap site use the tagged ptr ABI for exactly this reason, but this one wrapper's comment falsely claimed it was "identical" and cited stale line numbers (12 commits had landed since). The LLVM-declared `i64` signature disagreed with the indirect call site's `call ptr (ptr) %fv(...)`; the caller's conditional untag then reinterpreted the raw bits as tagged and `ashr`'d them whenever they were odd — halving odd `Int` results (`5` → `2`). Fixed: deleted the inline wrapper, call `clo_wrap_define` directly (its output is appended to `ctx.extra_fns`, matching every other call site). Also fixed a sibling bug this uncovered: `emit_repl_fn_with_closure_slot`'s final IR buffer never included `ctx.extra_fns`, so `clo_wrap_define`'s output — landing in that buffer — would have been silently dropped, turning `@<fn>$clo_wrap` into an undefined symbol; `Buffer.add_buffer out ctx.extra_fns` added before returning. New test `test/test_codegen.ml` "B11: stored closure returns untagged Int" (`repl_jit_cross_line` group): defines `fn mk() do 5 end` via `run_decl ~is_fn_decl:true` (the real `DFn` REPL path), then calls bare `mk()` from a second fragment — pre-fix this returned `"2"` (confirmed via `MARCH_KEEP_LL=1` IR inspection: `define i64 @mk$clo_wrap` dispatched through `call ptr (ptr) %fv(...)`, tag-test-and-`ashr` on the raw odd `5`); post-fix `define ptr @mk$clo_wrap` matches the call site and returns `"5"`. Review follow-up (same commit): the new `clo_wrap_define` call is guarded by the same `ctx.emitted_wraps` check-then-add the other two call sites use — the fn is registered in `ctx.top_fns` before `emit_fn` runs, so a body referencing ITSELF as a first-class value (`let g = selfref`) already emits the wrapper via emit_atom's top-fns wrap path, and an unconditional second emission was a duplicate LLVM symbol in one fragment (clang: "invalid redefinition of function 'selfref$clo_wrap'", reproduced RED). Second test "B11: self-referencing fn no duplicate clo_wrap": `fn selfref(n) do let g = selfref; if n > 0 do g(0) else 7 end end` then `selfref(1)` = `"7"`; IR confirmed exactly one `define ptr @selfref$clo_wrap` referenced by both the body's closure alloc and the slot init. **383 compiler / 226 eval / 334 codegen (+2) / 788 stdlib pass (incl. Slow: 6 JIT/interpreter parity + 5 compiled adversarial regression + 2 pbkdf2 tests) / 53 stdlib_march pass. `bench/list_ops.march` compiled+run unaffected (this code path is REPL-only; never reached by the ahead-of-time `--compile` pipeline).**

## Current State (as of 2026-07-02, B12 — `ctx.type_defs` replaces stale `cur_type_defs` global)

- **`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` — `ctx` gains a `type_defs : Tir.type_def list` field.** The module-level `cur_type_defs` ref was assigned exactly once, in `emit_module`; the REPL/fragment entry points (`emit_repl_expr`, `emit_repl_decl`, `emit_repl_fn`, `emit_repl_fn_with_closure_slot`, `emit_fns_fragment`) all receive a `types` parameter but never set the global, so every niche/newtype representation decision (`EAlloc`, `EReuse`, `emit_case`, `ensure_adt_eq_fn` — all via `Repr.is_niche_shaped`/`niche_payload_ok`/`repr_of_ty`/`payload_needs_tag`) ran against a stale-or-empty table in JIT fragments — the same failure family as the fixed cross-module `Option` repr bug. Mechanical fix: `make_ctx` gained an optional `?(type_defs=[])` param populated at all 6 construction sites from each entry point's own `types`/`m.tm_types`; every `!cur_type_defs` read became `ctx.type_defs`; the global and its lone `emit_module` assignment were deleted. Reproduced the pre-fix bug as a real SIGSEGV (not just a wrong-value assertion): a pure REPL/JIT-only process never calls `emit_module` at all, so `cur_type_defs` sits at `[]` for the whole session and both fragments agree (if accidentally) — no observable split. The RED required simulating an in-process `--compile` (the actual trigger, since compile and REPL share one executable): call `emit_module` on the ADT module first (populating the then-live global so `EAlloc` niche-encodes `Some(7)` as a bare tagged scalar), then let the global fall back to `[]` before the second REPL fragment's `emit_case` runs — `is_niche_shaped [] "Opt" = false` makes it load a ctor tag at offset 8 of what is actually a tagged scalar → SIGSEGV, reproduced 5/5 runs pre-fix, gone post-fix. New test `test/test_codegen.ml` "B12: niche ADT cross-fragment" (`repl_jit_cross_line` group) exercises a `:load`-style `DMod` (niche-eligible `Opt = None | Some(Int)` + producer fn) in fragment 1, matched by an expression in fragment 2, via `register_module_decl`/`run_expr` exactly as `lib/repl/repl.ml` drives real REPL sessions. Also fixed a pre-existing, unrelated test-infra gap discovered while chasing the RED: `test/test_helpers.ml`'s `setup_jit_runtime` was missing `march_dispatch.c`/`march_reload.c`/`march_remote_registry.c`/`march_monitor_registry.c` (added since Hot Code Reload phases 2–10), so every clang-gated JIT test — including the 6 `repl_compiler_parity` Slow-tier tests — silently no-op'd via the `None` skip branch instead of running; added the 4 missing `.c` files (no openssl/zlib needed, since `march_http.c`/`march_tls.c`/`march_compress.c` are deliberately not linked). **383 compiler / 226 eval / 332 codegen (+1) / 788 stdlib pass (incl. the 6 JIT/interpreter parity tests, now actually executing instead of skipping) / 53 stdlib_march pass. `bench/tree_transform.march` compiled+run unaffected (EAlloc/EReuse fast paths unchanged in behavior for the single-module compile path).**

## Current State (as of 2026-07-02, dotted-sibling module dependency ordering fix)

- **`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` — `module_refs_in_decls` records all dotted prefixes.** Fixes order-dependent monomorphization of functions in dotted sibling modules (`forge forgepm.seed` reported "expected: {ApiKey fields} but got: {audit_logs fields}" for every `Depot.Schema.define` call after the first, while `forge build/check` passed). The whole-program combine emits auto-discovered modules in MARCH_LIB_PATH order; `forge <task>` (archive run_task) builds that path own-lib-first, so `mod Depot.Schema` landed AFTER its callers. `dependency_order_dmod_run` exists to topo-sort sibling DMods definition-before-use, but its edge collector `module_refs_in_decls` kept only the FIRST dotted segment of a reference ("Depot" — an empty namespace container — from `EVar "Depot.Schema.define"`), so a dotted sibling never received a dependency edge. Unordered, every caller unified against the single shared pass-1 `Mono (fresh_var 1)` placeholder for the unannotated fn and the first call site pinned the parameter record types for the rest. `add` now records every dotted prefix ("Depot" AND "Depot.Schema"); the existing `StringSet.inter name_set` drops non-module prefixes, so extra edges are free. Same `add` serves `TyCon`/`ECon`/`EVar`, so dotted type/ctor references gain edges too. Regression test in `test/test_compiler.ml` ("dotted sibling module order": two callers with different record args declared before the dotted sibling that defines the shared fn). **373 compiler / 224 eval / 323 codegen / 788 stdlib pass.**

## Current State (as of 2026-07-01, P0 pipeline-review fixes — differential oracle, dual-position RC, FBIP arity)

Three P0 items from the 2026-07-01 pipeline deep review, in order:

- **Differential oracle no longer skips compiled crashes** (`test/test_properties.ml` `oracle_check`). A signal-killed compiled binary (`sh` exit ≥ 128: SIGABRT=134, SIGSEGV=139, …) when the interpreter succeeded is now reported as a FAILURE (`Some (Error …)` with the signal number) instead of `None`/skip. Clean nonzero error exits (`process_exit`, runtime panics, `timeout`'s 124) remain skips. Every RC miscompile that aborts — including both bugs below — was previously invisible to the property oracle.
- **Perceus dual-position owned+borrowed double-consumption** (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`, EApp + ECallPtr-extern sites). A variable passed at BOTH an owned and a borrowed argument position of the same call and dead afterwards got 0 dups from `find_inc_vars` (which only inspects owned positions: `count-1 = 0`) *plus* a borrowed-position post-call `EDecRC` from `post_dec_vars` — two consumptions of the caller's single reference (the owned position already transfers it) → RC underflow / use-after-free (`both(a:own, b:borrow, n)` called as `both(s, s, 1)` aborted exit 134 compiled; interpreter fine). Fix at both duplicated sites: for a normal call, keep the post-dec and add ONE balancing `EIncRC` (also keeps the value alive across the whole call even if the callee consumes its owned param before the last read of the borrowed alias); for a SELF call, drop the post-dec instead (TCO turns the trailing `ESeq`'d dec into dead code, so a balancing inc would leak once per iteration).
- **FBIP `same_arity` no longer conflates type-parameter count with constructor field count** (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`). `same_arity (TCon(_, ts)) nfields = (length ts = nfields)` approved reusing a dead binding's cell based on its declared type's PARAMETER count — e.g. a dead 1-field `Small(n) : Holder(Int,Int,Int,Int,Int)` cell (5 type params) reused for the 5-field `Big` constructor wrote 4 fields past the 24-byte cell, clobbering the neighbouring heap chunk (compiled binary printed the wrong match arm; interpreter correct). The arity encoding minted by `add_scrutinee_free_for` (where the real field count IS known, from the branch's bound vars) now carries an unforgeable `$fbip$` marker prefix, and `same_arity` accepts ONLY marked encodings — reuse is refused when the constructor arity can't be verified (a dead binding may hold any ctor of its type, each with its own arity). Audited `Borrow.has_matching_alloc` (`lib/tir/borrow.ml`): it compares only type-name prefixes to gate an OWNERSHIP decision (never a reuse size), so it has no arity conflation.

Regression tests: compiled Slow guards in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` ("dual-position owned+borrowed arg both(s,s,1)…", "FBIP same_arity: dead 1-field cell NOT reused for 5-field ctor…") assert interpreter/compiled output parity AND exit 0; `fbip_p8` unit tests updated to the `$fbip$` encoding + new `same_arity_raw_type_refused` case in `test/test_codegen.ml`. **372 compiler / 224 eval / 323 codegen / 790 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 36 properties pass (`scripts/run-tests.sh` green). RC benchmarks unchanged: tree_transform 104857600 in 7.8s (baseline 7.9s), list_ops 333333666666, binary_trees depth-15 check 65535; FBIP `reuse` on the scrutinee path intact (845 reuses in tree_transform).**
## Current State (as of 2026-07-01, MARCH_SANITIZE exit-abort fix — runtime keeps ASAN's sigaltstack)

- **`runtime/march_scheduler.c` — `setup_alt_stack()` is a no-op under ASAN.** MARCH_SANITIZE=1 binaries on macOS arm64 printed correct output then aborted at process exit (SIGTRAP, exit 133) with `AddressSanitizer failed to deallocate 0x10000 bytes ... (error code: 22)`. lldb backtrace pinned it: `_pthread_exit → _pthread_tsd_cleanup → AsanThread::Destroy → UnsetAlternateSignalStack → UnmapOrDie`. ASAN installs an mmap'd alternate signal stack on every thread and, at thread exit, `munmap()`s whatever altstack is then current; the scheduler replaced it with a **malloc'd** (non-page-aligned) 64 KiB buffer, so the munmap failed with EINVAL and ASAN CHECK-aborted. Under `__has_feature(address_sanitizer)` / `__SANITIZE_ADDRESS__` the runtime now keeps ASAN's altstack — it is SA_ONSTACK-capable and serves the lazy stack-growth SIGSEGV handler (verified: 2000-frame non-tail recursion grows the 4 KiB initial stack and exits 0 under ASAN; 200k frames still SIGBUSes past the 1 MiB `MARCH_STACK_MAX` cap in plain AND sanitized builds alike, by design). Non-sanitized builds are behavior-identical (change is preprocessed out). Regression test (compiled, Slow) in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`: "MARCH_SANITIZE binary exits 0 (ASAN altstack teardown, macOS arm64)" — verified red (exit 133) without the fix, green with it. Also fixed fresh-checkout builds: `lib/ed25519/dune` gained `(extra_deps %{project_root}/runtime/tweetnacl.h)` — the foreign stubs' `-I .../runtime` pointed into `_build`, where the header was never copied (the main repo built only via a stale `_build`). **789 stdlib / 372 compiler / 224 eval / 322 codegen / 53 stdlib_march pass.**

## Current State (as of 2026-06-29, monomorphization fix — record-field projection through a row-poly helper)

- **`lib/tir/mono.ml` — `Mono.refine_field_types` + `match_ty` `TRecord` case.** Fixes a compiled-only crash on DB-backed HTTP routes (forgepm publish/home; the `specs/2026-06-29-perceus-tuple-projection-rc-bug.md` repros). A generic helper that projects a record field carrying a polymorphic element and feeds it to another generic function (`get_req_header(conn, name) = lookup(conn.hdrs, name)`) typed `conn` as a row-polymorphic var, so the projection `conn.hdrs` got a fresh type var unrelated to `conn`. Monomorphising `conn → Conn` left the projection's binding (and thus the callee's value type) abstract, so `Option('V)` was emitted **Boxed** while the concrete caller read it as a **Niche** (`Some(x) ≡ x`) — the Some-box pointer was dereferenced as the payload String (SIGSEGV in `march_string_concat`/`string_split_first`). `refine_field_types` runs after `subst_fn_def` and before `rewrite_calls`: for every `let v = a.fld` with `a` a concrete record, it retypes `v` to the field type and propagates it to `v`'s uses, so callees specialise on the concrete type (`lookup$List_T_String_String$String`, niche `Option`). `match_ty` gained the missing `TRecord` case (matches field types by name, like the existing `TTuple`/`TCon`/`TFn` cases). The mis-attribution to a Perceus RC under-count (the original spec) was wrong — no Perceus/lower change was needed. Regression test in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` (compiled, Slow): "record-field poly projection: Option niche/box repr consistent". **789 stdlib / 372 compiler / 224 eval / 322 codegen pass; RC benchmarks (tree_transform/binary_trees/list_ops) unchanged (FBIP `reuse` intact).**

## Current State (as of 2026-06-26, pattern guard path threading)

- **`lib/refinecheck/division_safety.ml` — guard-aware path context.** `iter_div_sites` now carries a `path : (A.expr * bool) list` through EMatch arms (`when` guards accumulate as `(g, false)` conditions), EIf branches, and ECond arms. New `path_proves_nonzero` syntactically checks whether any accumulated guard (e.g. `when b != 0`, `when b > 0`) implies the divisor is non-zero. `check_var_divisor` uses this: unrefined variable divisors inside a `when b != 0` guard no longer produce errors; refined-param cases also include path conditions as additional Z3 assumptions.
- **`lib/refinecheck/return_infer.ml` — multi-position return inference.** New `return_positions` traverses `EBlock`, `EIf`, `ECond`, and `EMatch` (with `branch_guard`) to collect *all* leaf return positions with their accumulated path contexts — mirrors `tails` in `refine_check.ml`. `infer_clause` rewritten to probe each position with its guard-derived assumptions added to the context, then intersect the verified predicates across all positions. Let-binding context from the outer block is preserved. This means `abs(x) : {v != 0}` now infers `r > 0` via the two-arm intersection, and `fn f(x : {v >= 0}) : Int do match x do x when x > 0 -> x | _ -> 1 end end` infers `r > 0`.
- **Test coverage (+16 tests).** `test_refinecheck.ml`: new `guard-path-sensitivity` suite (4 cases): `when n >= 0` discharges `take_n(n)` call-site VC; `when n < 0` contradicts it (error); `when n >= 0` discharges `_ >= 0` postcondition; literal -1 in unguarded arm still violates postcondition. `test_compiler.ml`: 5 new `divsafety` guard tests (match guard `b != 0` / `b > 0`; if guard `b != 0` / `b > 0` for modulo; no guard still errors); 3 new `return_refine_infer` guard tests (match-arm guard infers `r > 0` for both arms; disagreeing arms kill `r > 0` in intersection; if-guard `abs` infers `r > 0`); `if body: no crash` test updated to new semantics (preds now inferred). **341 compiler tests pass; 224 eval / 320 codegen / 58 refinecheck unchanged.**

> **Authoritative test counts (2026-06-26): 341 compiler / 224 eval / 320 codegen / 58 refinecheck.** +8 compiler guard tests, +4 refinecheck guard tests; eval and codegen unchanged.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-26, HCR Phase 9 — epoch-tagged dispatch)

- **HCR Phase 8 — coordinated upgrade gate.** `.hcr_manifest` sidecar gained `callers:` annotations per exported function (pre-opt TIR scan in `bin/main.ml` builds a reverse boundary-call index). Server stores caller set per slot via `callers_str` field in `MarchDispatchSlot` (`runtime/march_dispatch.{h,c}`). `ACTIVATE` command parses optional `callers:` suffix and stores it via `march_dispatch_set_callers`; `ABI_QUERY` responses include `callers:` when set. `forge deploy hot` (`forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`): hard sig_hash rejection replaced with check — if all server-recorded callers of the changed function are in `to_activate`, allow the deploy (prints "Coordinated upgrade: sig_hash changed, all callers covered."); otherwise errors naming uncovered callers.
- **HCR Phase 9 — epoch-tagged dispatch.** Compiler emits `@__march_hcr_epoch = private global i32 0` (file-static cell) and an exported `@__march_init(i32)` init function into every `--compile-so` output (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`). `GET_EPOCH` server protocol command (`runtime/march_reload.c`) returns a monotonic epoch number assigned per deploy batch; after `dlopen`, the server calls `dlsym(handle,"__march_init")` to stamp the epoch into the loaded `.so`. Boundary call sites in `.so` mode call `march_dispatch_enter_gen(NAME_ID, epoch, &v)` which routes to the newest ring slot whose epoch ≤ caller_epoch — old deployed code naturally calls the old function version until it is redeployed. Phase 8 hard block on sig changes with missing callers downgrades to advisory warning when Phase 9 is active. Files: `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, `runtime/march_dispatch.c`, `runtime/march_reload.c`, `forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-25, HCR Phase 7 — fleet tooling P1–P6)

- **Audit log (`runtime/march_reload.c`).** `write_audit_log` + `pubkey_to_hex` helper. Every ACTIVATE (sig pass or fail) appends one JSON line to `$MARCH_AUDIT_LOG` (default `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-$HOME/.local/share}/march/audit.jsonl`). Fields: `ts/type/fn/impl_hash/signer/cas_hash/result`. Results: `ok | err_sig | err_abi | err_cas_miss | err_dlopen`.
- **`VERSIONS_DETAIL` protocol command (`runtime/march_reload.c`, `runtime/march_dispatch.{h,c}`).** New response format: `SLOT <id> <name> <impl_hash> <activated_at_ms> <signer_hex>`. Backed by `activated_at_ms : long long` + `signer_hex[65]` per-slot fields in `MarchDispatchSlot`. `march_dispatch_set_activation` called after each successful publish; `march_dispatch_activated_at`/`march_dispatch_signer_hex` getters.
- **`forge hot-reload status` (`forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`).** Opens SSH tunnel, sends `VERSIONS_DETAIL`, parses `detail_slot` list, prints a dashboard table (Function / impl_hash / activated / signer columns). `format_elapsed_ms` converts Unix ms timestamps to human-readable "Xs ago / Xm ago / never (baseline)".

- **`forge hot-reload keygen --rotate` + `use-key` (`forge/lib/cmd_hot_reload.ml`).** `keygen --rotate` generates versioned `ed25519_secret_v<N>.key` (scans `~/.march/` for highest existing N, uses N+1). `use-key <path>` copies the chosen key to the canonical `~/.march/ed25519_secret.key`.
- **Multi-env fan-out + `--canary` (`forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`, `forge/lib/project.ml`, `forge/lib/toml.ml`).** TOML parser extended to handle `[[array-of-tables]]` (double-bracket sections). `project.ml`: new `hot_reload_env` type + `hr_envs : hot_reload_env list` field on `hot_reload_config`. `deploy_env`: resolves server list from `[[hot-reload.env]]` filtered by `--env <name>`; sequential fan-out via `deploy_one`; with `--canary <n>`: deploys to first n servers, polls PING every 2s for `--timeout` ms, rolls out to rest on healthy, aborts on failure.
- **`forge deploy hot` CLI (`forge/bin/main.ml`).** New flags: `--env <name>`, `--canary <n>`, `--timeout <ms>`.
- **`forge hot-reload` CLI.** New subcommands: `status`, `init`, `use-key`; `keygen` gains `--rotate`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-24, HCR Phase 5 — actor state migration)

- **Runtime primitives (`runtime/march_runtime.{h,c}`).** `MARCH_MIGRATE_TAG` sentinel (`0x4D494752L`); `march_migrate_msg_t` (malloc/free, not RC); `dispatch_name_id` uint32 field in `march_actor_meta`; `march_actor_set_dispatch_id`; `march_actor_broadcast_migrate` (two-phase: snapshot under `g_tbl_mu` + `march_incrc`, release lock, inject `MARCH_MIGRATE_TAG` messages, `march_decrc`). `actor_green_thread` dispatches MIGRATE before the alive gate, calls `migrate_fn`, swaps state ptr atomically. Dispatch-table path for hot-reload actors via `dispatch_name_id`.
- **State indirection in codegen (`lib/tir/lower.ml`).** `--hot-reload` actor structs replace the flat state-fields list with a single `$f_state: TCon(State,[])` pointer at field 2. Handlers load state from the indirection pointer; reuse builds an updated inner record and writes back the new ptr; spawn passes the initial state record directly. `lower_module` takes `?(hot_reload=false)`.
- **`__migrate_*` alias + spawn wrapper (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`).** Functions named `*_migrate_state` get an `@__migrate_<actor>` LLVM alias so the reload server can `dlsym` it. Spawn functions in hot-reload mode are emitted as `*_spawn_impl` + a thin wrapper that also calls `march_actor_set_dispatch_id` with the compile-time slot ID.

- **`@compat` annotation (`lib/ast/ast.ml`, `lib/parser/parser.mly`).** `actor_def.actor_compat: string` field (default `"full"`). Parser extended with `fn_attr` rule supporting `@compat(forward|full|any)` on actor declarations. `collect_actor_compat` walks desugared AST to build name→compat map for `.schemas.json` emission.
- **Deploy-time schema enforcement (`forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`).** Loads prev/new schemas, calls `Schema_diff.check_compat`; aborts if incompatible before sending ACTIVATE. ACTIVATE payload extended with `migrate_required` (0|1) from `Schema_diff.requires_migration`. Saves new schemas as prev baseline after successful deploy.
- **ACTIVATE extension (`runtime/march_reload.c`).** Parses 5th `migrate_required` field; when set, dlsyms `__migrate_<actor>` from the new `.so` and calls `march_actor_broadcast_migrate`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-24, RRB.Vec v2 + atomic RC in scheduler threads)

> **Authoritative test counts (2026-06-24): 321 compiler / 224 eval / 786 stdlib / 49 stdlib_march.** No new tests added; fixes are in runtime and stdlib internals.

- **`stdlib/rrb_vec.march` v2 — Array-backed (32-way trie)** — `ptype Vec(a) = Vec(Array(a))` / `ptype Slice(a) = Slice(Array(a), Int, Int)`. All public API preserved; `push` is now O(1) amortised (was O(n) list rebuild), `get` is O(log₃₂ n), `slice`/`chunk` are O(1) zero-copy views sharing the underlying `Array`. `fold` iterates `[lo, hi)` via `Array.get` without allocating. `to_list`/`to_array`/`map`/`each`/`fold_left`/`range`/`tabulate`/`concat`/`from_list`/`from_array` all rewritten to use `Array` module. Private helpers `imax`/`imin`.
- **`stdlib/array.march` — off-by-5 trie push fix** — `descend` passed `s` instead of `s - 5` when computing the branch slot for an existing non-leaf node; produced wrong trie structure on larger pushes. Fixed `trie_slot(leaf_idx, s - 5)` in the `TrieBranch` arm.
- **`runtime/march_runtime.c` — atomic RC in scheduler worker threads** — `march_incrc_local`/`march_decrc_local` now delegate to `march_incrc`/`march_decrc` (atomic) when `march_sched_in_scheduler()` is true. Multiple tasks spawned by `Parallel.preduce_n` share the same `Array` backing RRB chunks; non-atomic RC ops on a shared heap object across 4 OS scheduler workers caused data races (RC corruption → segfault or "local RC underflow" abort). Root cause: `chunk(v, n)` zero-copy creates `n` Slices all pointing to the same `arr`; each task's `go$apply` calls `march_incrc_local(arr)` before `Array.get` on 4 different OS threads simultaneously.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-28, Toml compiled-RC bug cluster — 3 compiler bugs + tuple-field-escape)

> **Authoritative test counts (2026-06-28): 372 compiler / 224 eval / 320 codegen / 787 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march.** +1 compiled stdlib regression test (Toml 4-key section); +1 eval test group (test_toml.march "Section with many keys"). Benchmarks (tree_transform, list_ops, binary_trees) leak-neutral.

- **Toml `get_str` returned wrong values for 4+-key sections when COMPILED — three independent compiler RC/codegen bugs (all fixed).** Parsing a `[section]` with 4+ keys then calling `Toml.get_str` returned a sibling key's NAME (or crashed with OOM / RC underflow), only in the compiled (LLVM) backend; the interpreter was always correct. This blocked compiled `forge.toml` parsing. Bugs:
  1. **TCO mis-applied to non-tail self-calls (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`).** TCO was a whole-function transform: once enabled (any tail self-call present), the loop back-edge fired for EVERY self `EApp`, including a NON-tail one inside `Cons(x, f(t))` (an ELet rhs after ANF) — silently discarding the surrounding construction and miscompiling to wrong results. Fix: a `tco_in_tail` flag on the emit ctx, set true at the TCO function-body entry and cleared while emitting ELet rhs / ESeq prefixes; the self-call back-edge is now gated on it, so non-tail self-calls emit an ordinary `call`.
  2. **Niche/newtype scrutinee double-free (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`).** For niche- (`Some(x)≡x`, `None≡0`) and newtype- (`S(x)≡x`) represented scrutinees the bound payload IS the scrutinee object, so `add_scrutinee_free_for`'s separate dec double-freed it (toml `get_str`'s `Some(TStr(s))`). Fix: skip the scrutinee free when `Repr.repr_of_ty` of the scrutinee type is `Newtype`/`Niche`.
  3. **Borrow inference over-owned linear-search helpers (`lib/tir/borrow.ml`).** `owned_in` treated a pure alias binding `let p = name` (how `Lower` materialises destructured fields, frequently behind sibling field / join-point-closure bindings) as an *owning* use of `name`. So `table_get`/`tget` (which return an extracted element) were inferred `pairs:own`; consuming the list, they freed the spine/elements that `set_nested`/`table_has` then reused. Fix: `owned_in`'s `ELet` case flows ownership through the alias (`owned_in v body`) instead of treating the alias rhs as owning. Honours shadowing.
  - Fixing (3) exposed a latent gap: an escaping field of a borrowed-derived tuple was not inc'd, and a dead tuple-field `dec_rc` corrupted a borrowed list. Because `needs_rc(TTuple/TRecord)=false` (the aggregate is never RC-freed), its fields belong to the persistent aggregate — so `TTuple`/`TRecord` scrutinees are now treated as borrowed in `insert_rc_expr` (fields inc'd on escape, never dec'd as dead). Empirically leak-neutral (dropped vs used heap field: identical RSS).
- **Also fixed: stale `test/stdlib/test_dataframe.march`** (blocked `forge test`). Commit e1e5b589 migrated `IntCol`/`FloatCol`/`Nullable{Int,Float}Col` to `NativeIntArr`/`NativeFloatArr` but missed this test file. Updated the data constructors to `native_int_arr_from_list`/`native_float_arr_from_list` and the destructured-field reads to `native_int_arr_get` (null bitmaps stay `typed_array_from_list`).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-24, Perceus dead br_var RC leak + task_await_unwrap double-untag)

> **Authoritative test counts (2026-06-24): 321 compiler / 224 eval / 786 stdlib / 49 stdlib_march.** No new tests added; both bugs were in runtime codegen.

- **Perceus dead br_var EDecRC leak fixed (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`)** — Wildcard-bound constructor fields in pattern matches (e.g. `PVec(n, _, _, _) -> n`) now get `EDecRC` emitted when they are heap-typed and never used in the branch body. Previously the shared (RC > 1) path's `inc_rc` on those fields had no matching `dec_rc`, causing permanent RC inflation (eventually RC underflow crash in Perceus-managed code using `Array.length`, `Array.is_empty`, etc.). Also populates `_var_ctx` with br_vars so nested cross-branch EDecRC can find their types.
- **`task_await_unwrap` double-untag fixed (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`)** — For `i64` (integer) task results, the thunk trampoline stores `4*n+3` (doubly tagged). One `ptr→i64` coerce yielded `2*n+1` (still tagged). A second conditional `ashr 1` now recovers the raw `n`. Without this fix all integer-valued parallel reductions (`psum`, `preduce`, `pcount`) returned `2×correct + N_workers`.
- **`rrb_vec.march` and `parallel.march` registered in `stdlib_file_list` (`bin/main.ml`)** — These modules were added to the stdlib directory but missing from the compiler's hardcoded load list, causing "Unknown module `RRB`" errors in compiled programs.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-24, HashMap stdlib module + O(n) Enum.uniq/frequencies)

- **`stdlib/hash_map.march`** — new HAMT-backed persistent map using structural `==` and the built-in polymorphic `hash` function. No comparator anywhere in the API. Full public interface: `new`, `put`, `get`, `get_or`, `has`, `delete`, `size`, `fold`, `keys`, `values`, `entries`, `from_list`, `to_list`, `update`, `map_values`, `filter`, `merge`, `merge_with`. Self-contained HAMT with private `HEntry`/`HamtHashMap` types; does not depend on `hamt.march`. `hash_map.march` wired into `bin/main.ml` (after `set.march`), `test/test_stdlib_march.ml` (stdlib load list + `hash_map` test group), `test/test_helpers.ml` (`hash_map_decl` lazy val + wired into `eval_with_enum`). 40 tests in `test/stdlib/test_hash_map.march` (TDD, covers all public API including collision/stress paths).
- **`Enum.uniq` / `Enum.uniq_by` — O(n) rewrite** — previously O(n²) via linear `member`/`member_key` list scan; now O(n) average via `HashMap` membership. Output order (first occurrence) preserved.
- **`Enum.frequencies` — O(n) rewrite** — previously O(n²) quadratic list-scan rebuild; now O(n) average via `HashMap` counting + separate `order` list to preserve first-appearance output order. `frequencies_ord` (comparator-based, O(n log n)) unchanged.
- **Docs updated**: `docs/stdlib.md` (HashMap reference section), `docs/cookbook/basics.md` (counter pattern, merge, use-vs-Map guide).
- **Test counts (2026-06-24):** compiler 321 / eval 224 / stdlib (run_stdlib.exe) unchanged / stdlib_march 49 tests (was 47 + 2 new hash_map group). All suites green.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-23, node_rpc interpreter gap closed — remote_check/remote_invoke eval builtins)

- **`remote_check` / `remote_invoke` registered as interpreter builtins (`lib/eval/eval.ml`).** `stdlib/node_rpc.march`'s `handle_request` falls back to the remote registry (`remote_check` then `remote_invoke`) when a target isn't in the injected `Targets` table. Those were codegen-only builtins, so under the interpreter the call hit `unbound variable: remote_check` — the long-standing `test_stdlib_march` 46/47 failure. The interpreter has no registry (`remote_register_stub`/`remote_count` are deliberate no-ops — real dispatch is the compiled path), so the eval builtins are consistent with an empty registry: `remote_check` → `0` (NoTarget), `remote_invoke` → `None`. This is exactly what `march_remote_check_march` returns on an empty registry, and matches the "unknown target yields NoTarget" test; real RPC dispatch in eval still flows through the `Targets` table, not this fallback.
- **`test_stdlib_march` is now 47/47** (the `node_rpc` `unbound variable: remote_check` gap, carried across this whole arc, is closed). Full suite green — compiler 321, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-23, capacity-aware routing demo — end-to-end cross-host)

- **`demo/load_server.march` + `demo/load_client.march`** complete the original capacity-routing goal: a client queries each cluster node's live CPU/memory headroom over RPC (`report_load`, registered via the `__rpc_stub` mechanism), picks the node with the most spare CPU via `ClusterLoad.pick_by`, and routes the expensive call (`square`) there. Depends on every fix from this arc: the resource builtins, the `ClusterLoad` API, the niche-match codegen hardening, and — critically — the stable cross-binary RPC impl_hash (without it `report_load`, a non-trivial body, failed admission).
- **Cross-host verified against a real droplet.** `load_server` cross-compiled for `linux/amd64` and run on a 1-core droplet; the Mac client queried both nodes over an `ssh -L` tunnel and routed correctly:
  ```
  node A (Mac):     cpus=14 spare_cpu=-38364 mem_avail_mb=8052
  node B (droplet): cpus=1  spare_cpu=-2266  mem_avail_mb=324
  ==> routing square to node B (most spare CPU)   →   square(7)=49 executed on the droplet
  ```
  The Mac is more oversubscribed (load ~52 on 14 cores) than the idle droplet (load ~3 on 1 core), so the client routes CPU-bound work to the droplet — real cross-host headroom, decision by the user-supplied selector.
- **Demo naming footgun fixed:** the client's `connect` helper collided with `Socket.connect`'s unqualified name (Socket's own callers resolve bare `connect`), mis-routing and hanging; renamed to `dial`. (The underlying unqualified-name cross-module collision is a separate compiler sharp edge.)
- **Smoke test** `scripts/rpc/route-smoke.sh`: two local nodes; asserts the client queries both over RPC, routes, and runs `square(7)=49`.
- **Verification.** Full suite green (compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786; RPC + niche native tests pass). `test_stdlib_march` 46/47 (pre-existing `node_rpc` `remote_check` eval gap).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-23, RPC CAS impl_hash stable across separate compilations)

- **`lib/cas/serialize.ml` — alpha-normalized variable serialization (format v2).** The impl_hash serializer wrote generated variable names verbatim (`write_var`), so bodies with desugar/lower-introduced temps (`$t<n>`, `$f<n>`, `$lam<n>`) hashed differently across compilations — the counter depends on surrounding code. Bound variables (function params, `let`/branch/`letrec` binders) are now written by a binding INDEX assigned in deterministic traversal order; a variable occurrence is its binder's index if in scope, else its name (a free reference — a top-level/monomorphized callee in `EApp`, or a global — whose name is a stable cross-binary identity). `impl_hash` is now invariant to temp-name counters. Signatures (`sig_hash`) were already name-free.
- **`bin/main.ml` — uniform RPC admission hashes.** The maps feeding `remote_ref_hashes` constant-folding and the `@main march_remote_register` calls were taken from the post-opt SCC hashes (`h_sccs`) for *live* functions but from pre-opt `hash_fn_def` for dead-code-eliminated ones — so a function live on the server and eliminated on the caller got two different hashes (different stage AND algorithm). A new `rpc_impl_hashes`/`rpc_sig_hashes` pair is now built uniformly from the **pre-opt** TIR via `hash_fn_def` for ALL functions and passed to `emit_module` (separately from the HCR `hr_impl_hashes`, which keeps the CAS/artifact-cache scheme). Both client and server therefore hash the same pre-opt body with the same algorithm.
- **Result:** RPC admission now succeeds across separate client/server compilations for **non-trivial** function bodies (those calling stdlib functions), not just leaf arithmetic. Verified: two separately-compiled modules with an identical `probe` body that calls `ClusterLoad`/`List`/`Msgpack` — one keeping it live via a `__rpc_stub` (formerly post-opt), one eliminating it with padding fns that perturb the temp counter (formerly pre-opt) — now produce an identical `probe` impl_hash (`2932edd4…`). A real `report_load` RPC over TCP returns the remote node's metrics instead of hanging. The NoTarget path was verified to already return an error to the caller (`err_reply`), not hang.
- **Regression test** (`test/native/rpc_hash_live.march` + `rpc_hash_elim.march`, `test/dune`): compiles both modules and diffs their printed `probe` impl_hash — must be equal. This unblocks the capacity-aware routing demo (a client querying a remote node's `ClusterLoad` over RPC and routing the expensive call to the node with most headroom).
- **Verification.** Full suite green — compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786; existing RPC native tests (`rpc_auto_enroll`, `node_call_loopback`) and the niche `lazy_niche` test all pass. `test_stdlib_march` 46/47 (the 1 failure is the pre-existing `node_rpc` `unbound variable: remote_check` eval gap, orthogonal). Note: a *malformed* (undecodable) request frame still cannot be replied to — no correlation to answer — so a real recv timeout/deadline in `NodeCall.recv_reply` remains a separate robustness item.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-23, codegen hardening — niche match on unresolved scrutinee type)

- **`emit_case` recovers the niche match strategy when the scrutinee type is unresolved (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`).** A scrutinee typed `TVar "_"` — e.g. a lazily-loaded stdlib module body (one NOT in `stdlib_file_list`) whose expressions were never type-inferred — made `Repr.repr_of_ty` return `Boxed`, so the match used the heap-tag strategy (load a ctor tag at `[scrut+8]`). For a niche-encoded type (`Option`-shaped) the value is a tagged scalar or null, not a heap cell, so that load segfaulted at runtime — a silent miscompile. `emit_case` now detects when the branch constructors name a niche-shaped type and, for an unresolved scrutinee, uses the niche strategy with `tagged=false`: the payload is taken verbatim and the erased-i64 convention conditionally untags it at its concrete use sites (`coerce`). The generated niche code is identical for every niche type at `tagged=false`, so only *whether* the match is niche-shaped matters. Boxed ADTs are unaffected (the heap-tag path was already correct). Regression: `stdlib/lazy_niche_probe.march` (a module deliberately excluded from `stdlib_file_list`) + `test/native/lazy_niche` (compile-and-diff) — prints `42`/`99` instead of crashing. Full suite green (compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786); the deeper "typecheck lazy bodies" option remains an alternative but this codegen recovery makes valid lazy-loaded code compile correctly.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-23, per-node CPU/memory introspection + ClusterLoad capacity-routing API)

- **System resource builtins (`sys_cpu_load_milli`, `sys_mem_total_bytes`, `sys_mem_available_bytes`).** Extend the existing `sys_*` family with real, cross-platform local resource sampling — wired through `runtime/march_extras.c` (macOS: `getloadavg` + `sysctl hw.memsize` + `host_statistics64`; Linux: `getloadavg` + `sysinfo` + `/proc/meminfo MemAvailable`), `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` (preamble decl + `builtin_ret_ty` + C mangle), `lib/tir/defun.ml`, and `lib/eval/eval.ml` (best-effort `/proc` reads in the interpreter, 0 fallback). Exposed via `System.cpu_load_milli/0`, `System.mem_total_bytes/0`, `System.mem_available_bytes/0` (`stdlib/system.march`). `cpu_load_milli` is the 1-min load average × 1000 (integer millis) — all four return `Int` to avoid float boxing in codegen.
- **`ClusterLoad` capacity-aware routing API (`stdlib/cluster_load.march`).** `NodeLoad { node_id, cpu_count, cpu_load_milli, mem_total_mb, mem_avail_mb }`; `local/1` samples the current node; `spare_cpu_milli/1` and `spare_mem_mb/1` are ready-made scorers; `pick_by/2` is an argmax over a candidate list with a caller-supplied scoring function (raw metrics exposed — the user decides what "headroom" means); `to_ints/1` / `from_ints/2` marshal a NodeLoad to/from a flat Int list for the wire. Design: gossiped/cached propagation (each node samples + shares; selection reads a local candidate view).
- **Compiler fix — lazily-loaded stdlib modules now typecheck.** A new stdlib module must be registered in `stdlib_file_list` (`bin/main.ml`); otherwise it is loaded lazily via `resolve_qualified_var`, which registers signatures but **skips body type inference**, leaving expression types as `'_`. That made `lower.ml`'s `ty_of_span` fall back to `unknown_ty` and `llvm_emit` pick the heap-tag match strategy for a **niche-encoded `Option`** result (e.g. `match List.nth_opt(...)`), dereferencing a tagged int → segfault. Fixed by registering `cluster_load.march` in `stdlib_file_list`. (Deeper hardening — typecheck lazy bodies or fail loudly instead of silently miscompiling — tracked separately.)
- **`demo/resource_report.march`** prints a node's live `ClusterLoad` sample. **Cross-host verified on a real droplet:** cross-compiled for `linux/amd64` and run on a 1-core droplet, it reported `cpu_count=1`, `cpu_load_milli=3119`, `mem_total_mb=458`, `mem_avail_mb=322` — all matching `/proc` ground truth (`nproc`=1, loadavg 3.12, MemTotal 469352 kB, MemAvailable 330044 kB). Routing decision over the two real cross-host samples (Mac 14cpu/load≈57/9.9 GB vs droplet 1cpu/load≈3.1/322 MB): `pick_by(spare_cpu_milli)` → droplet, `pick_by(spare_mem_mb)` → mac.
- **Verification.** Full suite green — compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786 (incl. Slow); `test_stdlib_march` 46/47 (the 1 failure is the pre-existing `node_rpc` `unbound variable: remote_check` eval gap, orthogonal). `System.*` resource builtins return real values on both macOS and the Linux droplet (verified against `/proc`); `ClusterLoad.pick_by` routes correctly. Known limitation: a *live* cross-host load query over RPC is blocked by RPC CAS impl_hash instability for non-trivial function bodies across separate compilations (`square` works, `report_load` doesn't) — tracked separately; the routing logic and cross-host measurement are proven independently.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-23, usability diagnostics for docs-surfaced limitations)

> **Authoritative test counts (2026-06-23, verified by running the suites): 321 compiler / 224 eval / 320 codegen.** The 321 reflects the +3 `error_improvements` tests from the "usability diagnostics" entry immediately below; the earlier "318 compiler" figure is a superseded point-in-time count.

- **Three usability limitations surfaced while writing docs — `parser.mly`, `typecheck.ml`.** Two diagnostic improvements (by-design behavior kept, baffling errors fixed) and one confirmed non-regression:
  - **Top-level `mod` + sibling top-level decls now gives a clear error** (`parser.mly`, `module_` rule). A file with a complete top-level `mod ... end` followed by anything other than EOF (e.g. a sibling `fn main`) previously hit menhir's generic "I got stuck here". A new `MOD … END error` production raises a precise `ParseError`: "A file may have only one top-level `mod`; everything else must live inside it." with a corrective hint. One-top-level-module-per-file is intentional (see memory note); this only improves the message. Verified zero new grammar conflicts (7 states / 59 S-R, identical to baseline).
  - **Same-printed-name type collision now explained** (`typecheck.ml`, `report_mismatch`). A user type that shares its printed name with a stdlib/other-module type (e.g. local `Config` vs the stdlib's) produced the useless "expected `Config` but got `Config`" (one side a `TCon`, the other a `TRecord` recovered to the same display name). A `same_name_note` now detects "prints identically but structurally distinct" (TCon-vs-TRecord, or differing ctor name / arg count) and adds a note: "Two distinct types are both named `X` … March has a single global type namespace …" with a rename/qualify suggestion. Global name resolution is unchanged (the by-design limitation stays).
  - **Deeply-nested inline `match`-arm bodies: confirmed already working** — investigated the reported "needs do/end wrapper" parse failure and could not reproduce it across ~12 variations (nested match as last/non-last arm, 3-deep nesting, multi-expr inner arms, guards, `let`-prefixed bodies, followed by sibling top-level fns). The contextual-NL token filter (`token_filter.ml`) handles it; left untouched per the "don't destabilize the token filter" guidance. Added a regression test that locks in the working behavior.
  - 3 new `error_improvements` compiler tests (`test/test_compiler.ml`): `test_toplevel_mod_plus_sibling_fn_error`, `test_nested_inline_match_arm_parses`, `test_same_name_type_collision_note`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-23, two-host RPC over TCP — name-resolution + hash fixes, Linux portability, repeatable harness)

- **Two separate node binaries doing real RPC over TCP (`demo/two_node_server.march`, `demo/two_node_client.march`).** The server registers `square` via the compiler-emitted `__rpc_stub` mechanism and serves a TCP accept loop; the client connects, derives the CAS hash pair with `remote_ref_hashes("TwoNodeClient", "square")`, and calls `square(7)`/`square(12)` over the wire, getting back `49`/`144`. Unlike the in-process `demo/two_node_rpc.march`, these are independent executables, so they prove the same path across two hosts. `demo/dune` gains `two_node_server.exe`/`two_node_client.exe` rules.
- **Name-resolution fix (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`).** A bare call like `serve_loop(fd)` from inside a module that *also* sees a cross-module `NodeCall.serve_loop` was resolved through `unqualified_fns` to the cross-module function, so the server invoked `NodeCall.serve_loop` instead of its own recursive loop and exited right after `tcp_listen`. Every call-resolution site (EApp, AVar, and the ECallPtr raises/cross-module paths) now checks `ctx.top_fns` **before** `unqualified_fns`: a same-module top-level function shadows any cross-module suffix match. (This file also carries the committed RC/equality + niche-Option-eq and FBIP niche-reuse codegen fixes synced from main.)
- **Remote-hash completeness (`bin/main.ml`, both the CAS `--compile` and `--emit-llvm` paths).** `remote_ref_hashes("Mod", "fn")` is constant-folded at compile time from `hr_impl_hashes`/`remote_sig_hashes`, which were populated only from the *post-optimization* TIR. A caller-side `pfn square` that is never locally called gets eliminated by the optimizer, so its hash was missing and the builtin folded to empty strings → the server's admission check (`march_remote_check_march`) saw `NoTarget` and the call failed. A second pass now hashes **every remaining pre-opt function** not already in the table (`March_cas.Hash.hash_fn_def`), so the caller's hash matches the server's registered hash even when the function is optimized away on the caller side.
- **Linux portability (cross-compilation).** `runtime/march_runtime.c` falls back to `/dev/urandom` where macOS uses `arc4random_buf`; `runtime/march_scheduler.c`, `lib/eval/ffi_marshal.c`, `lib/eval/eval_ffi_stubs.c` define `_GNU_SOURCE` on Linux so `MAP_ANONYMOUS`/`RTLD_DEFAULT` are visible (and `ffi_marshal.c` prepends `./` to bare `.so` paths in `dlopen`); `lib/jit/detect_llvm.sh` links `-lLLVM-${LLVM_VER}` (Linux ships only the versioned LLVM lib). All guarded by `#if defined(__linux__)` so the macOS path is unchanged.
- **Repeatable RPC harness (`scripts/rpc/`).** `smoke-local.sh` builds both binaries, launches the server, runs the client against `127.0.0.1`, and asserts `49`/`144` (fast inner-loop regression). `build-linux.sh` cross-compiles `dist/two_node_{server,client}_linux` for `linux/amd64` via Docker, reusing the deps-ready `march-builder` toolchain image: it builds only the compiler with dune, stages a **complete** source runtime dir next to it, and invokes the compiler directly per demo file (a single-target build in a fresh dune build-dir only materializes that rule's listed deps and misses the rest of the runtime closure, e.g. `march_http.c`; `march_tls.c` is excluded since the demo uses plain TCP and the OpenSSL header detection is macOS-pathed). `deploy-droplet.sh` ships the Linux server to a remote host, frees the port by any holder, proves droplet-localhost RPC, and proves the macOS-native client over an `ssh -L` tunnel (the corporate socket filter blocks the unsigned binary's direct remote TCP but permits loopback + ssh, so the tunnel turns the remote hop into an allowed local one).
- **Verification.** Full suite green on macOS — `run_compiler` 318, `run_eval` 224, `run_codegen` 320, `run_stdlib` 786 (incl. Slow JIT/adversarial). `scripts/rpc/smoke-local.sh` returns `49`/`144`. **Cross-host proven end-to-end** with worktree-built `linux/amd64` binaries on a remote droplet: droplet-localhost client → `49`/`144`, and macOS-native client → droplet server over the `ssh -L` tunnel → `49`/`144`. Known-orthogonal: `test_stdlib_march`'s `node_rpc` eval test fails with `unbound variable: remote_check` — the RPC admission builtins are codegen/typecheck-only and not yet registered as interpreter builtins (pre-existing gap, tracked separately; the demo uses native compilation).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-23, Distributed OTP — L4 compiler-emitted @main enroll/stub integration test)

> **Authoritative test counts (2026-06-23, verified by running the suites): 318 compiler / 224 eval / 13 LSP-diagnostics.** The 318 reflects the +4 `error_improvements` tests from the "improved if-expression error messages" entry below; the earlier "314 compiler" figures in older entries are point-in-time and were superseded. *(SUPERSEDED: see the "usability diagnostics" entry above — current authoritative count is 321 compiler.)*

- **L4 `rpc_auto_enroll` integration test (`test/native/rpc_auto_enroll.march`) — compiler auto-registers stubs at `@main`.** Closes the loop on compiler-emitted `march_remote_register` calls: a server node defines `add(a, b) : Int` plus a hand-written `add__rpc_stub` marshalling function; the compiler detects the `__rpc_stub` suffix, pins the stub in `tm_exports` so DCE cannot remove it, hashes the base `add` function, and emits `march_remote_register(impl_hash, sig_hash, &add__rpc_stub)` at program startup. A client then calls `NodeCall.call` with `remote_ref_hashes("RpcAutoEnroll", "add")`, the registry dispatch in `NodeRpc.handle_request` resolves the target via `remote_check`/`remote_invoke`, and the expected result `15` (= 7 + 8) is returned. Port 29851. Test added to `test/dune` as a native compile-and-diff rule.
  - **DCE pinning (`bin/main.ml`, after mono, before `Opt.run`).** Scans `tm_fns` for functions with the `__rpc_stub` suffix and appends them to `tm_exports`. The `Dce.run` pass seeds its reachable set from `tm_exports`, so pinned stubs survive even if unreachable from `main`.
  - **Pre-opt hash fallback (`bin/main.ml`, after CAS `h_sccs` iteration).** Saves `pre_opt_tir` before `Opt.run`. After the CAS hash step, for each `__rpc_stub` whose base function is absent from `hr_impl_hashes` (because the optimizer inlined it), looks up the base in `pre_opt_tir` and computes its hash with `March_cas.Hash.hash_fn_def`. Ensures `stub_setup` in `llvm_emit.ml` has a valid `(impl_hash, sig_hash)` pair even when the base is trivial enough to be inlined away. Applied at both compile paths (CAS and `--emit-llvm`).
  - **`remote_check` / `remote_invoke` builtins** — added to `typecheck.ml` `builtin_bindings` and `llvm_emit.ml`; lower to `march_remote_check_march` / `march_remote_invoke_march` in `runtime/march_remote_registry.c`. `remote_check` looks up `impl_hash` and compares `sig_hash` (admission gate); `remote_invoke` calls the registered stub and returns `Option(List(Int))` (None = not found or admission fail).
  - **`NodeRpc.handle_request` C-registry fallback** (`stdlib/node_rpc.march`). When a target name is not in the injected `Targets` table, falls through to `remote_check` + `remote_invoke` against the C registry. Maps `None` → `NoTarget`, `Some(Err(e))` → `RemoteExit(e)`, `Some(Ok(r))` → `Returned(r)`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-22, FBIP niche fix + forge --target js + clustering docs)

- **FBIP niche self-reference fix (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`).** The FBIP optimization generated a self-referential object for niche-encoded patterns like `Some(result) -> Ok(result)`. For niche `Option`, `Some(x) = x` directly (no wrapper) — the branch variable and the scrutinee are the same runtime pointer. The FBIP reuse branch overwrote `x+8` (tag) and `x+16` (field = `x` itself → cycle); the fresh branch also wrongly called `march_decrc(x)` before storing `x` as Ok's field (use-after-free). Fix in the `EReuse (reuse_atom, TCon(ctor,_), args)` fallback branch: detect when `reuse_atom`'s parent type is niche-shaped and skip FBIP — always allocate fresh without touching the reuse atom's RC. 300 compiler + 320 codegen tests pass. This unblocks `NetKernel.recv_frame` which hung due to the self-referential result confusing downstream pattern matching.

- **`forge build --target js` / `forge run --compiled --target js` (`forge/bin/main.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_build.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_run.ml`).** `forge build --target js` compiles the project entry to `.march/build/{debug,release}/<name>.mjs` and auto-copies `march_runtime.mjs` + `march_dom.mjs` alongside it. `forge run --compiled --target js` builds then runs with `node`. All other targets (`native`, `wasm*`) unchanged.

- **Distributed OTP clustering & RPC reference guide (`docs/clustering.md`).** 391-line reference covering all five distributed layers: node identity, authenticated handshake, Membership CRDT, SWIM failure detection + driver, GlobalRegistry CRDT, GlobalPid, RemoteRef/CallError taxonomy, NodeRpc responder pattern. Notes what remains for P2 (two-node integration test) and P3 (compiler-emitted enroll/stub).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-22, Distributed OTP — L4 compiler enroll/stub)

- **L4 compiler enroll/stub — `remote_ref_hashes`, `remote_register_stub`, `remote_count` builtins.** The compiler touchpoint that closes the CAS-hash gap: functions now carry their real identity into the runtime at compile time. Three new builtins wired end-to-end (typecheck → eval → codegen):
  - **`remote_ref_hashes(module, fn) : (String, String)`** — constant-folded in `emit_expr`: the `EApp` handler intercepts the call, looks up `(sig_hash, impl_hash)` from the CAS `hash_module` output (Blake3 64-char hex), and emits a heap-allocated tuple. Two implementation fixes: (1) all three builtins added to `defun.ml`'s `builtin_names` so defun keeps them as `EApp` rather than `ECallPtr` — the constant-folding arm only fires for `EApp`; (2) top-level user-module functions have no module prefix in their TIR `fn_name` (`"fib"`, not `"TestRRH.fib"`), so the lookup tries the unqualified name when the qualified key misses. Eval path uses FNV-1a 32-char hex (deterministic, non-empty, shorter than CAS Blake3).
  - **`remote_register_stub(impl, sig, stub) : Int`** — maps to `march_remote_register` C via `mangle_extern`.
  - **`remote_count() : Int`** — maps to `march_remote_count`.
  - **`march_remote_init()` auto-emitted** in `@main` (before user code runs, after `march_process_argv_init`).
  - **Tests:** 8 eval tests in `test_stdlib_march` (`test_remote_ref_hashes.march`: non-empty, distinct, deterministic, per-module, per-fn, RemoteRef round-trip, register returns 0, count ≥ 0) + 1 compiled native test (`test/native/remote_ref_hashes.march`: uses recursive `fib` to survive DCE, verifies 64-char Blake3 hashes and `sig != impl`).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-22, Distributed OTP — L4 function-by-identity registry)

- **L4 runtime function-by-identity registry (`runtime/march_remote_registry.{c,h}`) — the one genuinely net-new runtime mechanism.** The design spec flagged this as the missing primitive ("there is no function-by-identity registry today; `runtime/` has none"); this is increment 3a, the foundation the compiler-side enroll/stub emission will build on. A chained hash table keyed by a remote target's CAS identity (its `impl_hash` string) → its compiler-emitted marshalling stub (stored as an opaque `void *` — the stub ABI is the emitter's concern). `march_remote_register(impl_hash, sig_hash, stub)` (idempotent on identity; copies the strings; records `sig_hash` so the responder can run the CAS admission check before invoking), `march_remote_lookup`, `march_remote_sig_hash`, `march_remote_has`, `march_remote_count`, `march_remote_init`/`shutdown`. Mutex-guarded (registration at `@main` startup is single-threaded; lookups during request handling may be multi-threaded). Distinct from the HCR `march_dispatch` table: that is NAME_ID-indexed + versioned for hot reload; this is identity-keyed for cross-node dispatch (a function can be both). Linked into compiled binaries at both `bin/main.ml` link sites alongside `march_dispatch.c`; the CAS cache key auto-includes it (runtime sources are dir-globbed). **7-group C unit test** (`test/test_remote_registry.c`, wired into `dune runtest`): register/lookup + sig_hash, unknown-misses, distinct identities + count, re-register-replaces (idempotent), NULL-rejected, init-clears, 200-key chaining. Regression-clean (codegen 320 — compiled binaries still link). **Next (remaining L4 increments):** `remote_ref` lowering (produce `{module, name, sig_hash, impl_hash}` from the CAS — Compiler Touchpoint 1); the serializable-signature compile-time gate; compiler-emitted marshalling stubs (`llvm_emit`) + their `march_remote_register` calls at `@main` + DCE pinning; then wire `NodeRpc.handle_frame` to the live net-kernel socket for an end-to-end loopback call. *(RESOLVED: `remote_ref_hashes`/`remote_register_stub`/`remote_count` lowering + `@main` stub registration + DCE pinning + the loopback wiring all landed — see the 2026-06-23 "L4 `rpc_auto_enroll`", 2026-06-22 "L4 Node.call loopback", and 2026-06-22 "L4 compiler enroll/stub" entries above.)*

## Current State (as of 2026-06-22, Distributed OTP — L4 RPC dispatcher)

- **L4 RPC receive-side dispatcher + caller-side logic (`node_rpc.march`, `NodeRpc`).** The pure orchestration between the net-kernel transport and a node's enrolled targets — increment 2 of L4, completing the RPC *logic* end-to-end (only the compiler-emitted enroll/stub registry and the live socket send/recv remain).
  - **Responder.** `Target = {sig_hash, impl_hash, invoke : List(Int) -> Result(List(Int), String)}` models one enrolled target (the `invoke` stub decodes MessagePack args, runs the body, encodes the result; `Err` models a crash/exit). `Targets` is a `name → Target` table — **injected here**, modelling exactly what the compiler-emitted enroll/stub mechanism must provide. `handle_request(targets, req) : CallReply` looks the target up by identity → runs `RemoteCall.verify` admission → invokes or maps the failure: no target → `NoTarget`, sig/impl mismatch → `TypeMismatch`/`VersionSkew`, a body crash → `RemoteExit(reason)`, success → `Returned`. `handle_frame` decodes an inbound request frame and dispatches (malformed frame → no reply).
  - **Caller.** `interpret` (reply → `Result(payload, CallError)`), `matches` (correlate a reply to its request — replies on one connection interleave), and the failure-isolation decisions `timed_out(now, deadline)` + `peer_down_error()` (→ `NoConnection` when the failure detector declares the node dead).
  - Added `NoTarget` to `RemoteCall.CallError` (additive: taxonomy + codec + message). 12 TDD asserts (`test_node_rpc.march`) with a real add/boom invoker stub: invoke-and-return, sig-mismatch-not-invoked, version-skew, unknown-target, crash→RemoteExit, frame round-trip + malformed→None, interpret Ok/Err, correlation match, deadline + peer-down. Pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 43→44). (Parser gotcha: `sig`/`impl` are reserved keywords — renamed the test's hash params.)
  - **Next (final L4 increment):** the **enroll/stub/registry runtime mechanism** — a function-by-identity registry in `runtime/`, compiler-emitted marshalling stubs that populate a real `Targets`-equivalent, DCE pinning, and a `remote_ref` builtin (Compiler Touchpoint 1); then wire `handle_frame` to the live net-kernel socket for an end-to-end loopback call. *(RESOLVED: the function-by-identity registry (`march_remote_registry.{c,h}`), compiler-emitted stubs + `@main` registration + DCE pinning, the `remote_ref_hashes` builtin, and the loopback wiring all landed — see the three later 2026-06-22/06-23 L4 entries above.)*

## Current State (as of 2026-06-22, Distributed OTP — L4 remote-call safety core)

- **L4 remote-procedure-call safety core — two pure modules (`global_pid.march`, `remote_call.march`).** The first increment of the headline RPC layer: the wire protocol + CAS admission decision, with the enroll/stub/registry runtime mechanism and the socket transport left to follow-on increments.
  - **`GlobalPid` (`Pid = {node_id, local_pid, creation}`)** — the serializable, cluster-wide process handle that location-transparent `send`/`call` need (the H5 prerequisite, pure part). `creation` distinguishes a *restarted* node's reused local pids from the originals, so a pre-restart Pid is detectably stale. `make`/`eq` + MessagePack `to_value`/`of_value` (for nesting) and `encode`/`decode`.
  - **`RemoteCall`** — `RemoteRef = {module_name, fn_name, sig_hash, impl_hash}` (the compile-time-stamped reference to an enrolled target; `sig_hash` pins the type, `impl_hash` the compiled body via the CAS Merkle root). **The safety core is `verify(local_sig, local_impl, fref) : Verdict`**: `sig_hash` mismatch → `Reject(TypeMismatch)` (incompatible signature — never invoke), `impl_hash` mismatch → `Reject(VersionSkew)` (same type, different body — peer could ship it under P6, else reject), both match → `Accept` (invoke the local native copy). Error taxonomy `CallError = DeadlineExceeded | NoConnection | RemoteExit(String) | TypeMismatch | VersionSkew` (failure isolation — a deadline/netsplit/crash returns an error, never a hang). Full MessagePack codecs for `CallRequest {fref, args, reply_to, deadline, correlation}` and `CallReply {correlation, Returned|Failed}`, framable inside a NetFrame (nested ref + pid round-trip).
  - 13 TDD asserts (`test_global_pid.march` 3, `test_remote_call.march` 10): pid eq + roundtrip + garbage-rejected; verify accept / type-mismatch / version-skew; request roundtrip (nested ref+pid); reply roundtrips for returned + each CallError kind. Pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 41→43). (Codec convention reused from `NodeIdentity`: the Msgpack `Value` type collides in the global namespace and a qualified `Msgpack.Value` annotation won't unify, so value-builder helpers go un-annotated and rely on inference + qualified constructors.)
  - **Next (L4 increments):** the **enroll/stub/registry runtime mechanism** (the one genuinely net-new compiler+runtime piece — a function-by-identity registry + marshalling stubs + DCE pinning + a `remote_ref` builtin; see Compiler Touchpoint 1); then **`Node.call` dispatch + failure isolation** wiring this core to the net-kernel transport (request frame → admission → invoke → reply frame, with deadline/netsplit → `CallError`). *(RESOLVED: both increments landed — the registry/stub/DCE/`remote_ref_hashes` mechanism and the `Node.call`→`handle_frame` loopback dispatch are covered by the later 2026-06-22/06-23 L4 entries above.)*

## Current State (as of 2026-06-22, Distributed OTP — L5 global registry CRDT)

- **Global name registry (`global_registry.march`, `GlobalRegistry`) — a composite, gossip-replicable CRDT mapping `name → {node_id, pid}`.** Each binding carries a `VectorClock` that causally orders updates to *that name*, plus a presence bit so an `unregister` converges like an LWW-element-set removal (tombstones merge by the same rule rather than vanishing). Per-name conflict resolution: causally-newer (`VectorClock.compare` → `After`) wins; older/`Equal` keeps the existing (idempotent); **`Concurrent` — a genuine same-name conflict — is broken by a total deterministic tiebreak on `(node_id, pid)`**, so every replica converges to the same winner. That makes `merge` associative/commutative/idempotent — the CRDT join the gossip/anti-entropy loop uses (Merkle anti-entropy belongs at this layer since the registry can be large; the small member set at L3 uses full-state gossip instead). API: `register`/`unregister`/`lookup`/`contains`/`names`/`size`/`observe`/`merge`. Builds on the existing `VectorClock` primitive (`compare`/`increment`/`merge`). 11 TDD asserts (`test_global_registry.march`): register→lookup, tombstone on newer-clock unregister, no-op unknown unregister, causal rebind-overrides + stale-ignored, concurrent same-name convergence (both merge orders), idempotence, union, and removal-converges (merging the older add back must not resurrect). Pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 40→41). (Parser gotcha hit + recorded: `actor` is a reserved keyword — renamed the test's clock-builder param.) **Next:** ConsistentHash-partitioned authoritative ownership + Merkle anti-entropy (`diff()`) for the gossip loop; serializable `Pid` (H5) so the stored pid is a real cross-node handle; load-aware dispatch (L6).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-22, Distributed OTP — L3 SWIM runtime driver)

- **SWIM runtime driver (`swim_driver.march`, `SwimDriver`) — the I/O glue for the pure Swim state machine.** Owns the RNG + period timer so the transport layer calls `step(state, now, events)` each tick. Picks probe targets and indirect helpers randomly, advances the SWIM FSM, and returns `List(Swim.Action)` for the transport to execute. Events: `PingAck(node_id)` / `GossipFrame(Member)` / `PeerDown(node_id)` (peer-down skips Suspect and goes straight to Dead at the current incarnation, so the verdict supersedes). Wire protocol: Msgpack arrays with integer tag dispatch (0=SwimPing, 1=SwimPingAck, 2=SwimPingReq, 3=SwimGossip); `encode_msg`/`decode_msg` round-trip verified. `dispatch`/`dispatch_all` send Actions to peers via `PeerRegistry` + `NetKernel`. 17 TDD asserts (`test_swim_driver.march`): period boundaries, event folding, indirect escalation, wire codec. Pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 44→45). **Next (P3):** L4 compiler enroll/stub — auto-generate the `invoke` stub and `sig_hash`/`impl_hash` from `pub fn`, wire `NodeRpc.handle_frame` to the live net-kernel socket. *(RESOLVED: L4 compiler enroll/stub landed — see the 2026-06-23 "L4 `rpc_auto_enroll`" entry and the 2026-06-22 "L4 Node.call loopback" entry above, which wire dispatch through `NodeRpc.handle_frame`/`handle_request` over real TCP loopback.)*
  - **Parser gotcha:** `else if` chaining is not yet implemented — use `match n do 0 -> ... | 1 -> ... end` (integer literal patterns) for multi-branch integer dispatch. Simple two-branch `if/else/end` works fine.

- **SWIM failure-detection probe loop (`swim.march`, `Swim`) — the pure protocol state machine over the `Membership` CRDT.** Each transition takes the state + clock + injected randomness (the member to probe, the helper set) and returns the next state plus the I/O `Action`s the runtime layer must perform (`SendPing` / `SendPingReq` / `Gossip`) — the socket/timer glue (random member selection, frame I/O, timers) lives in the runtime layer, same split as `NetKernel`/`ClusterConn`, keeping detection deterministic and unit-testable. One protocol period: `begin_period(target)` → `SendPing`; no ack by `ack_timeout_ms` → `escalate_indirect` fans out k-way `SendPingReq` (once); no ack by `end_period` → mark Suspect + `Gossip` + arm the suspect→dead timer; `expire_suspects` promotes timed-out Suspects to Dead. `on_gossip` folds a member delta in (a higher-incarnation Alive refutes and cancels the suspect timer); `refute` bumps our own incarnation and re-announces Alive when we are falsely suspected. 11 TDD asserts (`test_swim.march`). Pure + eval-tested. Two parser gotchas recorded (see above + parser-gotchas memory).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-25, Refinement types — Gap #1 full: record param + return refinements)

- **Gap #1 full (Part B): record-typed parameter refinements in postcondition VCs.** The `scope` entry gains an optional record-sort tag `(binder, pred, sort_opt)`. `refined_scope_ty` admits both `Int` and `TyCon`-based record refinements. `scope_add_param`/`scope_add_binding` use it; `sig_of_clause` keeps `refined_int_ty` so `check_call` never spawns record-param call-site VCs (Decision R3). `scope_facts` now returns `(decls, assumptions, has_record: bool)`: record entries declare an opaque datatype const (`SData sort_name`), build a `make_field_resolver` over it, and reflect the predicate via field selectors — so `s.count >= 0` over `old : {s:State|s.count>=0}` becomes `(>= (State_0 old) 0)` as an SMT assumption. `check_post` triggers the record preamble when the RETURN OR any scope entry is record-sorted (`scope_has_record`). A composite `scope_field_resolver` is threaded into `scalar` so that `old.count` in the return expression resolves via selectors. With concrete record preconditions in scope (`scope_has_record = true`), a SAT counterexample from the first discharge is reported as a real violation (the counterexample satisfies the precondition). `make_field_resolver` moved before `scope_facts` to fix forward-reference; `reflect_scalar` updated to 3-tuple. **62 tests in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`** (61 pass; `resolution.0` is a pre-existing failure). Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-25-refine-check-gap1-full.md`. **Remaining:** `@invariant` on `State` type; Gap #2 (persist invariant in `.schemas.json`), Gap #3 (`--check-migration` CLI).

- **Record postconditions + measures over record fields (Gap #1 Part A).** `smt_of` gains `?resolve_field`, `?resolve_measure_app`, and `ECon` handling; `concrete_len`/`concrete_measure_app`/`selector_reduce` evaluate measure applications at OCaml level to avoid Z3 quantifier axioms; `type_only_preamble()` emits ADT sorts without `forall` axioms when concrete evaluation succeeds (prevents Z3 `unknown` on SAT queries); `measure_arms` accepts `PatWild` (`_`) by assigning generated names, fixing a root-cause bug that silently prevented any measure with wildcard arms (e.g. `Cons(_, t)`) from being axiomatized — this unblocked axioms-ma, list-axioms-mb, mutual-mc, and flag-gating tests simultaneously. Predicates like `len(v.history)==v.count` and `mlength(v.history)==v.count` now discharge correctly over `ERecord` literals. Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-25-refine-check-record-spike.md`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-21, Refinement types — use/alias-aware call resolution)

- **Refinement call resolution now mirrors the typechecker (Path 1).** The old "qualified key OR globally-unique bare name" matching — which dropped sibling calls on any cross-module name collision and never resolved aliased / `use`-imported names — is replaced by proper lexical resolution: a call binds to the nearest enclosing module that defines it (with shadowing), an `alias X.Y as P` expands `P.foo` → `X.Y.foo`, and `use M.{f}` / `use M.*` bind imported bare names. Recording NON-refined definitions too keeps it sound (a bare call to a non-refined sibling no longer falls through to a refined outer function of the same name — no false positive). It catches violations the old code missed AND stays false-positive-free; a wrong resolution can at worst skip. Verified end-to-end (a `use`-imported `take_pos(-7)` is caught through the full pipeline). 54 tests in `test/test_refinecheck.ml` (count updated to reflect +3 record-postconditions group). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-refinement-types-state-and-forward-design.md`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-21, Refinement types — measure axioms M-a → M-c + perf/flag-gating)

- **Refinement measures reason structurally, call each other, and recurse mutually (M-a → M-c).** A `@[measure]` over an ADT is *axiomatised* in SMT (not just reflected symbolically): the ADT is modelled with `declare-datatypes`, the measure as an uninterpreted function, and each `match` arm as a `:pattern`-triggered recursion-equation axiom — so the solver computes measure values from structure (`size(Node(Leaf,x,Leaf)) = 1`, `length([10,20]) = 2`). The built-in `List(a)` is modelled (`Nil | Cons`); datatype sorts live in a private `M_` namespace so no ADT name collides with a z3-reserved sort. A **hard soundness gate** makes `@[measure]` a compile error when the function is not total/terminating/pure (effectful, divergent, non-exhaustive, divide-by-zero, or non-structurally-recursive). **M-c**: a measure may call another *axiomatisable* measure (argument still a structural component, so the whole — possibly mutually recursive — system is structurally decreasing and total); axiomatisability is resolved by a greatest fixpoint, all `declare-fun`s precede all axioms, and mutually-recursive datatypes are declared together in one combined `declare-datatypes` (transitive ADT closure; cross-ADT fields reflect to their sort). Verified on mutually-recursive `RTree`/`RForest`. **Performance measured + flag-gated:** measure-bound VCs are expensive cold (~155× a symbolic query; many near the 3 s timeout) but cheap warm (BLAKE3 VC cache); the datatype/quantifier preamble is attached *only* to VCs referencing an axiomatised measure, so non-measure refinement checks pay nothing (150 plain VCs + a measure defined ≈ 1 s). New `--no-measure-axioms` flag reflects measures symbolically and disables the gate (diagnostics-only, not in the CAS key); axioms remain on by default. 46 tests in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`. Remaining: M-d (relational postconditions). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-measure-axioms-design.md`.
