## Current State (as of 2026-06-21, Distributed OTP — P1 net-kernel L2 complete)

- **Distributed OTP P1 (transport + net-kernel) — L1+L2 complete.** Seven new stdlib modules compose a full authenticated peer connection (dial *and* accept):
  - `net_frame.march` — `NetFrame`: length-prefixed wire framing (partial-read safe).
  - `node_identity.march` — `NodeIdentity`: `{name, NodeId=sha256(pubkey), incarnation}` + MessagePack codec.
  - `cluster_auth.march` — `ClusterAuth`: shared-secret HMAC challenge/response (`prove`/`verify`).
  - `handshake.march` — `Handshake`: `Hello` greeting codec + `authenticate` decision (proof must bind to the nonce we issued).
  - `net_kernel.march` — `NetKernel`: byte bridge (`List(Int)`↔Socket `String`) + framed `send_frame`/`recv_frame` (buffered, partial-read safe) + symmetric authenticated `handshake` over a socket (client side: `Socket.connect`/`write`/`recv`, all available) + `fresh_nonce`.
  - `peer_registry.march` — `PeerRegistry`: one-connection-per-peer table keyed by NodeId (re-add replaces).
  - `cluster_conn.march` — `ClusterConn`: the connection lifecycle. `listen(port)` (`tcp_listen`), `connect_to_peer` (`Socket.connect` → handshake → register), `accept_one` (`tcp_accept` → handshake → register); a handshake failure closes the socket.
  - **Enabling compiler change:** `tcp_listen`/`tcp_accept` are now exposed to March (`Int -> Result(Int, String)`) — typecheck signatures + `IO.NetListen` capability and eval `Unix` impls (returning a tagged `Result`). Previously the typechecker had no `tcp_listen` name, so the server-accept side was unwritable in March. (Exposed concurrently on `main`; an earlier branch commit that exposed them as `Int -> Int` was superseded by this Result-typed design.) The C `march_tcp_listen/accept` still return a raw int64, so the *compiled* path's codegen ret-type stays `TInt` — a known surface/runtime divergence for the (unexercised) compiled path; the interpreted path used by the stdlib tests returns the tagged `Result`.
  - **Verification:** all pure logic is unit-tested via the eval harness (`test_stdlib_march` 30→37, 0 failures: net_frame, node_identity, cluster_auth, handshake, net_kernel byte bridge, peer_registry). The socket modules (`NetKernel.send/recv/handshake`, `ClusterConn.listen/connect/accept`) **typecheck cleanly** (verified: 0 errors/warnings) and are runtime-exercised — the eval harness doesn't run sockets/scheduler (same convention as `ChannelServer`); they compose only unit-tested logic over the available `Socket.*` / `tcp_listen` / `tcp_accept` APIs. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
  - **L3 membership — CRDT member view (`membership.march`, `Membership`).** SWIM-style member state: each node tracked as `{node_id, status (Alive|Suspect|Dead), incarnation}`. The merge rule is a lexicographic max over `(incarnation, severity)` — higher incarnation wins; ties broken by the more-severe status (Dead > Suspect > Alive) — making `merge` associative/commutative/idempotent so gossiped views converge regardless of order (the anti-entropy CRDT join; no Merkle for the small member set). `observe`/`merge`/`alive_members` + `alive`/`suspect`/`dead` constructors. 10 TDD asserts (`test_membership.march`): refutation via higher incarnation, stale-observation rejection, Suspect>Alive & Dead>Suspect at equal incarnation, restart-overrides-Dead, merge commutativity/idempotence/union. Pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 37→38). The SWIM probe loop (direct/indirect ping, suspicion timers) drives this from the runtime layer — a follow-on, like the socket I/O.
  - **Follow-ups:** a real loopback handshake integration test (now possible via `tcp_listen`/`tcp_accept` + a spawned accept task); the SWIM probe loop on top of `Membership` + the net-kernel; constant-time proof compare in `ClusterAuth` (`Crypto.ct_eq` exists). **Next:** the global registry CRDT (L5) and load-aware dispatch (L6). (Aside: whole-stdlib *flat* typecheck via `MARCH_LIB_PATH=stdlib` still cascades on other `Char`-migration breakages in unrelated modules — stdlib is validated per-file via forge, not flat-loaded; char.march itself is fixed.)

## Current State (as of 2026-06-22, External Function Interface — fully-typed Option/Result codec fields)

- **`forge ffi gen-c` now fully types `Option(T)`/`Result(T,E)` record/variant FIELDS** (previously a raw `march_value` passthrough). For each such field whose payloads are leaf types (`Int`/`Bool`/`String`/`Bytes`), it emits a synthetic mirror struct + decode/encode: `Option_T_c` = `{ int32_t is_some; <T> value; }` (niche: `None`=0, `Some(x)`=tagged payload); `Result_T_E_c` = `{ int32_t is_ok; <T> ok; <E> err; }` (boxed: tag 0/1, payload at field 0). These synthetic codecs are emitted ahead of the record/variant codecs (they depend only on leaf payloads), and record/variant mirrors reference them by value (`Option_Int_c maybe;`). The author works with plain `o.maybe.is_some` / `o.res.ok` C structs instead of poking `march_value` slots.
- **Still passthrough** (documented): `Option`/`Result` fields with Float/Unit/List/nested payloads, and `resource`/`List` fields.
- **Verified**: forge unit test (`gen-c: Option/Result field codecs`) → forge 89; end-to-end `test/native/ffi_optres` — a `Box { tag, maybe: Option(Int), name: Option(String), res: Result(Int,String) }` round-trips both ways (C `make()` encodes typed fields → March reads `7`/`42`/`hi`/`100`; March-built `Box` → C `classify()` decodes → `none/x/err:bad`). Generated codecs compile clean (`cc -c`).

- **Hot Code Reload Phase 5 — actor state migration.** Per-actor versioned handler dispatch with migrate-before-run ordering. **Runtime:** `MARCH_MIGRATE_TAG` system message (`march_migrate_msg_t`, malloc/free); `dispatch_name_id` in `march_actor_meta`; `actor_green_thread` dispatches MIGRATE before the alive gate; `march_actor_broadcast_migrate` (two-phase: snapshot under lock, inject outside lock). State indirection in `--hot-reload` builds: actor struct holds `$f_state` pointer at field 2 (pointer-swappable without lock); handlers load state via pointer; spawn initializes directly. `__migrate_<Actor>` LLVM alias exported from `.so` patches for `dlsym`. ACTIVATE payload extended with `migrate_required` flag; `march_reload.c` dlsyms `__migrate_<Actor>` and calls `broadcast_migrate`. Spawn wrapper: `*_spawn_impl` + thin wrapper calling `march_actor_set_dispatch_id`. **Type guardrails:** `.schemas.json` sidecar emitted alongside `.so`/`.hcr_manifest`; `forge/lib/schema_diff.ml` (pure OCaml JSON parser + `diff_schemas`/`check_compat`/`requires_migration`); `@compat` actor annotation; deploy-time compat check in `cmd_deploy_hot.ml`; sig_hash gate exemption for `*_dispatch` fns with `__migrate_*` symbol. **Bug fixes:** `march_sched_recv` null-ptr guard for niche-encoded zero-arg constructors (`MARCH_RECV_NO_MSG` sentinel); `emit_case` effective_repr for concrete `TCon(name, [])` niche types; `actor_green_thread` MIGRATE-before-alive guard. **E2E verified** on a DigitalOcean droplet: Counter v1→v2 migration preserves `count=3`, actor handles `Inc()` correctly post-migration (`count=4`). `phase5-actor-test.sh` exits 0. Files: `runtime/march_scheduler.{h,c}`, `runtime/march_runtime.c`, `runtime/march_reload.c`, `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, `lib/tir/dce.ml`, `lib/tir/mono.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`, `forge/lib/schema_diff.ml`, `demo/hcr_actor_demo/`, `scripts/rpc/phase5-actor-test.sh`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-25, HCR Phase 5 — e2e full verification with history field)

- **Schema JSON format fix (`bin/main.ml`).** The compiler's `.schemas.json` writer emitted all `state_fields` entries on a single line (`[{"name":"count","ty":"Int"}]`). `schema_diff.ml`'s hand-written parser expected each field on its own line; it saw `state_fields` but never detected the `{` that starts a field entry, so every actor parsed with 0 fields. `diff_schemas` produced an empty diff → `migrate_required=0` always. Fixed by writing each field on its own indented line (`[\n      {"name":…},\n    ]` format).

- **RTLD_DEEPBIND fix (`runtime/march_reload.c`).** `dlopen` called with `RTLD_NOW|RTLD_GLOBAL` but without `RTLD_DEEPBIND`. PLT calls inside the patch `.so` (e.g. v2's `Counter_dispatch` calling `Counter_PrintHistory$Counter_Actor$V__`) were resolved to the main executable's v1 symbols first (loaded first, visible globally) instead of the v2 symbols in the patch. This caused v2's `PrintHistory` handler to always print `"(none)"` even after migration. Fixed by adding `RTLD_DEEPBIND` (GNU extension, guarded `#ifndef RTLD_DEEPBIND; #define RTLD_DEEPBIND 0; #endif` for portability).

- **E2E fully verified** on DigitalOcean droplet (`do-march`) via `scripts/rpc/forge-deploy-hot-actors.sh`:
  - `pre_migrate:  count=3, history=(none)` — v1 Counter actor (no history field)
  - `post_migrate: count=3, history=[]` — `counter_migrate_state` called; history initialized
  - `post_inc:     count=4, history=[3]` — v2 Inc handler tracks history correctly
  - All three grep assertions exit 0. Script commits 51933f4e.

> **Authoritative test counts (2026-06-25): unchanged.** Bug fixes in `bin/main.ml` (schema JSON format) and `runtime/march_reload.c` (dlopen flags); no new test cases added.

- **Compiler test count:** 321
- **Stdlib test count:** 786 (full) / 766 (quick)

## Current State (as of 2026-06-24, HCR Phase 4 — `forge deploy hot` + ed25519 signing)

- **Hot Code Reload Phase 4 — CAS-native `forge deploy hot` + ed25519 signed activation.** Extended the reload protocol with `VERSIONS` (crash-restart drift detection), `CAS_CHECK`/`CAS_PUT` (inline CAS distribution over the SSH-tunneled socket), and a signed `ACTIVATE` command (`ACTIVATE <name> <impl_hash> <cas_hash> <sig64>`). Server verifies ed25519 signatures using a public key embedded at compile time via `-DMARCH_SIGNING_PUBKEY_HEX`. Ed25519 implementation: vendored `runtime/tweetnacl.{h,c}` (public domain, SHA-512 inline). New `lib/ed25519/` OCaml library wraps tweetnacl via C stubs; `forge hot-reload keygen` and `show-pubkey` manage the keypair. `forge deploy hot` opens an SSH tunnel, queries VERSIONS/ABI_QUERY, reads the `.hcr_manifest` sidecar (cas_hash + per-fn impl_hash/sig_hash written by `--compile-so`), computes set-diff, gates on sig_hash ABI safety, uploads the .so artifact via CAS_PUT, and sends signed ACTIVATE for each changed function. `march_dispatch` extended with `sig_hash` per version, `baseline_impl_hash` per slot, and `id→name` reverse lookup. `bin/main.ml`: fixed `remote_sig_hashes` (now covers all functions for HCR .so emit), `.hcr_manifest` written alongside every `--compile-so` output. Files: `runtime/tweetnacl.{h,c}`, `runtime/march_dispatch.{h,c}`, `runtime/march_reload.c`, `lib/ed25519/`, `forge/lib/cmd_hot_reload.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`, `forge/lib/project.ml` ([hot-reload] toml section), `bin/main.ml`. All tests green: compiler 321, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 766 (quick).
- **ed25519 scalar-arithmetic fix (2026-06-24)** — the initial `runtime/tweetnacl.c` produced invalid signatures (every `ACTIVATE` returned `ERR bad_signature` and even self-verify failed). Two bugs: (1) `gf_reduce` (the `car25519` analog) propagated carry `c` instead of `c-1`, leaving a per-limb `2^16` bias that accumulated through every GF multiplication; (2) `sc_reduce`/`mod_l` used 21-bit-limb reduction coefficients (ref10 representation) while being fed byte-level (base-256) input — a representation mismatch that corrupted both the nonce and the `hram` scalar in every sign/verify. Replaced `mod_l`+`sc_reduce`+`sc_muladd` with the verbatim TweetNaCl `modL` (base-256) algorithm; `crypto_sign` now computes `S = nonce + hram*az mod l` with TweetNaCl's byte-level polynomial multiply. `crypto_sign`+`crypto_sign_open` round-trip now returns 0. **End-to-end verified on the DigitalOcean droplet:** `forge deploy hot` activates `Server.square` over the signed ACTIVATE handshake and `square(7): 49 → 343` on the live server with no restart. Note: any keypair generated before this fix has an invalid pubkey embedded in `sk[32..63]` and must be regenerated.
- **Compiler test count:** 321
- **Stdlib test count:** 766 (quick) / 786 (full)

## Current State (as of 2026-06-21, Hot Code Reload — Phase 1: `impl_hash` is a true Merkle root)

- **CAS prerequisite for Hot Code Reload + Distributed OTP — DONE.** `hash_module` (`lib/cas/pipeline.ml`) now makes each definition's `impl_hash` a Merkle root over both its call graph and its type usage: it folds in (a) the `impl_hash`es of its earlier-SCC callees (SCCs walked dependencies-first) and (b) the base hashes of every named type it *transitively* references. A callee body change propagates into every direct/transitive caller; a record/variant layout change propagates into every definition that uses the type. This fixes a real latent stale-cache bug (cross-SCC `known_call` inlining served stale inlined callees because callers referenced callees by name) and closes HCR Part 5's shared-type soundness gate (TIR references types by name, so a layout change moved GEP offsets while leaving the user's hash unchanged).
- **Approach**: threaded callee/type hashes into the hash computation rather than emitting `ADefRef` nodes or extending `compute_sccs` to types — same Merkle guarantee, no TIR mutation. Type folding uses a transitive closure of type base hashes (cycle-safe; no type-SCC machinery). Externally-defined types are not folded (no base hash), mirroring the cross-SCC callee rule.
- **Tests**: 6 new `test_cas.ml` `def_id` cases (direct + two-hop call propagation, unrelated-def isolation, direct + transitive type-layout propagation, unreferenced-type isolation). `test_cas` suite 44→50. Full TDD (bug reproduced via the real `hash_module` pipeline before the fix). Specs: `specs/hot-code-reload.md` (Part 1 sharpened: `ADefRef` is never constructed today), `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md` (P0).
- **Test counts**: compiler 293, codegen 319, eval 224, stdlib 766 (-q) — all green, no regressions through the real CAS compile path; `test_cas` 50.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-21, Parallel list ops + parallelization lint)

- **Parallel `List` functions** (`stdlib/list.march`): `pmap`, `pmap_n` (bounded concurrency), `pfilter`, and `preduce` (parallel associative reduction; caller asserts associativity). All are order-preserving and produce results identical to their sequential equivalents. They chunk the input with the existing `List.chunks` and spawn one task per chunk over the existing multithreaded scheduler — real CPU parallelism in compiled code (verified ~281% CPU on a 200k-element workload), correct-but-sequential in the interpreter.
- **Configurable threshold**: new nullary builtin `pmap_threshold() -> Int` (typecheck `Mono Int`; interpreter reads `Eval.pmap_threshold_value`; LLVM backend emits it as a compile-time constant). Below it, the parallel functions fall back to sequential. Set via the `--pmap-threshold=N` compiler flag (default 1024), threaded into both the interpreter and codegen, included in the CAS cache key, and passed through by `forge` via `MARCH_PMAP_THRESHOLD`.
- **Parallelization lint (LSP)**: a `Hint`-severity diagnostic (`perf/parallelizable`) flags a pure `List.map`/`List.filter` as a `pmap`/`pfilter` candidate, with a "Convert to `List.pmap`/`List.pfilter`" quick-fix. Purity is checked on the surface AST sharing `March_tir.Purity.impure_builtins` (single source of truth); folds/reduces are never flagged (purity ≠ associativity).
- **Tests**: `test/stdlib/test_list_parallel.march` (registered in `test_stdlib_march.ml`) covers all four functions across empty/below/above-threshold sizes; a Slow compiled end-to-end parity test in `test_stdlib_suite.ml`; 6 LSP tests for the lint + code action. Design: `specs/2026-06-21-parallel-list-ops-design.md`; plan: `specs/2026-06-21-parallel-list-ops-plan.md`. Deferred (designed, not built): a `--auto-parallel` TIR rewrite pass.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-23, RingBuf stdlib module with non-sendable enforcement)

- **`stdlib/ring_buf.march`** — `RingBuf(a)`: mutable fixed-capacity circular buffer. All operations O(1) and allocation-free after `make()`. Index 0 = oldest (FIFO drain order). `push` overwrites oldest when full. `pop` removes oldest (FIFO). Shared-reference semantics within one actor. Non-sendable: typechecker rejects `RingBuf` values in `send()` payloads via a new `check_sendable`/`non_sendable_types` mechanism in `typecheck.ml`, with a clear diagnostic directing users to pass the buffer as actor init state instead. 10 builtins: `ring_buf_make`, `ring_buf_push`, `ring_buf_pop`, `ring_buf_get`, `ring_buf_peek_oldest`, `ring_buf_peek_newest`, `ring_buf_size`, `ring_buf_cap`, `ring_buf_clear`, `ring_buf_to_list`. `ring_pop_oldest` helper added to `eval.ml`. `VRingBuf of value ring` value constructor; `ring` type hoisted before `value` ADT. 28 tests in `test/stdlib/test_ring_buf.march`. All 1610 quick-suite tests pass.
- **Test counts**: compiler 300, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 766 (march-test-suite) + 28 ring_buf tests in `test_stdlib_march.exe`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-20, External Function Interface — Phase 6: blocking dispatch)

- **`blocking fn` externs run on a dedicated OS thread** while the calling green thread cooperatively yields, so a long/blocking C call no longer stalls the scheduler worker. Runtime: `march_run_blocking_i`/`_d` (`runtime/march_ffi.c`) spawn a pthread that runs the call through a fixed-arity GP-register trampoline, then `march_sched_yield` in a loop until done (falls back to inline if `pthread_create` fails). Codegen: a `blocking` extern call marshals its args into a stack i64 array and dispatches via `march_run_blocking_*` instead of a direct call (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`).
- **`blocking` is a contextual word** before `fn` in an extern block (not a reserved keyword), threaded `ef_blocking` → `ed_blocking` → a `blocking_externs` codegen set.
- **Scope**: Int/Bool/pointer (String/Bytes/resource handle) args + Int/Bool/Float returns — same GP-register trampoline constraints as the interpreter path (float-as-arg unsupported → compile error). Poll-based (per-call pthread, no pool yet; cooperative-yield loop spins in a no-scheduler context).
- **Verified**: `test/native/ffi_blocking` (`blocking fn dbl`, literal + variable args) → `42`/`200`; C unit test calls `march_run_blocking_i` on a real OS thread. compiler 293, codegen 318 (str_concat pre-existing), stdlib 783, forge 83, C ABI green.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-20, External Function Interface — Phase 5: forge build integration)

- **`forge.toml [ffi]` compiles + links C shim sources** so real C bindings work without editing the runtime. `[ffi] sources = ["native/x.c"]` (relative to project root) and `link = ["-lz"]` (raw linker flags). `forge build` resolves each source to an absolute path and passes `--ffi-c`/`--ffi-link` to the compiler. (`forge/lib/project.ml` parses the `[ffi]` section; `forge/lib/cmd_build.ml` `ffi_flags_of`/`compile_entry`.)
- **Compiler `--ffi-c <file.c>` / `--ffi-link <flag>`** (repeatable) — the native `--compile` path compiles each shim alongside the runtime and appends the link flags to the clang invocation (`bin/main.ml`).
- **Cache correctness**: the FFI shim sources + link flags are digested into the CAS binary key (`ffi_cas_tag`, applied to both the source-hash and TIR-module-hash cache layers), so editing a shim `.c` (which doesn't change the `.march` source) invalidates the cached binary — verified (edit `+1`→`+100` recompiles, no stale hit).
- **Verified**: a fixture project (`[ffi] sources=["native/checksum.c"]`, `extern … = "checksum_add"`) builds via `forge build` and runs → `42`; shim edit → `141`. forge 83 tests, build-check 7, compiler 293, codegen 318 (str_concat pre-existing), C ABI all green.
- **Limitations** (see `specs/c-ffi-gaps.md`): single `[ffi]` section only (no named `[[ffi]]` multi-lib yet); interpreter FFI (Phase 4) dlopens only the runtime `.so`, so user `[ffi]` shims are callable in **compiled** mode only, not under `march file.march`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-20, External Function Interface — Phase 4: interpreter FFI (primitives))

- **Primitive extern calls now run under the tree-walking interpreter** (`march file.march`, no `--compile`), not just compiled. `lib/eval/eval_ffi_stubs.c` provides `dlopen`/`dlsym` + fixed-arity trampolines (`march_eval_dyncall_i`/`_d`, distinct from the JIT's stubs); `VForeign` now carries the extern's param/return types, and the call path falls back from the static `foreign_stubs` table to a dynamic dlopen+trampoline call. `bin/main.ml` sets `Eval.ffi_runtime_so` to a lazy thunk over `ensure_runtime_so` so the runtime `.so` (with FFI symbols) is built/dlopened only when an extern is actually invoked — non-FFI programs pay nothing.
- **Scope**: no new dependency (no libffi). Supported in the interpreter: **Int/Bool arguments and Int/Bool/Float returns** (GP-register trampoline). Float-as-argument, String/Bytes, Option/Result, and resources are not marshallable interpreter-side (value-representation mismatch) and produce a clear `… run with --compile` error rather than crashing. Compiled mode remains fully featured.
- **Verified**: `test/native/ffi_interp` (interpreter mode) → `42`/`200`; unsupported bindings error cleanly; non-FFI programs and compiled FFI unaffected. compiler 293, codegen 318 (str_concat pre-existing; fixed on main), stdlib 783, C ABI green.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-20, External Function Interface — `consume` ownership + error protocol)

- **`consume` parameter mode** (`lib/parser/parser.mly`, `lib/ast/ast.ml` `ef_param_consumed`, `lib/tir/tir.ml` `ed_consumed`, `lib/tir/lower.ml`, `lib/tir/borrow.ml`): an extern param prefixed `consume` transfers ownership to the binding — Perceus does NOT emit a caller-side drop, so a binding like `fn finish(consume h: Acc): Int` reads then `march_drop`s the resource itself. Without it, a binding that drops its arg double-freed (RC underflow). `consume` is a **contextual** word, not a reserved keyword (it is a common identifier in linear-types code): recognized only as the leading word of an extern parameter via a two-identifier grammar rule. The formatter prints it back (round-trip safe).
- **Error protocol confirmed**: a fallible binding declared `: Result(T, E)` returns `Ok`/`Err` built on the C side via `march_ok`/`march_err` — both arms round-trip (`test/native/ffi_result` → `7`/`nan`). (The env-routed `march_raise(env, …)` convenience is still deferred; `march_ok`/`march_err` cover the cases today.)
- **Verified** (`test/native/ffi_resource` now also exercises `consume finish` over 2000 cycles → `42`/`0`, no leak, no double-free). compiler 293, codegen 318 (2 pre-existing str_concat), stdlib 783, C ABI unit test all green.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-20, External Function Interface — Phase 3: resources)

- **Opaque native resources with destructor-on-drop** (`runtime/march_ffi.{h,c}`, `runtime/march_runtime.{c,h}`): `march_resource_type(name, dtor)` (idempotent registry), `march_resource_new(type_id, native_ptr)` (mints an `MARCH_RESOURCE_TAG` heap cell `[rc][tag][pad][native_ptr@16][dtor@24][type_id@32]`), `march_resource_get(v, type_id)` (type-checked borrow, aborts on mismatch via new `march_fatal`). Every RC free-on-zero path (`march_decrc`/`decrc_freed`/`decrc_local`) now runs the cell's destructor on the native pointer before freeing — so Perceus drops fire C cleanup deterministically.
- **`resource Name` declaration** (`lib/lexer/lexer.mll`, `lib/parser/parser.mly`): a new keyword that desugars to an abstract type (`DType` with empty `TDVariant []`) — stays heap-Boxed (never newtype/niche-unboxed by P6), RC-managed, marshallable in `extern` signatures, and only constructible/inspectable via extern functions.
- **Verified end-to-end** (`test/native/ffi_resource`): a `resource Acc` accumulator created/used/dropped through compiled March → correct value (42) and zero leak across 2000 create/drop cycles (destructor ran each time). C ABI unit test covers register/idempotency/new/get/dtor-on-drop/leak-balance.
- **Test count:** +`native_ffi_resource`, +resources block in `test/test_ffi.c`. Deferred to later increments: `march_env`/`march_raise` error protocol + `consume` syntax; the `resource` keyword could also gain generics (`resource Conn(a)`) later.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-20, compiler fix — interface-method-name collision)

- **User fn colliding with an interface method no longer hijacks dispatch** (`lib/tir/mono.ml`, `lib/tir/lower.ml`): defining a free top-level `fn show` (or `to_string`, etc. — names of `Show`/`ToString`/`Eq`/`Ord`/`Hash` methods) silently miscompiled, because prelude generics (`println`→`show`, `str`/`debug`→`to_string`) and direct calls mis-resolved, feeding a String where an Int was expected (segfault in `march_print`). Two fixes: mono dispatches a user-fn call to the interface impl when the user fn's first-param type doesn't match the concrete arg type but an impl does; lower scopes the entry module's own fn names during Pass 2 (like nested modules) so a bare call to the user's `show` resolves to the user's fn, matching the typechecker. Regression test: `test/native/iface_method_collision` (prints 7 then ok).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-20, P6 Milestone 2 — Option-shaped niche encoding)

- **Option-niche encoding** (`lib/tir/repr.ml`, `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, `runtime/march_extras.c`): Option-like ADTs (1 nullary + 1 single-field ctor) now use `None=0, Some(x)=x` — no heap allocation for either variant. `repr_of_ty` classifies as `Niche { payload; tagged }` when payload passes `niche_payload_ok` (TInt, TBool, TString, TPtr, Boxed TCon); Float/Unit/TVar/nested-niche stay Boxed. `llvm_emit`: `EAlloc` niche fast path, `EReuse` skips reuse, `emit_case` null-check dispatch, `llvm_param_ty` suppresses nonnull for niche params. `record_get` passes kind char as 3rd arg. Runtime: `make_some(v)=v`, `make_none()=null`; kind-aware `rec_some_k`/`rec_none_k`. 6 new repr unit tests.
- **Test count: 1602** (281 compiler + 224 eval + 317 codegen [315 pass; 2 pre-existing str_concat failures] + 782 stdlib; vault scalar adversarial test green).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-20, P6 Milestone 1 — unboxed single-field constructors)

- **Newtype unboxing** (`lib/tir/repr.ml` new, `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`): single-variant single-field ADTs (newtypes) now bypass heap allocation. `repr_of_ty` classifies a monomorphic `TCon` as `Newtype payload` or `Boxed`; Float-payload newtypes stay Boxed. `payload_needs_tag` detects scalars (Int/Bool and newtypes-over-scalars) that must be tagged `(v<<1)|1` to satisfy `IS_HEAP_PTR`. Three `llvm_emit` fast paths: `EAlloc` (construct — tag+inttoptr for scalar, ptr coerce for pointer), `EReuse` (FBIP reconstruct — skip RC reuse, manual DecRC of consumed box, emit new payload), `emit_case` (match — ptrtoint+ashr to recover scalar; strip Perceus `EDecRC` inside branch since ownership transfer is net-zero). Global `cur_type_defs` ref makes `repr_of_ty` available at all three sites.
- **1 new native test** (`test/native/newtype_counter.march`): `Counter(Int)` — `bump`/`unwrap` across function boundaries; output 42. Test count: **1596** (281 compiler + 224 eval + 309 codegen [307 pass; 2 pre-existing str_concat failures] + 782 stdlib; native newtype_counter green).
## Current State (as of 2026-06-20, External Function Interface — Phase 2: strings/bytes, Option/Result, borrow ownership)

- **ABI constructors** (`runtime/march_ffi.{h,c}`): `march_str_borrow`/`march_bytes_borrow` (zero-copy slice into the `march_string` data region), `march_str_new`/`march_bytes_new` (owned copies), `march_none`/`some`/`ok`/`err` (Option/Result cells; tags None/Ok=0, Some/Err=1), and `march_utf8_valid` (rejects bad leads, truncation, overlong, surrogates, out-of-range).
- **RC-leak gauge** (`runtime/march_runtime.c`): `march_live_allocs()` — a global atomic net-allocation counter (incremented in `march_alloc` + `march_string_alloc`, decremented in every free-on-rc=0 branch). Readable from March by binding it as an extern (`fn live_allocs(): Int = "march_live_allocs"`), no builtin needed.
- **Borrow-default ownership for extern args** (`lib/tir/borrow.ml`, `lib/tir/perceus.ml`): extern args were implicitly *consume* (no caller-side drop) → borrow-style bindings leaked one allocation per call. `Borrow.infer_module` now seeds user externs into the borrow map (heap params borrowed); Perceus gained a borrow-aware `ECallPtr` branch for known externs (gated on an `_extern_names` set, so closures/HOFs are untouched) that emits a caller-side `EDecRC` for borrowed dead-after args. Verified: leak delta 0 (was 2000) — regression test `test/native/ffi_leak`.
- **Strings cross the boundary as raw `ptr`** (no value-marshalling codegen needed); the C side calls `march_str_borrow`/`march_str_new`.
- **Option niche reconciliation (post-P6):** `march_some`/`march_none` now emit the compiler niche representation (`Some(x)=x`, `None=0`) for niche-eligible payloads, matching `make_some`/`make_none` — an FFI binding returning `Option(Int)` round-trips correctly (regression test `test/native/ffi_option` → `7`/`none`). `march_ok`/`march_err` stay boxed (Result is not niche-shaped). Option(Float)/Option(Unit) returns (boxed) are not yet supported by the bare constructors.
- **Test count: 1594** (281 compiler + 224 eval + 307 codegen + 782 stdlib; new: `capabilities/extern block counts as cap use`, `native_ffi_leak`) plus the expanded hermetic C ABI unit test (`test/test_ffi.c`: dup/drop gauge, borrow, embedded NUL, UTF-8 edges, constructors, 1000-iter leak balance). **Deferred to later increments:** `march_env`/`march_raise` env-routed errors and `consume` syntax (bindings build `Result` via `march_ok`/`march_err` directly); resources (Phase 3). (The `Some(7)`/`Ok(7)` cross-function crash first seen while testing this — originally suspected to be an optimizer bug — turned out to be an interface-method-name collision and is now FIXED; see the entry above. `--no-opt` builds remain broken by a missing `_http_fetch` symbol, tracked separately.)

## Current State (as of 2026-06-19, External Function Interface — Phase 1: ABI core + primitives)

- **FFI ABI core** (`runtime/march_ffi.h`, `runtime/march_ffi.c`): the stable C ABI bindings link against (the `erl_nif.h` analog). Version handshake (`march_ffi_abi_version`/`MARCH_FFI_ABI_VERSION`); value accessors that hide the tagged-word convention (`march_make_int`/`get_int` = `(n<<1)|1`/`>>1`, `make_bool`/`get_bool`, `make_float`/`get_float` = untagged bitcast, `is_heap`/`as_ptr`/`from_ptr`); tag-aware `march_dup`/`march_drop` (no-op on tagged ints, incrc/decrc on heap). Wired into both the JIT `.so` build and the native `--compile` link in `bin/main.ml` (and `test/test_helpers.ml`); auto-invalidated by the runtime-header digest. Spec: `specs/2026-06-19-c-ffi-abi-design.md`; plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-19-c-ffi-phase1.md`.
- **`extern fn name(...) : T = "symbol"`** — bind an existing C symbol by explicit name (`lib/ast/ast.ml` `ef_symbol`, `lib/parser/parser.mly`, `lib/format/format.ml`, `lib/tir/lower.ml`). Falls back to the original `<lib>_<fn>` convention when omitted. Primitive (Int/Float/Bool) marshalling verified end-to-end (compiled `dbl(21)` → `42`).
- **Test count: 1575** (281 compiler + 224 eval + 286 codegen + 780 stdlib; 2 new codegen tests in the `capabilities` group) plus a standalone hermetic C ABI unit test (`test/test_ffi.c`, `dune build test/test_ffi_runner`). ## Current State (as of 2026-06-19, P1 Layer 1 — alpha-merge let-floating pre-Perceus)

- **P1 join-points Layer 1** (`lib/tir/join_points.ml`, `bin/main.ml`): new `run_pre` entry point floats a common leading `let` above an `ECase` even when arms bind the shared RHS under *different* fresh ANF names — it mints one `$jp…` binder and substitutes each arm's binder onto it via a new `rename_expr` helper. Wired BEFORE `Perceus.perceus` (post-Perceus renaming of RC-decorated bindings is unsafe), so RC is inserted once for the hoisted binding. The existing conservative `run` (identical binder names) still runs in the post-Perceus opt loop. Because TIR is in ANF, this captures the spec's motivating shared-inline-subexpr case. Layers 2–3 (interleaved lets; general non-let CSE) remain open — see `specs/optimizations.md` §P1.
- **2 new tests** in `join_points` group (`pre_float_alpha`, `pre_no_float_diff_rhs`). Verified: compiler 281 + eval 224 + codegen 304 (302 pass; 2 pre-existing unrelated `simplify` str-concat failures) + stdlib 782 all green; `tree_transform`/`list_ops` benchmarks unchanged.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-18, Phase 3c — `cap no_panic` module directive)

- **`cap no_panic` directive** (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`): `check_no_panic_module` with static `panic_surface_direct` (`/`, `%`, `int_div`, `panic_`, `todo_`, `unreachable_`), `panic_surface_prelude` (`unwrap`, `expect`, `head`, `tail`, `last`), and `panic_surface_stdlib` (`List.nth`, `Option.unwrap`, `Array.get`, etc.) tables. Seed+fixpoint transitive analysis: direct callers seeded, then propagated to transitive callers within the same module. Errors point at call-site span with per-site suggestion (e.g. use `Math.checked_div` instead of `/`).
- **AST, lexer, parser changes**: `CAP_NO_PANIC` compound token (`"cap" whitespace+ "no_panic"`); `DOpts of string list * span` node; passthrough in desugar/eval/coverage/format/LSP/lower/refactor/span_remap.

- **12 new tests** in `cap_no_panic` suite. **248 compiler / 1535 total tests pass.**

## Current State (as of 2026-06-18, Phase 3b — Policy-tag DCE/audit pass)

- **`lib/tir/policy_dce.ml` (new, ~260 lines)** — audit pass that walks every `Tagged(_, P)`-parameterized function in a TIR module after monomorphization and reports policy violations. Policy table: `NoAlloc` (no `EAlloc`/`EStackAlloc`), `NoPanic` (no transitive calls to panic-surface builtins), `NoIO` (no calls to IO-needful modules), and `Realtime` (all three). Panic analysis uses a fixpoint seeded from `direct_panic_builtins` (`int_div`, `panic_`, etc.). IO analysis uses `tm_io_fns` module-name list via prefix matching and a second fixpoint for transitive callers.
- **`tm_io_fns : string list`** — new field on `tir_module` that stores IO-module names extracted from `typecheck_env.module_caps` at compile time. Populated in `bin/main.ml` after `check_module_full`; empty in JIT/eval paths.
- **Pipeline integration** — audit runs after `Fusion.run` and before `Defun.defunctionalize` in `bin/main.ml`; same position added to JIT pipeline in `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml`. Violations print to stderr and cause `exit 1`.
- **8 new unit tests** in `policy_dce` suite (TIR-level direct calls to `Policy_dce.audit`). **236 compiler / 780 total tests pass.**

## Current State (as of 2026-06-18, P1 + P8 + P10 all phases + P11 + P12 + P15 compiler optimizations)

- **P10 Phase 3 — NativeArray-backed DataFrame columns** (`stdlib/dataframe.march`): Migrated `IntCol`/`FloatCol`/`NullableIntCol`/`NullableFloatCol` from `TypedArray(Int/Float)` to `NativeIntArr`/`NativeFloatArr`. Added `native_int_arr_filter_mask` and `native_float_arr_filter_mask` builtins (eval.ml + typecheck.ml). Updated 20+ sites: col_len, col_value_at, col_to_value_list, filter_col_by_indices, filter_col_by_mask, builder_to_column, get_int/float_col, float_list, eval_col_expr literals + arithmetic, col_from_idx_opts, aggregation helpers, window ops. StrCol/BoolCol and null bitmaps stay as TypedArray. 780 tests pass.
- **P11 — Beta-ADT / case-of-known-constructor** (`lib/tir/beta_adt.ml`, new ~84 lines): matches `let r = EAlloc(tag, args) in ECase(r, [{tag; vs; body}], default)` and reduces to `let v0=args[0] in … in body`. Run pre-Perceus in `bin/main.ml` (before RC insertion). NOT in post-Perceus opt loop: Perceus inserts `EDecRC(scrutinee)` in the matching branch body, which would reference unbound `r` after P11 eliminates the ELet. `tags_match` handles qualified (`Result.Ok`) vs bare (`Ok`) tag names via `short_name` helper.
- **P12 — Variable copy propagation** (`lib/tir/cprop.ml`): added `avar_env : (string * Tir.var) list` tracking `let x = y` variable aliases in addition to the existing literal env. Excludes `TFn`/`TVar` closure types (ECallPtr dispatch is name-sensitive in llvm_emit). Uses `~allow_avar:false` for ECallPtr's function argument position. RC operations (EFree/EIncRC/EDecRC/EAtomicIncRC/EAtomicDecRC) are never substituted.
- **P10 Phase 2 — NativeArray compiled path** (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, `runtime/march_runtime.c`): All 16 `native_int_arr_*` / `native_float_arr_*` builtins registered in `is_builtin_fn`, `builtin_ret_ty`, and the LLVM preamble. Fixed `native_int_arr_from_list` / `to_list` integer-tagging bug (`(raw & 1) ? raw >> 1 : raw` untag, `(v << 1) | 1` retag). 2 new IR-level tests in `native_arrays` group.
- **P8 — FBIP cross-tag constructor reuse** (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`): replaced `shape_matches` (name equality) with `same_arity` (field-count equality). `add_scrutinee_free_for` encodes the consumed ctor's arity as dummy `TUnit` type-args in the DecRC var's type (`TCon(qualified_tag, [TUnit; …])`). `try_fbip_sink` and `fbip_expr` both use `same_arity dec_v.v_ty (List.length args)`. Cross-tag reuse safe because March allocates `[tag + nfields × ptr]` blocks — same arity ⟺ same block size.
- **P1 — Let-floating past ECase** (`lib/tir/join_points.ml`, new file): detects `let` bindings that appear identically at the start of EVERY case branch and hoists them above the `ECase`. Safety: same var name, `expr_eq` RHS, RHS doesn't mention pattern-bound vars, name not pattern-bound. First pass in the `opt.ml` fixed-point loop.
- **P15 — Boolean absorber peepholes** (`lib/tir/simplify.ml`): `x && false = false`, `false && x = false`, `x || true = true`, `true || x = true`, `not true = false`, `not false = true`. Fire after cprop propagates boolean constants into guards.
- **Fold: boolean comparison identities + int literal comparison folding** (`lib/tir/fold.ml`): `x==true→x`, `x==false→not x`, `a<b→bool` for literal operands. Fire after cprop produces known-constant comparisons.
- **29 new tests** (3 beta_adt + 3 cprop P12 + 3 cprop previously + 5 fbip_p8 + 4 join_points + 5 simplify P15 + 4 fold + 2 native_arrays). Test count: **1537** (302 codegen + 780 stdlib).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-18, P13 + P14 compiler optimizations)

- **P13 — EField of known record** (`lib/tir/cprop.ml`): added a `field_env` (separate from the literal env) that tracks `ERecord`, `EUpdate`, and record-alias bindings. `EField(AVar r, k)` folds to the atom at position `k` when `r` is in fenv. `EUpdate` merges new fields over the base record's known fields, so `{r with y=99}.x` still folds `x` from the base. RC operations are never substituted.
- **P14 — Boolean simplify peepholes** (`lib/tir/simplify.ml`): `if x then true else false → x`, `if x then false else true → not x`, `x == x → true` (non-float), `x != x → false` (non-float). Guards against float identity (IEEE 754 NaN semantics).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-18, `let?` result-propagation binding)

- **`let? p = e` syntax** — binds the `Ok` payload of a `Result` expression and propagates `Err` upward automatically. Requires `Result` on the RHS; the continuation (everything after the `let?`) must also produce a `Result` with a compatible error type. Empty continuation (i.e. `let?` as the last expression in a block) is a clear compile-time error.
- **`fold_letq`** in the parser right-folds a flat list of block statements into nested `ELetQ` continuations at parse time, so the typechecker sees the full chain as a single expression.
- **TIR lowering** desugars `ELetQ` to `match Ok(p) -> cont | Err($letq_err) -> Err($letq_err)`, keeping the interpreter path separate for precise diagnostics.
- **9 new tests** (4 eval + 5 compiler). Test count: **1485** (219 compiler + 219 eval + 267 codegen + 780 stdlib; 2 pre-existing `parser gaps` failures).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-17, Phase 2c/2d — Tagged(X,T) specialization tags + env records)

- **`Tagged(X, T)` type constructor** added to `builtin_types` with arity 2. `Tagged(DSP, Realtime)` and similar phantom specialization tags are now valid type annotations. `cap_paths_in_surface_ty` explicitly skips `Tagged` args so the capability-checking pass does not extract spurious cap paths from inside a `Tagged` type.
- **Realtime exclusion (Check 7)** added to `check_module_needs`: a function with `Tagged(_, Realtime)` in its parameter list cannot also take `Cap(Alloc)`, `Cap(IO)`, or `Cap(Panic)`. The compiler emits a clear error with the forbidden capability name and a hint to remove it. Functions tagged with non-`Realtime` policies (e.g. `Tagged(DSP, Standard)`) are unrestricted.
- **Phase 2d — environment records**: library convention documented. Bundle capabilities into a record type; narrow by constructing a smaller record at the call site; test with function fields alongside cap fields (swap behavior, keep the cap as the compile-time permission gate). No new compiler work — ordinary March record syntax already expresses the pattern. Documented in `docs/capabilities.md §Phase 2d`.
- **5 new tests** in the `tag_and_typestate` suite: `Tagged(X,T) valid annotation`, `Tagged+Cap(IO) error`, `Tagged+Cap(Alloc) error`, `Tagged+Cap(Panic) error`, `Tagged(Standard) no exclusion`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-19, Phase 3f tooling — forge cap coverage, LSP proof cap go-to-def/find-refs, cap inlay hints)

- **`forge cap coverage --static`** (`forge/lib/cmd_cap.ml`): static call-graph BFS analysis. Collects all `proof cap` declarations across the project, builds a cross-file call graph by scanning function bodies for `DFn`/`DLet` calls, BFS-expands from each `proof cap` declaration, and reports which capability declarations reach which files/modules. Output groups by capability name with the transitive file reach. Exposed as `forge cap coverage [--dir DIR]`.
- **LSP `proof cap` go-to-def and find-references**: `Analysis.t` gains a `proof_cap_defs` field (`(string, Ast.span) Hashtbl.t`). Phase 1 (`register_proof_caps`) recurses into `DMod` to populate both `def_map` and `proof_cap_defs`. Phase 2 (`collect_proof_cap_uses`, `collect_cap_ty_refs`) walks `Cap(X)` type annotations in function parameters and `DNeeds` paths, adding each use to `use_map`. Existing go-to-def/find-refs machinery handles dispatch from there.
- **LSP cap inlay hints** (`⬡ IO.Console` after call sites): emitted only when the file contains `DNeeds` declarations. A `builtin_cap_tbl` of 25 builtins maps call names to capability paths (e.g. `"println" → "IO.Console"`, `"file_read" → "IO.FileRead"`, `"http_get" → "Net.HTTP"`). `walk_cap_decls` visits `DFn`/`DLet`/`DMod`/`DTest`/`DDescribe` bodies and annotates `EApp(EVar n, ...)` call sites that match the table. Gated on the `perf_annotations` flag.
- **`user_decls` filter fix**: the `analyse` filter that selects user-file declarations was returning `dummy_span` for `DNeeds`, `DProofCap`, `DOpts`, `DTransitions`, `DAlwaysLinearType`, `DSetup`, and `DSetupAll`, silently dropping them. Added explicit span extraction for all seven types — this was the root cause of all capability LSP features being invisible to prior analysis passes. Two pre-existing LSP test failures were inadvertently fixed as a side effect.
- **4 new LSP tests** in the `capability tooling (phase 3f)` suite: `proof cap defs registered`, `proof cap go-to-def resolves declaration`, `proof cap find-refs finds type annotations`, `cap inlay hint emitted for builtin in needs module`. All passing.
- **Test count: 1485** (unchanged; same 2 pre-existing `parser gaps` failures; LSP failures reduced from 24 to 22 pre-existing).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-17, Phase 2f tooling — transitions hover, via completions, forge cap query)

- **LSP transitions hover**: `Analysis.t` gains `transitions_index` (handle name → `Ast.transition list`) and `always_linear_names` fields, both populated from `user_decls` in `analyse`. New `typestate_hover_at` finds the `Handle(R, S)` type at the cursor, matches against `transitions_index`, and emits a markdown card showing "always-linear" badge, current state, and all declared transitions from that state. Wired via `query_typestate_hover_at` into `server.ml`'s hover assembler alongside the existing perf/doc/actor hover sections.
- **LSP `via` completions**: `via_completions` in `completions_at` detects the `via <cursor>` position in a `transitions` arm (line contains `->` + ` via`), scans backwards in source for the enclosing handle name, and filters `a.vars` to type-compatible transition functions (`handle_name(...) -> handle_name(...)`) — surfaced before the generic var/type/ctor fallback.
- **`forge cap query`** (`forge/lib/cmd_cap.ml`): walks all `.march` files under the project root (or `--dir`), parses each with `March_parser`, recursively extracts `DNeeds` / `DAlwaysLinearType` / `DTransitions` / `DProofCap` declarations (through `DMod`), and prints a grouped file-by-file summary. Exposed as `forge cap query [--dir DIR]`. `forge/lib/dune` gains `march_ast march_lexer march_parser` deps.
- **Test count: 1470** (unchanged; 2 pre-existing `parser gaps` failures).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-17, Phase 2c compiler-enforced transitions blocks)

- **`transitions Handle do R: S1 -> S2 via fn_name end` — compiler-enforced typestate transition declarations.** Verifies that each `via` function exists and has the correct transition-shaped type (`Handle(..., S1) -> Handle(..., S2)`). Mismatched from/to-state types and missing functions are reported as errors. The typechecker also scans local functions for transition-shaped types not listed in the block and emits a warning with the suggested `R: from -> to via fn` declaration text. `DTransitions` AST node propagated through all pass-through sites (span_remap, dump, eval/repl, format, tir/lower, jit). Format.ml emits syntactically valid `transitions ... do ... end` blocks. LSP `collect_decl` registers each `via fn_name` span in `use_map` enabling go-to-def on via names.
- **`always_linear type T(params) = ...` — unconditional linearity declaration.** Every binding of an always-linear type is automatically tracked as linear by the typechecker — no `linear` annotation at each call site. Drop-without-use and double-use are compile-time errors. Auto-promotion fires at all three binding sites: `let`-bindings (infer_block, with Poly-scheme fix for phantom type params), named `fn` parameters (check_fn FPNamed path), and lambda parameters (bind_lam_param). Both bare (`"Handle"`) and qualified (`"Handle.Handle"`) names registered in `env.always_linear_types` so TCon matching works regardless of call site. `DAlwaysLinearType` AST node propagated through all 17 pattern-match sites (span_remap, coverage, desugar, dump, eval, format, jit, lint, modules, refactor, repl, search, tir/lower, lsp/analysis). `check_linear_all_consumed` called after each linear let-block completes.
- **`tag Foo` — zero-arg phantom label type.** Parser-level sugar: `tag Open` desugars to `type Open = Open`. Used to declare typestate labels without boilerplate. No new AST node; two files changed (lexer + parser).
- **`stdlib/handle.march` — `Handle(r, s)` typestate handle.** `always_linear type Handle(r, s) = Handle(Int)` — phantom params `r` (resource) and `s` (state) enforced at call sites via the type system. Thread-through idiom validated: `connect → open → query → close` compiles and runs; wrong-state calls and leaked handles produce clear type errors.
- **Test count: 1470** (209 compiler + 214 eval + 267 codegen + 780 stdlib; 2 pre-existing `parser gaps` failures).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-16, Phase 2a proof capability tokens)

- **`proof cap Name` — unforgeable ambient-fact capability tokens.** A module declares `proof cap Migrated` and the compiler enforces that only that module can mint `Cap(Db.Migrated)`; external code must receive it as a parameter (pass-through is allowed). New surface syntax (`proof cap` compound token in lexer), `DProofCap` AST node, producer registry in `env.proof_caps`, two new typecheck checks: enhanced Check 1 names the declaring module when a proof cap is used without `needs`, and new Check 6 rejects return-type forgery outside the declaring module. No runtime changes — proof caps are runtime-erased like all `Cap(X)` types.
- **6 new tests** (capabilities 19–24), all passing. Test count: **~1460**.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-16, LSP Depot-aware intelligence)

- **LSP now understands the [Depot](https://github.com/march-language/depot) query-builder / schema / migration library** (`lsp/lib/depot.ml` new; `lsp/lib/analysis.ml` extended). The Depot pass is gated to files that call `Depot.*` (zero cost otherwise) and reuses the existing cross-file import resolver to see schemas defined in imported files.
  - **Schema model (A0–A3):** `Depot.Schema.define("table", { fields = {…} })` calls are extracted into a `schema` record list (`depot_schemas`) with column names, types, and source spans; query pipelines (`from_table("t") |> where_eq("col", v)`) are resolved back to their schema table to produce a `col_occ` index (`depot_col_occs`). Table occurrences (`depot_table_occs`) track `from_table`/`Migration.*` string args.
  - **Column intelligence (B1–B3):** column-name completion inside `where_eq`/`order_asc`/… string args (shows field names with type details); `depot/unknown-column` Warning with Levenshtein did-you-mean for typos; go-to-definition on a column string jumps to the field's definition span in the `Schema.define` call; hover on a column string returns the declared field type (e.g. `Int`).
  - **Table intelligence (B4):** table-name completion inside `from_table` arg; `depot/unknown-table` Warning with did-you-mean when no schema matches.
  - **Migration intelligence (C1–C4):** `migration_ops` extracts `CreateTable`/`AlterTable`/`CreateIndex`/`References` ops from `Migration.*` calls; `depot/schema-drift` Hint when a migration column isn't in the schema; `depot/missing-migration` Hint when a queried column has no creating migration; `depot/unknown-fk-column` Warning when a `references(table, { column = … })` column isn't in the referenced table's schema.
  - **SQL injection (D1):** `depot/sql-injection` Warning when `Connection.simple_query` receives a non-literal SQL arg.
  - All features conservative: silent when schema/table can't be resolved (no false positives on cross-file or partial code). **+19 LSP tests** (**319** total `march-lsp`). Full suite green.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-15, LSP `~H` Tier 4 — interpolation intelligence: hover/def/completion/diagnostics inside `${…}`)

- **`~H` Tier 4 complete.** Hover type, go-to-definition, completion, and diagnostic positioning all work inside `${…}` interpolation expressions in `~H` sigils — no new infrastructure required. `${expr}` is parsed as a real `expr` by `interp_parts` in `parser.mly`, so each interpolation expression has accurate source spans that land in `type_map`/`def_map`/`use_map` via the normal typechecker. The existing `query_type_at`/`query_definition_at`/`query_completions_at` position-based query path works transparently. Scanner unification done (previous commit). No changes to `analysis.ml` were needed for Tier 4 — the queries worked through the existing path. **+8 LSP tests** (**300** total `march-lsp`), covering: (a) attribute-value interp `href=${url}`, (b) field-access `${u.uname}`, (c) let-bound local def-jump, (d) multiple interps on one line with distinct types, (e) triple-quoted `~H"""…"""` sigil, (f) type-error diagnostic range lands on the interpolation line (not collapsed to line 0), (g) island `props=${someVar}` hover/def. All tests pass; no edge-case fixes to `analysis.ml` were required.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-15, LSP `~H` HTML-sigil intelligence: islands, structural editing, linting)

- **The LSP now understands `~H` HTML templates** (`lsp/lib/analysis.ml`). A shared traversal `collect_h_sigils` yields each `~H` sigil's content + byte offsets, mapped to source positions via `ofs_to_pos`/`extract_h_content` (string-literal spans only cover the opening quote, so content is recovered textually). Built on that:
  - **Island intelligence:** `islands_in_sigil` parses `<island name='X' …>` tags with the name's source span; go-to-definition jumps to `X.create`/`X.render`/module; completion after `name='` offers same-file/loaded components (modules exposing `create`+`render`); validation emits `html/unknown-island` Warning only for the high-confidence case (a module that IS loaded but lacks `create`/`render`) — deliberately conservative to avoid false positives on cross-file components (per-file analysis can't see them).
  - **Structural editing:** matching-tag-pair documentHighlight + linkedEditingRange (rename `<div>`/`</div>` together) via `tag_pairs_in_sigil`; folding ranges for multi-line elements (`collect_h_fold_ranges`); auto-close on typing `>` (`autoclose_tag_at`) wired through a new `documentOnTypeFormattingProvider` (`firstTriggerCharacter:">"`) + `textDocument/onTypeFormatting` handler (skips void/self-closing/`island`).
  - **Richer linting** (one `collect_html_lint` accumulator → diagnostics): `html/unknown-tag` (known-elements set + Levenshtein "did you mean", custom `-` elements skipped), `html/duplicate-attr`, `html/void-with-children` + `html/self-closing-nonvoid`, and `html/unsafe-interpolation` (`${…}` inside `<script>`/`<style>`). Scanner edge cases (`>`/quotes/`${}` inside attributes) were hardened after review; exact `~H` (not `~h`) matching.
  - Deferred follow-ups: scanner unification done (subsequent commit, −232 lines); Tier 4 hover/def/completion/diagnostics inside `${…}` done (subsequent commit, +8 tests). +21 LSP tests at this stage (**292** total `march-lsp` at time of commit). Full suite green.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-15, actionable Run/Debug code lenses + workspace/executeCommand)

- **LSP code lenses are now runnable.** `code_lens_item` carries an optional LSP `Command` (`cl_command : string option` + `cl_args : Yojson.Safe.t list`); perf-summary lenses keep `cl_command = None`. `build_action_lenses` (`lsp/lib/analysis.ml`) emits `▶ Run`/`🐞 Debug` lens pairs above each `test "…"` block (`march.runTest`/`march.debugTest`, args `[file_uri, test_name]`) and above `fn main` (`march.run`/`march.debug`, args `[file_uri]`), built in `analyse` independent of the TIR pass; `run_tir_pass` now appends perf lenses so action lenses survive. Server advertises `executeCommandProvider` (the four ids), attaches `Command.create` in the `textDocument/codeLens` handler, and dispatches `workspace/executeCommand` via the pure `resolve_lens_command` resolver: RUN commands shell out (`forge test --filter=<name> <file>` / `forge run`) returning `{status, exitCode, message}`; DEBUG commands return a non-blocking structured echo (`{status:"debug", command, dap:"march dap", arguments}`) so the editor drives its own DAP session. +7 LSP tests (215 in `test_lsp`).

- **Named record types now recover their declared name in `Tc.pp_ty`** (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`). Records are structural (`TRecord of (string * ty) list`, no nominal name), so a `type R = { … }` returned from a fn used to render as `{ a : Int, b : Int }`. A rendering-only fix (no unification-semantics change, so none of the 82 `TRecord` sites across typecheck/TIR/codegen were touched): a module-level `_record_names` index maps a record's sorted field-name signature → declared name, populated by `register_record_name` at all 5 `TDRecord` declaration sites; `pp_ty` consults `recover_record_name` and prefers the name when unambiguous (identical field sets across distinct records poison the signature → fall back to `{ … }`). All LSP type rendering routes through `pp_ty`, so hover (`Int -> R`), inlay hints (`R`), and the **"Add type annotation"** code action all now show/insert the name. The `annotatable_ty_str` guard in `lsp/lib/analysis.ml` (skip types containing `{`) now lets named records through because they no longer render with braces. +3 LSP tests; the stale `test_no_annotation_for_record_return` was rewritten to assert the new (correct) behavior. **1442** main-suite tests + **228** LSP tests pass (both exit 0). Caveat: the LSP `AnnFnReturn` action inserts `-> T` but March return syntax is `: T` — a pre-existing bug (fails for `Int` too), flagged separately; with the correct `: R` form the recovered annotation parses cleanly.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-15, fire-and-forget task RC fix + 250K-task scheduler stress test)

- **1450 tests passing** (unchanged — runtime fix, no new test suite entries).
- **Fire-and-forget task RC fix** (`runtime/march_runtime.c`): `march_task_spawn_thunk` and `march_task_spawn_with_cancel_thunk` now call `march_incrc(task)` after allocating the Task object (RC 1→2). `march_thunk_trampoline` calls `march_decrc(task)` after storing the tagged result and done flag (RC drops by 1). This prevents UAF when callers discard the handle (`let _ = task_spawn(...)`) — previously the caller's decrc could free the Task object while the trampoline's raw `wa->task` pointer was still live.
- **Scheduler stress test** (`test/native/sched_stress.march`): 2-level fan-out — 250 batch tasks each spawning 1000 worker tasks (250,250 green threads total). Each worker computes `fib(8..19)`. Throughput measured on 4-core M1: **250K tasks in 1.32s wall (~189K/s)**, **1M tasks in 4.79s wall (~209K/s)**. Scheduler exits cleanly (EXIT:0) as all fire-and-forget tasks drain to `g_live_procs == 0`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-15, fix: parallel task result tagging + task_await_unwrap + scheduler LIFO livelock)

- **1450 tests passing** (unchanged — these are pure correctness fixes, no new tests added).
- **Parallel task result tagging fixed** (`runtime/march_runtime.c`): `march_thunk_trampoline` now stores results as `(raw << 1) | 1` (tagged) in `task[3]`. The raw i64 from the apply function must be tagged because the `task_await` path goes through `mk_ok(result)` → `Ok(n)` pattern match, which loads the field as `ptr` (TVar → "ptr" in llvm_ty) and applies conditional `ashr 1` untag. Without tagging, integer results were halved (e.g., count = 25 was returned as 12). Added `march_task_await_value` C function that spins until done and returns the tagged raw value directly (no `Ok` wrapper), used by `task_await_unwrap` to avoid a heap allocation.
- **`task_await_unwrap` fixed** (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`): was reading field index 0 (offset 16 = proc ptr) with no spin-wait. Now calls `march_task_await_value` (spins on done flag), reads the tagged result, applies `coerce ptr→i64` (conditional ashr untag), and for heap-ptr inner types applies bare `inttoptr`. Also added `declare ptr @march_task_await_value(ptr %task)` in both LLVM preamble sections.
- **Scheduler LIFO livelock fixed** (`runtime/march_scheduler.c`): When all workers had spin-waiting green threads in their local deques (pop end), leaf tasks at the steal end never got scheduled — LIFO pop kept re-running the waiting task before executing its subtasks. Fix: when the previous task cooperatively yielded (`last_yielded` flag), the scheduler tries to steal from other workers first before re-running the yielded task. This breaks the livelock by ensuring leaf tasks (which complete the work spin-waiters are blocked on) get scheduled. Verified: `par_fib(N, 3)` for N up to 20 gives correct Fibonacci values; prime counting with `par(k)` matches sequential counts.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-15, actor field-order fix + scheduler Phase 5 + async mailbox dispatch)

- **1450 tests passing** (up from 1442 before this work).
- **Actor struct field-order bug fixed** (`lib/tir/lower.ml`): sentinel fields renamed `$dispatch` → `$d_dispatch` and `$alive` → `$e_alive` so alphabetical sort in `get_record_fields` matches allocation order; `march_send_checked` implemented in `runtime/march_runtime.c` with revocation check and proper Result-object cleanup; native test `test/native/actor_counter` added.
- **Green-thread scheduler Phase 5 wired** (`bin/main.ml`, `runtime/march_runtime.c`, `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`): `march_scheduler.c` now always linked (not HTTP-gated); `task_await` replaced with real spin-blocking C implementation that stores the task return value; `task_yield` calls `march_sched_yield()`; `task_reductions` reads TLS counter; 5 cancel-token builtins fully codegen'd; 4 IR correctness tests added.
- **Async mailbox dispatch** (`lib/eval/eval.ml`, `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`): `BlockedOnReceive` cooperative blocking in the interpreter — `receive()` parks when mailbox empty, scheduler re-queues the message and retries next pass; `receive()` → `@march_sched_recv()` wired in compiled mode with LLVM declaration; 4 tests covering blocking, no-deadlock, FIFO ordering, and LLVM IR.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-13, forge toolchain/version-manager security & robustness hardening)

- **Adversarial-review fixes across the forge version manager** (`forge/lib/toolchain.ml`, `cmd_build.ml`, `cmd_run.ml`, `resolver_lockfile.ml`). A 3-lens adversarial review (security / correctness / resources) plus verification turned up and fixed: (1) **shell injection** — `MARCH_LIB_PATH` paths are now `Filename.quote`d in `cmd_build`/`cmd_run` (a dep `path` or project dir with a shell metachar previously injected into the `Sys.command` build/run line). (2) **Tag validation** — `valid_tag` rejects anything outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` (and `.`/`..`), applied to `.march-version` pins (ignored + warned), `resolve_tag`, and the `use`/`uninstall`/`pin` args; closes path traversal via `forge toolchain uninstall '../..'` and `versions/<tag>` escapes. (3) **Install data-loss** — the download/extract temp dir is now a race-free `mktemp -d` **under `~/.march`**, so the final move into `versions/<tag>` is a same-filesystem rename rather than a cross-fs copy that could fail after `rm -rf dest` had already destroyed a working toolchain. (4) **Constraint gap** — a `forge.toml` `march` constraint with no pinned/active toolchain now warns instead of silently building with PATH `march`. (5) **`resolve_tag` consistency** — stable detection uses `not (is_nightly …)` (a new pure `default_tag`) instead of a `v`-prefix check. (6) **Channel safety** — `Fun.protect` + temp-file-and-rename for `forge.lock` writes (no truncated lockfile on `ENOSPC`), and protected reads/wrapper-writes/pin-writes. Verified e2e (traversal rejected, injection does not fire, install/reinstall intact) + 8 new unit tests (`valid_tag`, `default_tag`). The review's one "critical" (build → `rm -rf` arbitrary path) was **downgraded after verification**: `install` network-gates on the GitHub API before any delete, so a bad pin 404s first.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-13, prebuilt install hardening: static macOS link, march --version, fail-closed checksums)

- **Hardening pass over the prebuilt-install path.** (1) **Static macOS linking** — the macOS release binary now statically links `blake3`/`zstd`/`brotli` (via `lib/cas/discover.ml` preferring `libblake3.a` and a new `lib/eval/discover_compress.ml` preferring the zstd/brotli `.a`), so `otool -L` shows only `/usr/lib/libSystem` + `libz`; no Homebrew dylib dependency. Both have a **dynamic fallback** when the static archive is absent (local dev unaffected), verified both ways. `build.yml` builds `libblake3.a` from source on macOS (Homebrew ships only the dylib). (2) **`march --version`** added (prints `march 0.1.0`; install.sh uses it as a post-install run check that prints a friendly hint if the binary won't start). (3) **Checksums now fail closed** — `install.sh` and `Toolchain.verify_checksum` reject a download with no checksums asset / no matching entry / a mismatch (previously a missing entry was a silent skip). (4) **Docs corrected** — `march --compile` requires a `clang`/LLVM toolchain (it shells out to clang); the prebuilt only needs that for native compile, not for interpreting. (5) **`forge toolchain install` no longer auto-switches** the active version unless it's the first install (rustup-style), and `set_current` swaps the `current` symlink atomically (temp + rename). (6) **`install.sh` bootstrap** now uses the stable `raw.githubusercontent.com/.../main/install.sh` URL (works without a tagged stable release; `releases/latest` skips prereleases). (7) **Unit tests** for the pure `Toolchain` functions (`platform_of_uname`, `expected_hash_for`) — 7 cases in `forge/test/test_forge.ml`.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-13, prebuilt-binary install + forge toolchain version manager)

- **March is now installable from a downloaded prebuilt, and forge manages toolchain versions rustup-style.** Three pieces land together: (1) **`install.sh`** (repo root, attached to releases) — a POSIX installer that detects the platform, resolves a release via the GitHub API (latest stable, falling back to newest nightly; `MARCH_VERSION` override), downloads the tarball + checksums, verifies sha256, extracts into `~/.march/versions/<tag>/`, points `~/.march/current` at it, and writes `~/.march/bin/{march,forge}` wrapper scripts. (2) **`forge toolchain install|use|list|uninstall`** (`forge/lib/toolchain.ml`, wired in `forge/bin/main.ml`) — the same layout, managed in-process (yojson for the API, digestif for checksums, curl/tar shelled out); a new subcommand group that does **not** collide with the existing archive-install `forge install`. (3) **Release archives now bundle `runtime/*.c` + `*.h`** (`build.yml`, all three build steps), so `march --compile` works from a prebuilt — previously the archive shipped only `bin/` + `stdlib/` and native compile failed with `cannot find runtime/march_runtime.c`. The wrapper-through-`current` indirection makes `march` resolve its bundled `stdlib/` and `runtime/` correctly on both macOS (invoked-path) and Linux (`/proc/self/exe`). Verified end-to-end on darwin-arm64 against the live nightly: download → checksum → install → interpret, native `--compile` → runnable binary (with runtime present), and all four `forge toolchain` subcommands (install/list/use/uninstall, including the active-toolchain uninstall guard). Also fixed: every `github.com/march-lang/*` URL → `march-language` (the org renamed; the old org 404s), the checksums file now uses the standard `<hash>  <file>` format (`sha256sum -c` works), the release-note `forge install/use` wording → `forge toolchain`, and the phantom `darwin-x86_64` nightly-manifest entry (no such build target) removed.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-12, fix(resolver): lib-module type errors no longer silently swallowed)

- **Type errors in modules loaded via a relative `MARCH_LIB_PATH` (or relative source-dir imports) are reported again.** The resolver canonicalises `loaded_paths` via realpath (LSP/CLI unification) but parses files under their as-given path — spans carry the as-given string, so `is_user_file`'s exact-string membership failed and every lib-module diagnostic was tolerated as "stdlib-internal". Whole-program builds of ill-typed projects proceeded into codegen: the depot repro had **70 swallowed type errors** and failed only at clang with `use of undefined value '@$f27180'` (an `Ok(v)` pattern binder never bound because the ill-typed Bool scrutinee took `emit_case`'s integer-switch path). `user_files` now records both the as-given and canonical spelling of every loaded file. Regression: `test/imports/lib_path_ill_typed/` (relative lib path, `--check` + `march test` exit 1); the pre-existing `entry_imports_ill_typed` fixtures (which had silently become live failures) pass again. Note: depot itself still has those 70 genuine type errors to fix — the compiler now *reports* them instead of miscompiling.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-18, feat(typecheck): Phase 3a explicit bounded type parameters)

- **Explicit bounded type parameters land.** `fn f[s : ConnState](h : Handle(s)) : ...` syntax is now parsed and typechecked. Parser: optional `[lower_name : BoundType, ...]` bracket clause between function name and `(`. AST: `fn_bounds : (string * ty) list` on `DFn`/`DPFn`. Typecheck: `check_fn` registers each bound var in `fn_tvars` and adds constraints to `pending_constraints` — `CADTBound` for user ADT bounds, `CInterface` for known interfaces (Eq/Ord/Show/etc.), `CTNatBound` for `Nat`. `discharge_constraints` checks concrete-typed vars against their ADT/Nat bound; polymorphic type vars are skipped (constraint deferred). Error message: "`Int` is not a variant of `ConnState`." Multiple bounds per function (`[s : A, t : B]`) are supported. 9 new tests (tag_and_typestate 12–20). **228 compiler tests pass.**

## Current State (as of 2026-06-12, fix(llvm): erased-type closure indirect call)

- **Indirect calls through an erased-type closure no longer produce invalid IR.** A closure parameter captured into another lambda's environment loses its `TFn` type through the erased env slot (TVar → i64); `ECallPtr` dispatch in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` assumed the callee atom was already `ptr` and fed the raw i64 to the fn-pointer `getelementptr` — clang rejected the module (`'%ldNN' defined with type 'i64' but expected 'ptr'`). Hit by depot's `Depot.Migration.run` (`__try_call(fn _ -> thunk())`). The callee is now coerced to ptr before the field load, matching the arg-coercion idiom already on that path. Fixture: `test/imports/erased_clo_native/` (compile-and-run). Found while verifying the record introspection builtins against the depot repro; that repro's remaining failure is depot-side (`lib/transaction.march` violates `__try_call`'s Bool-only contract — march rejects it when typechecked standalone, but multi-module whole-program inference misses it; tracked as a separate typecheck-consistency issue).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-12, cold-checkout `dune runtest` self-sufficiency + `march check` imported-module errors)

- **`dune runtest` now passes from a cold checkout (no prior `dune build`), and every native-compile fixture tracks `runtime/*.c`.** Each native-compile fixture rule in `test/dune` invokes `main.exe --compile`, which locates the C runtime exe-relative (`../runtime` from `_build/default/bin/main.exe`) — but `_build/default/runtime` was only materialized by the `warm-cache` alias in `bin/dune`, which is attached to `@all`, not `@runtest`. On a warm `_build` the tests passed, so the gap was invisible; cold, they failed with `march: cannot find runtime/march_runtime.c`. **All five** such rules now declare `(source_tree ../runtime)` in their deps — `adt_eq_native`, `record_native`, `param_shadow_native` (a5af8ab) plus `native_default_args_nested` and `native_zero_arg_closure_default` (the remaining two `--compile` rules). Besides making `@runtest` self-sufficient, the dep also correctly invalidates each cached native binary whenever any `runtime/*.c` changes — closing the stale-runtime footgun that produced phantom link failures. Verified: both newly-tracked fixtures compile, run, and diff-match expected output through dune.
- **The `march check` subcommand (multi-file, used by forge) now reports type errors in modules pulled in by import resolution.** `run_check_cmd` filtered diagnostics against the command-line file list only, so errors in auto-discovered source-dir / `MARCH_LIB_PATH` siblings were dropped as "stdlib-internal" — the same class of bug as the a8ca488/resolver regression, on a third code path. It now merges each entry's `user_files` (which carry parse-time span paths since the resolver fix) into the membership filter. A dedicated `test/dune` regression rule now guards this third path (the bare `check` subcommand — previously only `march test` and `--check` were covered). Verified: `march check imports/entry_imports_ill_typed.march` exits 1 with the imported module's arity error rendered, for both source-dir siblings and relative `MARCH_LIB_PATH`; well-typed multi-file entries still exit 0.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-12, cross-session-safe ~/.cache/march)

- **The shared `~/.cache/march` directory is now safe under concurrent sessions/worktrees.** All five cache writers are content-hash-keyed and write atomically (pid-suffixed temp + `rename(2)`). Previously, `libmarch_runtime.so` (`ensure_runtime_so`, mtime check on `march_runtime.c` only) and `libmarch_rt_test.so` (`setup_jit_runtime`, existence check only — never invalidated) were single fixed-name files: a concurrent worktree with diverged runtime sources overwrote them with ABI-mismatched binaries, hanging whichever session dlopen'd the wrong build, and readers could dlopen half-written .so files mid-compile. Now `libmarch_runtime_<hash16>.so` / `libmarch_rt_test_<hash16>.so` digest every compiled .c, every `runtime/*.h`, and the clang flags — identical inputs share one artifact, divergent trees get separate files. The already-hash-keyed writers (`stdlib_prelude_<h>.so`/`.names`, `stdlib_ast_<h>.bin`, `stdlib_tcenv_<h>.bin`) gained atomic writes; the prelude `.names` publishes before its `.so` so a visible .so always implies complete names. Verified: fresh/warm REPL runs, runtime-edit produces a new key without touching the old artifact, JIT alcotest group green on an isolated HOME, full suite green.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-12, MARCH_LIB_PATH auto-discovery regression + codegen guard for type-incorrect TIR)

- **Hardening on top of the 78afbbc user_files fix, from an independent rediscovery of the same root cause.** Traced from a fresh symptom: depot's `MARCH_LIB_PATH=lib:test --compile --test` accepted `Transaction.run`'s `__try_call` contract violation and died in clang with `use of undefined value '@$f27180'` — an i64-typed `match` scrutinee took `emit_case`'s integer-switch path (which never binds `br_vars`), and each unbound binder use became a 0-arg call to an undefined global. This change adds the two pieces 78afbbc lacked: (1) regression fixture `test/libpath_ill_typed/` + `test/libpath_entry.march` covering the **auto-discovery** load path specifically — a relative `MARCH_LIB_PATH` module with a body type error that the entry never imports must fail `--check` with exit 1 (the existing `entry_imports_ill_typed` rules only exercise the explicit-`use` path; red/green verified against the pre-78afbbc resolver); and (2) defense-in-depth in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`: `emit_case` now `failwith`s ("type-incorrect TIR reached codegen") when an ADT constructor branch with binders meets a non-pointer scrutinee, naming the pattern and scrutinee variable, instead of silently emitting an integer switch whose branch bodies reference unbound binder symbols that only surface as clang link errors. Suite: full alcotest + fixture rules green; depot repro now reports the `lib/transaction.march` diagnostics and exits 1.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-12, fix(cas): early-exit hash now covers source-dir sibling modules)

- **Editing an imported sibling module now invalidates cached `--check`/`--compile` results.** The early source-hash CAS in `bin/main.ml compile` hashed the entry source, the stdlib hash, and all `.march` files under `MARCH_LIB_PATH` — but not the entry's own source-dir siblings, which `resolve_imports` auto-discovers as user code (`search_path = source_dir :: lib paths`). Editing a sibling to introduce a type error therefore hit the stale OK artifact: exit 0, no typechecking, on an ill-typed program (the cache-level twin of the diagnostic-filter regression fixed the same day). The hash walk now covers `Filename.dirname entry :: MARCH_LIB_PATH` dirs (same sorted `collect_lib_files` walk), skipping the realpath-equal entry file whose bytes are already the first buffer element. Adding/deleting a sibling also changes the key (file paths are hashed alongside contents); reverting an edit re-hits the original OK artifact. Cost: sibling reads on every cached run — strictly cheaper than the parse+typecheck the resolver would otherwise do on every run. Regression rule in `test/dune` (`cas_stale_dir`): checks a generated two-file sandbox (caching OK), injects an arity error into the sibling, and requires the second `--check` to exit 1. `dune build @runtest --force` exits 0.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-12, fix(resolver): imported-module type errors silently swallowed again)

- **Type errors in imported user modules abort the build again** (`march test` and `--check` exit 1). The a8ca488 fix made `resolve_imports` return `user_files` — every path loaded as user code — and `bin/main.ml`'s `is_user_file` does an **exact** `List.mem` of each diagnostic's span file against it. Commit 2545039 (the LSP/CLI resolver unification, one day later) consolidated `resolve_imports` into `lib/resolver/resolver.ml` and canonicalised the explicit-import dedup with `Unix.realpath`, so `user_files` held canonical **absolute** paths while spans still carry the **relative** path the file was parsed under (`pos_fname`) — membership never matched, imported-module errors were filtered as "stdlib-internal", and ill-typed programs compiled cleanly (exit 0, no diagnostics). The regression fixture `test/imports/entry_imports_ill_typed.march` caught this on day one — its two `with-accepted-exit-codes 1` dune rules failed from 2545039 onward — but the failures were misread as "pre-existing / unrelated" because the test-suite tail output looked green; only `dune runtest`'s exit code showed it. Fix: the resolver now tracks `user_files` separately from the canonical-keyed `loaded_paths` dedup table, recording **both** the parse-time path string (what spans carry) and the realpath at all three load sites (entry pre-mark, explicit `load`, auto-discovery phase 2; phase 1 threads the original path through to phase 2). `dune build @runtest --force` exits 0 with all rules re-executed. Known adjacent gap (separate task): the early-CAS `--check`/`--compile` source hash does not include source-dir sibling files, so editing an imported sibling can serve a stale cached OK.
## Current State (as of 2026-06-12, two native-codegen bugs blocking depot)

- **Fixed two pre-existing native-codegen bugs that the record-introspection builtins exposed** (see `specs/bug_todos.md` #40, #41). Both blocked depot's `test_depot_schema` from running natively (an apparent hang + wrong `blank()` defaults); the interpreter is the behavioral oracle.
  - **#40 Tag/untag mismatch on type-erased tuple/ADT slots** (adopted from the prior `20e53f2` work). Compiled code read tuple/erased-cell slots as `ptr` and untagged scalar views with an *unconditional* `ashr`, but `ETuple`/`EAlloc`/`EStackAlloc`/`EReuse` stored scalars in their NATURAL repr — so `let (a,b)=(10,20); a+b` returned `15` and `match [("a",5)] do Cons((k,v),_)->v` returned `2` in every compiled build. All tuple/erased slots now use the UNIFORM convention (scalars tagged via `coerce i64→ptr`), and `coerce ptr→i64` untags **conditionally** (`ashr` iff low bit set) so a heap pointer flowing through a lying scalar static type (depot's heterogeneous `opts` alist typed `List((String,Bool))` carrying a `String` default) survives verbatim instead of being halved. `march_extras.c` record builtins aligned to the uniform convention.
  - **#41 Generic `to_string` on type-erased strings** printed `#<tag:len>`. `march_string` now carries the 16-byte `march_hdr` prefix — `{rc; tag; pad; len; data}` with the reserved `MARCH_STRING_TAG` (`-1`) at the tag word — so `march_value_to_string` discriminates a string cell from an ADT/tuple/record cell and returns it verbatim. All string allocations route through a new `march_string_alloc` header-init helper.
