The JIT/REPL string reader (`jit_stubs.c`) + tuple pretty-printer (`repl_jit.ml`) follow the new offsets and uniform tuple tagging. Compiled code is unaffected (strings are built via `march_string_lit`); WASM keeps its own `march_string`.
  - **Along the way:** fixed a latent call-emission bug where a function registered with an unresolved `TVar` return type (definition emits `ret ptr`) was *called* as `call <scalar>` at sites that resolved it concretely — reinterpreting a tagged generic value as a raw scalar (e.g. `record_from_list(...).a` returning `3` instead of `1`). The EApp call paths now call as `ptr` and let the consumer coerce, mirroring the zero-arg path. This makes the `record_native` fixture fully green (18/18; it had a pre-existing `entries hetero int` failure on main that #40 also fixes).
  - **Verification:** full alcotest suite green (1428, 0 fail, 0 skip); `record_native` 18/18, `adt_eq_native` 6/6 compile-and-run fixtures green; depot `test_depot_schema` no longer hangs (42 tests; the remaining 3 failures are a separate generic-`to_string`-on-ADT-cells gap — `to_string(Some(0.0))` → `#<tag:1>`, needs ADT ctor-shape stamping — and a `record_entries` insertion-vs-sorted-order test expectation; neither is one of the two diagnosed bugs).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-11, fix(llvm): non-pointer tuple fields dropped on destructure)

- **Native tuple destructure no longer corrupts non-pointer (`Bool`/`Int`/`Float`) fields.** A `match (a, b, c) do (x, y, z) -> …` over a tuple — the shape `desugar_fn_def` emits for any module-nested fn with default-arg params (`fn greet_d(name, loud : Bool \\ false, suffix : String \\ "?")`), since `FPDefault` forces the general match-merge path — was silently miscompiled while the interpreter stayed correct. Root cause in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`'s `emit_case`: tuples are **built** storing each field with the atom's native LLVM type (a `Bool`/`Int` field is a raw `i64`, via `ETuple`/`EAlloc`), but the **destructure** typed every field via the synthetic `$TupleN` `ctor_info` entry whose fields are all `TVar "_"` → `ptr`, so the load untagged (`ashr 1`) a value that was never tagged. An explicitly-passed `loud : Bool = true` thus arrived as `false` (`1 >> 1 = 0`); concretely the repro printed `Def!` instead of `Def *!` natively. Fix: `resolve_ctor_fields` gained a `TTuple ts -> ts` case, and the `emit_case` field-binding loop now loads tuple fields with their **concrete element types** (matching how `ETuple`/`EField` already build and read them) instead of the polymorphic `ce_fields` fallback. Variant ctors are unchanged — their generic `TVar` fields ARE stored tagged on construction (`coerce i64→ptr` in `EAlloc`), so they keep using `ce_fields`. This also fixes the latent over-eager IncRC of scalar tuple fields (`concrete_fields` was likewise `TVar "_"` → every field treated as a heap pointer). Regression wired into `dune runtest`: `test/native/default_args_nested.march` is compiled natively, run, and its stdout diffed against `test/native/default_args_nested.expected`. Downstream: bastion's `test/test_form.march` "required marker appended to label" test (`Form.Input.render`'s `required = true` was dropped) now passes natively.
## Current State (as of 2026-06-11, fix(desugar): nested-module default-arg fns boxed params into a tuple → erased-closure use-after-free)

- **`expand_defaults_decl` only ran on top-level decls, so default-arg functions inside nested modules were never expanded.** A default param makes a clause non-trivial (`is_trivial_param` returns false for `FPDefault`), so `desugar_fn_def` routed such a function through the GENERAL path, which boxes every parameter into a synthesised tuple and immediately destructures it: `fn render(__arg0..__arg6) : (Conn,...,IOList) = let $t = (__arg0,...) in case $t of $Tuple7(...) -> body`. (Note the bogus return type — the param tuple.) When the function also takes a **type-erased closure parameter** (`render_inner`, untyped → erased to `IOList`), this tuple adapter mismanaged the closure's refcount: the optimizer/erasure interplay freed `render_inner` before its `call_ptr render_inner()` (at `-O0` the crash is a jump to `0x2`; at `-O2` the corruption cascades to `find$apply` reading a freed assigns list → `RC underflow in march_decrc_freed`). This is the deeper root cause of the same bastion `Form.Wrapper.render`/CSRF crash the prior defun fix (838a37c) papered over at the call site. Fix: `desugar_fn_def` now has a fast-path case for a single clause whose only non-trivial params are `FPDefault` — it strips the defaults to plain `FPNamed` and keeps the real signature, so NO param tuple is synthesised (top-level default-arg fns already avoid this because `expand_defaults_decl` expands them into `$N` arities with plain named params; this brings nested ones in line). Verified end-to-end: bastion's whole `Form.Wrapper.render` test group now passes (was aborting). Regression: `test/test_march.ml` "nested default-arg fn keeps signature (no param tuple)". 1422 → 1423 tests.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-11, fix(defun): erased-type closure call left as direct EApp → Perceus use-after-free)

- **Defun now converts `EApp` of any locally-bound callee to `ECallPtr`, not just `TFn`/`TVar`-typed ones.** When a closure value is threaded through a position that erases its function type to a concrete type — most commonly a tuple field, where the checker types the whole tuple uniformly (e.g. `(String, String, String)` for `(a, b, closure)`) — defun's old guard `(TFn | TVar) ∧ ¬top_level` did not fire, so the call `g()` stayed a direct `EApp(g, [])`. Perceus's `EApp` liveness deliberately ignores the callee (assuming it is always a top-level code symbol after defun), so the closure variable was considered dead at the call and received an early `EDecRC` — freeing the closure before the indirect call that codegen still emitted, a use-after-free. This is exactly the documented "tuple-switch dispatcher MISCOMPILES closure args" failure for **module-nested default-arg functions** (the default param makes the clause non-trivial → params are packed into a tuple and destructured → the closure argument is reached through a tuple field): bastion's `Form.Wrapper.render` (a default-arg fn taking a trailing `render_inner` closure) crashed (`march_decrc_freed` / SIGBUS) whenever the test suite ran it. Fix: `Defun.rewrite_expr` threads a set of locally-bound names (params, `let`, case-arm vars, recursive-fn names) and converts `EApp(f, args) → ECallPtr` whenever `f` is locally bound (a closure) regardless of its erased type; operators/builtins are never let/param-bound so they are unaffected. With the closure call now an `ECallPtr`, Perceus counts the callee live and the spurious early drop disappears. Regression: `test/test_march.ml` "defun erased closure callptr" (a closure built into a tuple, destructured, then called). 1421 → 1422 tests.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-11, native record introspection builtins)

- **The 7 record introspection builtins (`record_keys`, `record_values`, `record_entries`, `record_get`, `record_put`, `record_has_key`, `record_from_list`) now work in compiled binaries.** Previously interpreter-only — native builds emitted bare `call ptr @record_entries(...)` and failed at link time. Design: a runtime **record shape registry** (`runtime/march_extras.c`) interns canonical descriptors (`"name:kind;..."`, fields sorted by name, kind chars i/f/p/g for raw-i64/double-bits/heap-ptr/generic); every record allocation stamps the interned shape id into the header pad word (offset 12, previously always 0). The builtins are C functions reading the descriptor; `record_put` with a new key and `record_from_list` intern NEW shapes at runtime, so dynamically-built records (depot's `Depot.Schema.blank` fold over `record_from_list([])`) work natively. `EField` on statically-unknown record types compiles to a dynamic by-name lookup. Interpreter parity: eval's `record_keys/values/entries` now return fields sorted by name (matching the native sorted-by-name slot layout). Also fixed along the way: scope-aware `rename_tir_vars` in `lower.ml` (a local binder sharing a module fn name no longer emits an undefined zero-arg global call) and structural `==` for tuples/records via `ensure_adt_eq_fn` (was pointer identity, breaking `List.member` over `record_entries` results natively). Fixture: `test/imports/record_native/` (18 compile-and-run checks). Suite: 1422 alcotest tests + doctests + fixtures green (2 pre-existing `entry_imports_ill_typed` rule failures unrelated). WASM targets skip shape metadata.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-11, fix(llvm): nested immediate-literal patterns miscompiled)

- **Bool/Int/Atom literal patterns nested inside constructor patterns now compile correctly in the native path.** `match r do Ok(true) -> "T" Ok(false) -> "F" Err(_) -> "E" end` printed T T E (first bool arm always taken); nested Int/Atom literal matches segfaulted. `emit_case` in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` compiled the inner literal test as a heap ctor-tag switch on the field — but ADT fields holding immediates are low-bit-tagged scalars (`(n<<1)|1`), not heap pointers, so the tag load was a wild dereference that LLVM folded away (the switch's default was `unreachable` and only one case label survived). Resolves the "found-but-deferred" miscompile noted in the __try_call entry below. Fix: literal tags are syntactically unambiguous (lowercase `true`/`false`, integers, `:atoms` vs capitalized ADT ctor names), so all-immediate-literal-tag ECases are now routed off the heap path — bools via the existing untag+`trunc`+`br i1` path, ints/atoms via a switch on the raw `ptrtoint` bits against `(lit<<1)|1` labels (tagged-vs-tagged comparison is exact; untagging would lose bit 63 of atom hashes). User ADTs literally named `True | False(Int)` still take the ctor-tag switch. Also fixes the latent miscompile in `stdlib/check.march` `format_error`. `binary_trees` benchmark (the ECase-mapped check) unchanged at ~290 ms. +3 IR-level regression tests (1423 → 1427 after merge).
## Current State (as of 2026-06-11, fix(typecheck): hmac_sha256 signature matched no implementation)

- **`hmac_sha256` is now typed `String -> String -> Result(Bytes, String)`, matching all implementations.** The typechecker entry declared `Bytes -> Bytes -> Bytes` while the eval builtin returns `VCon("Ok",[Bytes])`, the native runtime (`march_hmac_sha256`) reads `march_string` args and returns `Result(Bytes, String)`, and llvm_emit's `builtin_ret_ty` already said `Result(Bytes, String)`. The typechecker therefore rejected correct usage (matching `Ok(b)`/`Err(e)` with String args) and accepted only usage that would misbehave at runtime. The fix aligns the typecheck entry with the other three sites (and with the existing `stdlib_hmac_sha256` entry, which was already correct). `hmac_sha256_bytes` (`Bytes -> Bytes -> Bytes`, bare result) was already consistent everywhere and is unchanged. Added typecheck-level regression tests for both builtins — prior coverage called the eval builtin directly and never exercised the typechecker signature. 1424 → 1426 tests.
## Current State (as of 2026-06-11, fix(tir): locals shadow sibling module fns in native codegen)

- **Locals that share a name with a sibling module-level fn no longer miscompile in native builds.** `rename_tir_vars` (lib/tir/lower.ml) qualifies unqualified intra-module references (`from_string` → `IOList.from_string`) after lowering, but renamed EVERY `AVar` whose name matched a sibling fn — including function parameters, match-pattern bindings, and let bindings. A param like `fields` in `pfn blank_fold(fields, acc)` next to `fn fields(schema)` was rewritten to `Depot.Schema.fields`, which llvm_emit then materialized as the fn's global address (GEP'd as data → SIGBUS) or a zero-arg `"gl"` call (`call ptr @Depot.Schema.fields()` → "use of undefined value" at clang time). Fix: the rename walk now threads an in-scope binder set (fn params, ELet/ELetRec bindings, ECase branch vars) and skips shadowed names — the TIR/emit-level analog of typecheck commit 02d212d "local fns shadow bulk imports". Fixture: `test/imports/param_shadow_native/` (native compile + run; covers param, pattern-binding, and let-binding shadowing). Driven by depot's test suite (`Depot.Schema.blank_fold`).

## Current State (as of 2026-06-11, whole-program native codegen fixes)

- **Cross-module ADT `==` now compiles and runs correctly in native whole-program builds.** `ensure_adt_eq_fn` resolves short TCon names against module-qualified ctor_info keys (with layout-compatible merging for same-short-name types like Ast.SortDir vs DataFrame.SortDir) and no longer memoizes before validating — previously every `==` on an imported ADT emitted calls to `@__march_eq_*` functions whose bodies were never generated. `lower.ml` no longer embeds qualified ctor references verbatim in EAlloc keys (silently tagging values 0). Interface impls now pre-register unit-wide in pass 1, and the exhaustiveness checker recognizes qualified constructor patterns. Fixture: `test/imports/adt_eq_native/`. Driven by depot's 5370-test suite.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-11, fix(runtime): __try_call stored untagged Bool — compiled Check.all properties always "failed")

- **Compiled `Check.all` properties no longer report "returned false" for trivially-true properties.** `__try_call` in `runtime/march_runtime.c` (the panic-catching trampoline behind `Check.try_prop`) stored the property thunk's raw i64 Bool directly into the `Result` Ok field — but compiled March reads polymorphic ADT immediate fields with the uniform low-bit tag convention (`ptrtoint` + `ashr 1`), so `Ok(1)` read back as `0` and `Check.is_failing` saw every passing property as failing on run 1. The interpreter has no tagging and passed the same properties, which is the discriminating symptom. Fix: `__try_call` now stores `((result << 1) | 1)`, and its typechecker signature is tightened from `(Bool -> a) -> Result(a, String)` to `(Bool -> Bool) -> Result(Bool, String)` so a future heap-typed use fails to typecheck instead of being silently corrupted by the immediate-tagging path. End-to-end regression test compiles a trivially-true property through the real `--compile --test` pipeline and asserts exit 0 (skips gracefully without clang, like the JIT tests). Found-but-deferred during verification: (1) Bool literal patterns nested in constructor patterns (`Ok(true)`/`Ok(false)`) compile to a heap-tag switch on a tagged immediate — LLVM's unreachable-default folding makes the FIRST Bool arm always taken (`T T E` instead of `T F E`), a real miscompile tracked as a separate task; (2) generic `to_string` on lists prints `#<tag:N>` in compiled failure reports. 1422 → 1423 tests.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-11, fix(lower): DMod test bodies dropped helpers via unqualified refs)

- **Test bodies inside a `DMod` now qualify sibling-fn references, fixing "use of undefined value '@helper'" at the clang stage of `forge test`.** Multi-test-file projects aggregate non-entry test files as `DMod` entries (via MARCH_LIB_PATH auto-discovery); `lower_mod_decls` registers their functions qualified (`TestB.helper`) and applies `rename_tir_vars` to the module's own `DFn` bodies — but `collect_tests` lowered `test`/`setup`/`setup_all` bodies with plain `lower_expr`, leaving references unqualified (`helper`). A helper referenced ONLY through a closure (e.g. a `Check.all(gen, fn xs -> helper(xs))` property) then failed down the pipeline: defun didn't recognize `helper` as a top-level fn and captured it as a free variable; DCE pruned `TestB.helper` (reachability intersects `free_vars` with fn names — name mismatch); and llvm_emit's no-slot AVar fallback emitted a `@helper$clo_wrap` trampoline calling a dangling `@helper`. Direct (non-closure) calls survived only because inlining erased them before DCE. Fix in `lib/tir/lower.ml`: `collect_tests` now threads the enclosing module prefix + direct fn/let names through `DMod` descent and applies `rename_tir_vars` to lowered test/setup bodies, mirroring the module's own `DFn` handling. Regression test compiles a DMod test referencing a sibling helper through a closure down the full native pipeline (mono→defun→perceus→opt→llvm) and asserts no dangling unqualified trampoline. Note: a separate pre-existing bug remained where compiled `Check.all` properties failed at runtime (`returned false`) — fixed in the __try_call entry above. (Merge note: an equivalent collect_tests qualification — `qualify_locals`/`direct_names_of` — landed independently on main via the whole-program codegen fixes; the merge kept main's implementation, and this branch's full-pipeline regression test covers it.) 1421 → 1422 tests.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-10, fix(llvm): string ordering comparison miscompiled)

- **String `<`/`<=`/`>`/`>=` now lower to `march_compare_string` in the native path.** The LLVM backend's comparison emission (`is_int_cmp` in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`) only special-cased string `==`/`!=` (via `march_string_eq`); ordering operators fell through to the integer/pointer `icmp` fallback, comparing the string STRUCT POINTERS as integers — meaningless for lexicographic order. Every compiled program that ordered strings through a comparator was silently miscompiled while the interpreter stayed correct; concretely `List.sort_by` on strings became an effective no-op / garbage order. Fix: added an `is_string_ord` branch that calls `march_compare_string(a, b)` (returns −1/0/1) and compares the result against 0 with the same predicate. This fires when the operands carry a concrete `TString` type after monomorphization. Verified that monomorphization fully specializes comparator closures to the concrete element type even when threaded through generic functions and data structures (e.g. a `(a -> a -> Bool, a, a)` tuple passed to a generic `pick` that never mentions `String` still yields `String`-typed apply params) — so the polymorphic-`'_` case does not reach codegen for well-formed programs; `'_`-typed apply functions survive only as dead/uninstantiated code. 1418 → 1421 tests.

## Current State (as of 2026-06-06, fix(perceus): locally-owned record field RC underflow)

- **Perceus: locally-owned record field extraction no longer emits spurious `EDecRC`.** Before the fix, `is_borrowed_field` only fired when the source record was a borrowed function parameter (in `live_after`) or itself a borrowed field (`_borrowed_field_vars`). For locally-owned records returned by cross-module function calls, the source was only in `live_into_e2`—not `live_after`—so String fields were incorrectly treated as independently owned. `post_dec` then emitted `EDecRC(field)` at the field's last borrowed-call use, freeing `meta.title` while the record still held a reference → dangling pointer + potential RC underflow. Fix: `is_borrowed_field` now also checks `live_before(e2, live_after)` (sequential multi-field access in body) and `_var_ctx ∧ ¬TPtr` (locally-bound heap record still in scope). The `¬TPtr` exclusion prevents the closure struct `$clo: TPtr TUnit` from accidentally suppressing the IncRC emitted for closure FVs before consuming calls. 1413 → 1414 tests.

## Current State (as of 2026-04-23, fix dune runtest infinite hang)

- **`dune runtest` now completes in ~90s (was: infinite hang).** Two root causes fixed: (1) `caml_march_deflate_decode` / `caml_march_gzip_decode` in `lib/eval/compress_stubs.c` spun forever on `Z_BUF_ERROR` with exhausted input — `inflate()` returning `Z_BUF_ERROR` with `avail_in == 0 && avail_out > 0` is zlib's way of saying "more input needed but none remains"; now detected and raised as `caml_failwith`. The "Compress.Deflate.decode invalid → Err" test (test 24 in the compress group) was the culprit. (2) `test_heap.c` section 5c `expect_abort()` called `fork()` + `march_gc_collect()` → `abort()`. On macOS, `abort()` after `fork()` routes through the Mach exception mechanism → ReportCrash stalls the child for 10+ minutes per crash. Fix: `march_gc_crash()` weak hook added to `runtime/march_gc.c` (all `abort()` calls in `gc_corrupt` / `pass1_visit` / `pass2_visit` now go through it); the test binary provides a strong override that calls `_exit(134)` instead, bypassing ReportCrash.

## Current State (as of 2026-04-23, JIT Phase 3.2 — actors work in the REPL)

- **JIT/REPL: `spawn`/`send`/`run_until_idle` now go through the interpreter whenever an actor has been declared in the session.** Per Phase 3.2 of `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md`. Actors parse/typecheck fine, and the JIT happily compiles `send` / `run_until_idle` into calls on the *runtime* actor scheduler (`march_send`, `march_run_until_idle`). But the REPL registers actor definitions only in the interpreter's scheduler — so JIT-compiled calls hit a scheduler with no actors registered: messages enqueued but never dispatched, `println` inside `on Msg ...` handlers silently dropped, `run_until_idle()` returning `1` instead of `()`. Fix: new `actors_declared` ref in both `run_simple` and `run_tui`; flipped true the moment any `DActor` is processed; guards the three JIT paths (fn decl, let decl, ReplExpr) so the interpreter owns the scheduler whenever actors are in scope. End-to-end check: `actor Counter do state {value: Int} ... on Probe() do println(int_to_string(state.value)); state end end; let pid = spawn(Counter); send(pid, Inc(5)); send(pid, Probe()); run_until_idle()` — REPL now prints `5` and returns `()`, matching file mode. Basic JIT path still works for non-actor sessions (arith, lets, fns all go through ORC/clang). All REPL Phase 3 items (3.1 impls, 3.2 actors, 3.3 records, 3.4 multi-file imports) complete; 3.5 async is explicitly out of scope.

## Current State (as of 2026-04-23, JIT Phase 3.4 — multi-file user imports in REPL)

- **JIT/REPL: `use UserModule` now resolves against `MARCH_LIB_PATH` + CWD.** Per Phase 3.4 of `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md`. The REPL already ran `Resolver.resolve_imports` when it saw a `DUse`, typechecked the returned `DMod`, and evaled it into the interpreter env — but `Module_registry` (which `qualified_error_msg` consults for `Mod.member` accesses) was NEVER updated for user modules, so `MyMod.greet("world")` always tripped `Unknown module MyMod`. Fix: after `resolve_imports` returns, each `DMod` is run through `Module_registry.extract_exports` (newly exposed in `lib/modules/module_registry.mli`) and registered via `Module_registry.register`. Applied to both REPL paths — `run_simple` (stdin-piped / scroll-mode) and `run_tui` (interactive terminal). Verified end-to-end: `use MyMod` in a fresh REPL with `MARCH_LIB_PATH=/tmp/march_mod` set now resolves `MyMod.greet("world")` → `"hello, world"`. Smoke test still 54/54 PASS; full dune suite: 147/147 PASS in 29.7s. Also validated that 3.1 (user impls for `Show` / stdlib interfaces) and 3.3 (records) already work without additional changes.

## Current State (as of 2026-04-22, JIT Phase 2.3 — ORC LLJIT backend)

- **JIT/REPL: in-process LLJIT backend lands behind `MARCH_JIT_BACKEND=orc`.** Per Phase 2.3 of `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md`. New C stubs in `lib/jit/jit_orc_stubs.c` (200 lines) drive LLVM's ORCv2 C API directly — `LLVMOrcCreateLLJIT`, `LLVMOrcLLJITAddLLVMIRModule`, `LLVMOrcLLJITLookup`, process-wide `DynamicLibrarySearchGenerator` so stdlib/runtime symbols already dlopen'd at startup are resolvable by JIT'd code. libLLVM.dylib is `dlopen`'d lazily on first `Jit_orc.create` with fallback paths (`/opt/homebrew/opt/llvm`, `/opt/homebrew`, `/usr/local/opt/llvm`) or `MARCH_LLVM_LIB`; linker flag `-Wl,-undefined,dynamic_lookup` in `lib/jit/dune` lets us reference LLVM symbols without a link-time dependency (avoids adding 157 MB to every march startup). `Repl_jit.compile_fragment` became a backend dispatcher returning a `fragment_handle` sum; `lookup_sym` abstracts `Jit.dlsym` vs `LLVMOrcLLJITLookup`. Clang path still compiles the stdlib prelude and is the default. **Measured impact — 30-fragment REPL session:** 4.52 s (clang) → 0.19 s (ORC) = **~24× end-to-end speedup**; per-fragment latency drops from ~145 ms (48 ms clang + 97 ms dlopen) to ~0.2 ms after a one-shot 11 ms LLJIT warmup. Smoke test: 54/54 PASS on both backends. First Phase-2 change with measurable speedup — profile showed 2.1 and 2.2 were nibbling at the wrong layer (clang CLI + dyld) while the real win was eliminating both via in-process JIT.

## Current State (as of 2026-04-22, JIT Phase 2.2 — pipe IR via stdin)

- **JIT/REPL: fragment compilation now pipes IR to clang via stdin.** Per Phase 2.2 of `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md`. `compile_fragment` in `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml` replaced the `.ll` write + `clang path.ll` invocation with `Unix.open_process` feeding `clang -x ir ... -` on stdin. Common path now skips the file write entirely; `MARCH_KEEP_LL=1` mirrors IR to `repl_N.ll` before compilation for debugging. Compile failures dump IR to `repl_N.ll` so the user can reproduce with clang directly. Measured impact on 30-fragment session: 4.58 s baseline → 4.58 s (within noise). Plan expected 5–15 ms/fragment; actual gain is not measurable — macOS APFS with a hot FS cache makes a 15 KB file write essentially free, and clang's internal IR-parsing cost is the same regardless of source. The change still pays off as simplification (fewer filesystem side effects, no stale `.ll` on interrupted compiles), consistent with the "fragments are ephemeral" intent of Phase 2.1. Smoke test: 54/54 PASS. MARCH_KEEP_LL path verified.

## Current State (as of 2026-04-22, JIT Phase 2.1 — fragment clang -O0)

- **JIT/REPL: fragments now compile with `clang -O0 -fno-lto` instead of `-O1`.** Per Phase 2.1 of `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md`. Stdlib still compiles once at -O1 (cached), only per-fragment `.so` builds drop to -O0. Measured impact: **no observable speedup** on current fragment sizes (~320 lines / 15 KB IR per fragment). clang wall time is ~40 ms at -O0 and ~40 ms at -O1 — optimization passes at this IR size are negligible compared to clang startup (~25 ms). 30-fragment REPL session: 4.56 s baseline → 4.55 s with -O0 (within noise). The change is still worth keeping as a defensive 1-line simplification: fragments are one-shot code we dlopen once and discard, so paying for optimization passes at all is wrong in principle; -fno-lto guards against future toolchain default changes. Real fragment-latency budget lives on the OCaml side (parse/typecheck/TIR lower/LLVM emit) and in dlopen, not in clang optimization. Smoke test: 54/54 PASS.

## Current State (as of 2026-04-22, JIT Phase 1 correctness — tuple pp, user ADT/record printing, retty recovery)

- **JIT/REPL pretty-printer now correct for tuples, user-defined ADTs, and records.** Six Phase-1 fixes in `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml` per `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md`. (1.1) Tuple fields stored untagged — `pp_field` previously shifted every scalar slot right by one bit assuming an ADT tag, so `(42, 99)` rendered as `(21, 49)` and `(true, false)` as `(false, false)`. Added `~tagged:bool` parameter; tuples call with `tagged:false`, ADT/List/Option/Result continue with `tagged:true`. (1.2) User-declared variants now render with their real names. New `global_type_defs : (string, Tir.type_def) Hashtbl.t` on the JIT context is populated from every `tir.tm_types` in `run_expr`/`run_decl`, and `register_user_type_decl` is called from `repl.ml` when a DType declaration arrives. `pp_heap_value` consults it: TCon name → TDVariant → constructor by heap tag → substitute free TVars positionally against TCon args → recurse. `Red` prints as `Red`; `Circle(5)` as `Circle(5)`. (1.3) `find_retvar` (renamed to `find_retty`) recurses through `ECase` branches and prefers the EAtom `v_ty` (concrete post-mono) over a global-table lookup — polymorphic return paths through match/if no longer drop to `#<0xADDR>`. (1.4) Added TRecord case to `pp_heap_value` and fixed TDRecord to treat scalar fields as UNTAGGED (same heap layout as tuples, not payload-tagged); `{x = 1, y = 2}` now prints `{x: 1, y: 2}`. (1.5) `create` now wipes `repl_*` leftovers from `march_jit/` on startup so SIGKILL'd sessions don't leak. `MARCH_KEEP_LL` opts out. (1.6) Stale KNOWN ISSUE comment in `test/repl_smoke_test.sh` replaced; new acceptance tests added (4 tuple, 3 user ADT, 2 record). REPL smoke test: 53 PASS / 0 FAIL / 1 XFAIL (pre-existing cross-fragment HOF issue).

## Current State (as of 2026-04-22, JIT numeric defaulting — cross-fragment HOF correctness)

- **JIT/REPL: cross-fragment higher-order functions now produce correct results.** `let add = fn (a, x) -> a + x` followed by `List.fold_left([1,2,3,4,5], 0, add)` previously returned `15` only inside the same JIT fragment; in a multi-expression REPL session the result was wrong (shifted arithmetic). Root cause: `discharge_constraints` in `typecheck.ml` silently ignored `CNum` constraints on unresolved `TVar` cells — a standalone `fn (a, x) -> a + x` (no call site to force `a`/`x` to `Int`) kept its parameters as `TVar`, which propagated to the TIR as `TVar("_273")`, which lowered to `"ptr"` params in LLVM IR, which caused the slot mechanism to apply tagged-int untag/retag on every cross-fragment call, corrupting arithmetic. Fix: numeric defaulting in `discharge_constraints` — any unresolved `TVar` under a `CNum` constraint is linked to `TCon("Int", [])`. All other unresolved constraints (COrd, etc.) are left polymorphic as before. REPL smoke test: both previously-xfailing cross-fragment HOF tests now pass. Smoke test updated: 45 PASS, 0 FAIL, 0 XFAIL. 147 dune tests still pass.

## Current State (as of 2026-04-18, interpreter backtraces + assert location)

- **Good backtraces — interpreter call stack.** Panics, match failures, and assert failures now print a March-level stack trace in the interpreter. `EApp` evaluation pushes a frame (function name + file:line from the call-site span) before `apply` and pops on normal return; exceptions leave frames live so the top-level handler reads the full call stack. Stdlib frames (paths containing `/stdlib/`) are hidden by default; `MARCH_BACKTRACE=full` shows all frames. `EAssert` failure messages include the source file:line of the failing assert. `Assert_failure` is now caught in `bin/main.ml` alongside `Eval_error` and `Match_failure`. C runtime gains `march_frame_t` / `march_frame_push` / `march_frame_pop` / `march_frame_reset` for compiled-binary backtraces (LLVM codegen instrumentation is a follow-on step). Plan updated: threading clarification, `String.starts_with` fix, `#[no_backtrace]` scoped to future, testing strategy section. Spec: `specs/plans/2026-04-18-backtraces-plan.md`.

## Current State (as of 2026-04-18, adversarial security/safety audit + 7 regression tests)

## Current State (as of 2026-04-18, serialization stdlib — Msgpack, Toml, Xml, Yaml, Socket, Dns)

- **Adversarial compiler audit: 8 bugs found and fixed across eval, runtime C, and LLVM emit.** A systematic adversarial review found safety and correctness issues across the stack: (1) **Float division by zero** — `eval.ml` silently returned `+inf` for `x /. 0.0` (IEEE 754 `fdiv` never traps); added `b <> 0.0` guards + `eval_error` in both `/` and `/.` float branches. Runtime: new `march_checked_fdiv` in `march_runtime.c` wraps the C division with `b == 0.0` check + `exit(1)`. LLVM: `llvm_emit.ml` now emits `call double @march_checked_fdiv(...)` instead of bare `fdiv` for both `/` and `/.`. (2) **Array bounds** — `march_typed_array_get/set` had no bounds check; added `typed_array_check_bounds` static helper used by both. `set` was also bypassing `typed_array_alloc`'s overflow guard. (3) **String overflow** — `march_string_concat` and `march_string_repeat` used unchecked `sa->len + sb->len` / `ss->len * n` arithmetic; added `__builtin_add_overflow` / `__builtin_mul_overflow` guards. (4) **Malloc NULL** — `typed_array_alloc` did not check `malloc` return; added NULL check. (5) **Unsafe closure dispatch** — `march_typed_array_filter/map/fold` inlined fragile offset arithmetic (`*(void**)(base+8)`) to call callbacks; extracted to `call_closure_1` / `call_closure_2` inline helpers with documented layout assumption. (6) **Perceus scrutinee-in-body** — the `scrutinee_borrowed` conservative analysis in `perceus.ml` is intentional (uses any-path `name_free_in` rather than all-path `name_free_on_every_path` to prevent RC underflow on the sort_by pattern); added extensive documentation comment explaining the choice, the 9930ce5 history, and what a proper fix would require. (7) **TRecord sort invariant** — added `assert_trecord_sorted` debug assertion (gated on `MARCH_DEBUG_TC` env var) wired into TRecord unification; also extended the TRecord docstring to explain the `(sorted)` invariant. 7 new regression tests in `adversarial-regressions` group. 1363 → 1370 tests.

- **Perceus EApp callee spurious EIncRC (`@__` link error).** Commit `831e315` changed `needs_rc(TFn _)` to `true`, causing Perceus to insert `EIncRC` for the callee in every `EApp`. After defun all `EApp` callees are top-level function symbols (code addresses), not heap closures. Operators `&&`/`||` have `llvm_name` → `@__` (undefined), so the spurious `EIncRC` generated a call to `@__()` that failed to link. Fix in `lib/tir/perceus.ml`: removed the callee `f.v_name` from (1) liveness propagation in `live_before`, (2) `find_inc_vars` in `insert_rc_expr`, and (3) live-before propagation in the EApp arm. `ECallPtr` (indirect closure dispatch) is unchanged. 147 tests pass.

- **Let-generalization hole for forward-referenced polymorphic `pfn` helpers.** When a function `count(lst)` calls a forward-referenced helper `go(lst, n)` defined later in source order, pass-1's placeholder `Mono (TVar r)` for `go` was unified into the type chain during `count`'s body inference. `generalize` wrapped those shared mutable TVar refs directly in the `Poly` scheme. When `go`'s body was subsequently checked, its recursive call unified the same TVar (e.g., `?lst_elem → List(Int)`). This corrupted the already-stored scheme for `count`, so a second call site with a different element type failed with a spurious type mismatch. Fix in `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` `generalize`: after collecting quantified ids, allocate fresh isolated `TVar ref` cells (at level 0, so they're never generalized again) and deep-copy the type structure using these new refs. The scheme now exclusively owns its quantified vars; future unification of the original refs cannot reach the scheme. Regression tests added: `typecheck/forward-ref pfn poly two call sites` and `typecheck/normal-order pfn poly two call sites`. `perceus/no EIncRC for && / || callee (831e315)` and `llvm_emit/no @__ call for && / || (831e315)` added for the Perceus bug. 1358 → 1363 tests.

- **Perceus RC crash fix: `TFn` ownership tracking + cross-branch EDecRC.** Fixed a `march_decrc` crash where a `.text-segment address` was passed to the RC system during `Map.fold` with a `Bytes` accumulator (crash stack: `march_decrc → Bytes.list_append → Bytes.concat → Encode.encode`). Two interlocking bugs in `lib/tir/perceus.ml`: (1) `needs_rc (TFn _) = false` — Perceus silently skipped all RC operations for function-typed parameters, so `f` in `Map.node_fold` was invisible to liveness tracking: no `EIncRC` when `f` was captured into the `go` closure, no `EDecRC` in the HEmpty arm where `f` was unused. The dangling slot in the closure was later interpreted as a Bytes payload pointer and freed via `march_decrc`, crashing. Fix: `needs_rc (TFn _) = true` — any `AVar` with `TFn` type is a heap-allocated closure struct after defun (raw code pointers are `ADefRef` and never appear in AVar liveness); `llvm_ty (TFn _) = "ptr"` so RC ops emit correctly at the LLVM level without any additional changes. (2) ECase dropped `live_before_br` for every branch — variables live in some branches but dead in others never got `EDecRC` in the dead branches, a second class of silent RC leak. Fix: collect `live_before_br` per branch, compute `union_live_br`, and insert `EDecRC v` in branches where `v ∈ union_live_br` but `v ∉ live_before_br` (excluding closure FVs, arm-bound vars, and post-case live vars). New `_var_ctx` global (`Tir.var StringMap.t ref`) provides correctly-typed `Tir.var` records for cross-branch EDecRC emission; seeded from fn params, extended via ELet with save/restore. All 1358 tests pass.

- **`List.range` (and any local recursive fn with non-ptr params) now generates correct LLVM.** Self-calls inside inner `fn go(...)` functions were typed as `TVar("_")` because `ty_of_span` returned `unknown_ty` for stdlib spans not in the type_map. The `ECallPtr` emitter fell through to the all-ptr fallback, generating `ptr (ptr, ptr, ptr)` instead of `ptr (ptr, i64, ptr)` for Int params. `List.range(0, 5)` produced 63 wrong elements instead of `[0,1,2,3,4]`. Fix: register `fn_name → TFn(param_tys, ret_ty)` in `_fn_param_types` before lowering both the body and block continuation of any `ELetFn`. Also fixed `List.fold_left`/`fold_right` type annotations: `(b, a) -> b` was parsed as a 1-arg tuple function; changed to curried form `b -> a -> b`. All 1359 tests pass.

- **Map.get returns correct value for 2-element maps (compiled path).** Four interlocking compiler bugs caused `Map.get` to return `None` on 2-element HAMT maps in compiled binaries (interpreter path was unaffected). (1) `typecheck.ml`: `ELetFn` param types were not recorded in `type_map`, so `lower.ml` saw `TVar "_"` for every inner-function param (collapsing `Map.from_list`'s inner `go` params to one unknown type). (2) `lower.ml`: a stdlib sub-module was not marked as lowered before processing its declarations, causing `_ensure_module_lowered` to re-parse it from disk without the `type_map` and overwrite correctly-typed functions via last-write-wins. (3) `mono.ml`: interface dispatch (e.g. `hash → march_hash_string`) could not match primitive TIR types (`TString`/`TInt`/`TFloat`/`TBool`) because only `TCon` was handled in the type-name lookup — left a bare `@hash` extern that the linker could not resolve. Also added an `ELetRec` local-subst pass so generalized inner functions specialize from concrete call-site types. (4) `llvm_emit.ml`: the `$fv` fast-path matched only `ELet(v, EField(clo, $fvN), body)`, but Perceus wraps live-closure field reads as `ESeq(EIncRC(clo), EField(...))`. The regular `ELet` fallback then used `EField`'s hardcoded `"ptr"` return type, and `coerce("ptr" → "i64")` via `ptrtoint + ashr 1` halved the raw `i64` hash stored in the closure — causing slot mismatches between insert and get in 2-element maps. Fix: peel `ESeq` wrappers in the `$fv` pattern and load the field with `v.v_ty`. All 1359 worktree tests + 147 main tests pass.

- **BigInt.eq / Decimal.eq stack overflow fix.** Two pre-existing bugs in `stdlib/bigint.march` and `stdlib/decimal.march` caused infinite recursion (stack overflow) when `==`, `!=`, `<`, `>`, `<=`, `>=` were applied to BigInt or Decimal values. Root cause: `impl Eq(BigInt)` and `impl Ord(BigInt)` processing in the evaluator binds method names `eq`/`compare` into the shared `inner_ref` for the module, shadowing the module-level functions of the same name. Any closure that resolved `eq`/`compare` via `!inner_ref` at call time found the impl version, causing cycles (`BigInt.eq → impl_eq → BigInt.eq → …`). Fix: introduced private `pfn bigint_cmp_core(a, b)` in `bigint.march` and `pfn cmp(a, b)` in `decimal.march` — names not shadowed by any impl block. All public-facing functions (`eq`, `compare`, `lt`, `gt`, `lte`, `gte`) and both `impl Eq` / `impl Ord` blocks delegate to these private helpers.  Second bug in `compare_digits`: `-1` literals were parsed as `EApp(EVar "negate", [EInt 1])` by the unary-minus parser rule, which inside the BigInt module resolved `negate` to `BigInt.negate` (expecting a BigInt, not an Int) and panicked with "no branch matched the value 1". Fixed by replacing all `-1` literals with `0 - 1` in the BigInt module. **Effect**: `BigInt.eq(BigInt.from_int(0), BigInt.from_int(0))`, `BigInt.compare`, and all `==`/`<`/`>` operators on BigInt and Decimal values now work correctly. `test/stdlib/test_bigint.march` 129 tests pass; `test/stdlib/test_decimal.march` 115 tests pass.
- **Stdlib property test expansion: 37 → 134 tests.** `test/stdlib/test_properties.march` grew from 37 to 134 property tests after the BigInt/Decimal fixes unlocked direct `==` and ordering operator use. New properties: `BigInt.eq matches Int equality`, `BigInt.compare matches Int compare`, and all negative-value tests for `BigInt.add` and `BigInt.mul` that previously required `to_string` workarounds. All 134 tests pass with no workarounds.

- **Property-based testing stdlib (Hedgehog-style).** New `Gen` and `Check` stdlib modules provide property-based testing with integrated shrinking, producing genuinely minimal counterexamples without user-written shrink functions.
  - **`Gen` (generators with rose-tree shrinking)**: `type GenTree(a) = GenTree(a, Thunk(List(GenTree(a))))` is a lazy rose tree — each generated value carries its own shrink candidates embedded in the tree structure. Primitives: `int(lo, hi)` (shrinks toward 0 or range endpoint), `int_sized`, `bool` (shrinks True→False), `float`, `constant`, `element`, `ascii_char`, `lowercase_char`. Combinators: `map`, `filter` (rejection sampling with pruned shrinks), `bind` (uses Hedgehog's RNG-replay trick so dependent-generator shrinking is coherent), `one_of`, `frequency`. Collections: `list`, `list_of_size`, `option`, `tuple2`, `tuple3`, `string`, `lowercase_string`. List shrinking drops power-of-two-length prefixes then shrinks individual elements — finds minimal failing lists fast.
  - **`Check` (property runner)**: `Check.all(gen, prop)` runs the property 100 times (size grows 0 → 100 across runs), and on failure walks the shrink tree DFS-greedy to find the minimal counterexample. Panics on failure with `counterexample: <value>; shrunk N step(s) from <original>; reproduce with seed: N`. Properties may `return Bool`, use `assert` (multiple assertions supported), or crash — crashes are caught via the new `__try_call` runtime builtin and reported as shrinkable failures (option-(b) crash-shrinking from the design discussion). `Check.all_with(gen, prop, config)` overrides `num_runs`, `seed`, `max_shrink_steps`, `max_size`.
  - **Seed reproducibility**: seed priority is `CheckConfig.seed` > `MARCH_PROP_SEED` env > Unix time. CLI flag `--seed=N` wired through both `march test --seed=N` and `forge test --seed=N`, setting the env var before the test binary runs. A failing property's output includes the seed used, so reruns are a copy-paste.
  - **Runtime additions**: `__try_call(thunk : Bool -> a) -> Result(a, String)` primitive catches `Assert_failure`, `Match_failure`, `Eval_error`, `Division_by_zero`, `Stack_overflow`, `Invalid_argument`, and generic exceptions. The `Bool -> a` thunk shape (rather than `() -> a`) works around a typechecker quirk where `() -> T` in argument position fails to unify; the lambda discards its Bool argument with `fn _ -> body`.
  - **Library workarounds for compiler quirks** (worth fixing separately): (a) parser rejects `() -> T` / `Unit -> T` in ADT field types — worked around with `type Thunk(a) = Thunk(Bool -> a)` wrapper; (b) cross-module named type aliases aren't unified with their structural form — `Random.Rng` can't be annotated cross-module, so `Gen` re-declares the same record as `GenRng`; (c) module name `Gen` conflicts with type `Gen(a)` — renamed the type to `Generator(a)` to keep the `Gen.int(...)` API.
  - **Tests**: 14 in `test/stdlib/test_gen.march`, 11 in `test/stdlib/test_check.march`. Covers generator range, seed determinism, shrinking behavior, all combinators, crash-handling, seed reproducibility, multi-assert properties, shrunk counterexample correctness.

- **Seq source constructors — Phase A (TDD).** `stdlib/seq.march` adds the source-end primitives the existing transform/consume API was missing.  All yield the canonical `Seq(a)` so they compose with `map`/`filter`/`take`/`batch`/`to_list`/`fold` like `from_list`.  New API: `from_string_lines(s)` (split by `\n`, trailing-newline-tolerant), `from_file_lines(path)` and `from_file_chunks(path, size)` (lazy pull, auto-close on EOF, return `Result(Seq, FileError)`), `repeat(x, n)`, `iterate(seed, step, n)` (yields seed first), `cycle(seq, n)` (re-folds source per cycle — not safe for one-shot file/http sources), `batched(seq, n)` (alias for existing `batch`), `unfold_until(seed, next, max)` (bounded `unfold`).  Documented limitation: church-encoded folds drive the source to EOF even when the consumer is `take(_, k)`; a true pull-stream representation (`Step = Done | Yield(a, () -> Stream)`) is the planned Phase B follow-up, also needed for `from_http`/`from_channel` with real back-pressure.  Tests written first (red-then-green TDD): 21 new tests in `test/stdlib/test_seq.march` covering every new constructor plus the lazy-take and EOF-close paths.  test_seq 101 → 122; test_stdlib_march 18 → 19 (now wires `test_seq.march` into the runner).
- **DateTime timezones — Phase A (fixed offsets).** Two new ADTs in `stdlib/datetime.march`: `Tz(name, offset_seconds)` and `LocalDateTime(civil, tz)`.  Constructors: `utc_zone()`, `fixed_zone(name, secs)`, `fixed_zone_hours(h)`, `fixed_zone_hm(h, m)` (auto-derives ISO label like `"+05:30"`).  `format_offset(tz)` renders `"Z"` for UTC and `"+HH:MM"`/`"-HH:MM"` otherwise — sub-minute offsets ignored (no real zone uses them since 1972).  Construction from UTC: `local_of(dt, tz)`, `local_now(tz)`, `local_from_timestamp(ts, tz)`, `local_from_utc(utc_dt, tz)`.  Conversion: `local_to_utc`, `local_to_timestamp`, `local_with_zone(ldt, new_tz)` (same instant rendered in a different zone).  `local_format` extends `format`'s spec set with `%z` (compact `+HHMM`), `%:z` (ISO `+HH:MM`/`Z`), `%Z` (zone name).  `parse_offset` accepts ISO 8601 with explicit suffix: `2024-01-15T10:30:45Z`, `2024-01-15 10:30:45+05:30`, and the compact `±HHMM` form; rejects strings without an offset.  DST-aware IANA zones (`America/New_York` etc.) deliberately deferred to Phase B since they need either tzdata loading or hard-coded transition rules; the fixed-offset primitive shipping now is the substrate that approach will sit on top of.  11 new tests in `test_march` `stdlib_datetime` group; `test_march` 1330 → 1341.
- **Perceus RC field-escape fix (borrow inference).** Fixes two observable symptoms: (1) `local RC underflow at 0x... — aborting` in compiled code that pattern-matches a heap-allocated ADT and then uses the extracted field in an owning position (e.g. passed to a function); (2) "second read returns stale data" — two independent scans of a `List` extracted from an ADT in the same branch, where the second scan sees a freed / aliased list.  Root cause: `Borrow.owned_in` was marking a scrutinee as only *borrowing* when a `br_var` with a placeholder `TVar "_"` (which `Lower` always stamps on pattern-bound vars, regardless of concrete constructor field type) escaped in an owning position.  Since `needs_rc(TVar "_")` is false, the old check filtered the `br_var` out and concluded the parent was only being read.  The caller then never incref'd, the field was passed owned without rc transfer, and subsequent use of the parent decref'd a cell whose rc was already 0.  Fix: a new `field_escape_owns` branch in `owned_in` — if the scrutinee itself needs RC and any `br_var` escapes in an owning position in the branch body, the scrutinee is owning.  Follows through `let v = x in ...` alias chains to avoid over-promoting the common `match conn do Conn(s) -> println(s) end` read-only pattern.  Bastion's `FallbackController.call_json` test suite and `TypedMiddleware.full_pipeline` test pass again against the fixed compiler.
- **UUID v7 (RFC 9562) — time-ordered UUIDs.** `UUID.v7()` generates timestamp-ordered UUIDs: 48-bit Unix ms timestamp, 4-bit version=7, 12 random bits, 2-bit variant=`10`, 62 random bits.  `UUID.v7_at(unix_ms)` pins the timestamp (useful for backfilling old records and deterministic tests).  `UUID.timestamp_ms(uuid)` extracts the embedded ms stamp, returning `None` for non-v7 UUIDs.  String-sort of v7 UUIDs exactly matches timestamp-sort order — the entire reason to pick v7 over v4 for DB primary keys (much better B-tree locality on writes).  Random bytes come from `/dev/urandom` (CSPRNG).  Runtime additions: `uuid_v7`, `uuid_v7_at`, `unix_time_ms` builtins + typecheck signatures.  5 new tests in `test_march` `uuid stdlib` group; `test_march` 1315 → 1320.
- **Random non-uniform distributions.** `Random.standard_normal(rng)` via Box-Muller; `Random.normal(rng, mu, sigma)` with sigma=0 shortcut and sigma<0 panic; `Random.exponential(rng, lambda)` via inverse-CDF (`-ln(U)/lambda`); `Random.bernoulli(rng, p)`; `Random.choice(rng, xs)` uniform; `Random.choice_weighted(rng, List((a, Float)))` with cumulative scan.  All samplers pure: `(value, new_rng)` following existing `Random` style.  Parameters validated (negative sigma panics, non-positive lambda panics, p∉[0,1] panics, negative or all-zero weights panic).  13 new tests in `test/stdlib/test_random.march` — determinism checks, sigma=0 shortcut, law-of-large-numbers mean-near-zero on 500 N(0,1) samples, weight-collapse behaviour (`[0, 100, 0]` always picks middle).
- **Stats.quantile — Hyndman & Fan's 9 methods.** `Stats.quantile(xs, q, method)` implements all nine standard interpolation rules that R's `quantile(type=k)` and numpy's `quantile(method=...)` expose: `InvertedCdf`, `AveragedInvertedCdf`, `ClosestObservation`, `InterpolatedInvertedCdf`, `Hazen`, `Weibull`, `Linear` (R/numpy/Excel default), `MedianUnbiased` (distribution-free), `NormalUnbiased` (normal-theory).  Batched `Stats.quantiles(xs, qs, method)` sorts once and reuses.  `Stats.iqr(xs, method)` returns Q3-Q1; `Stats.iqr_default(xs)` uses `Linear`.  `Stats.five_number_summary(xs, method)` returns `(min, Q1, median, Q3, max)`.  `Stats.quantile_default` is a Linear alias consistent with the existing `percentile`.  18 new tests cross-checked against R reference values.  `stats.march` and `test.march` now join the stdlib test-runner manifest.
- **Test.assert_eq — generic structural assertion.** Single generic `Test.assert_eq(expected, actual, msg)` using March's structural `==` (which falls through to OCaml equality when no `Eq` impl is registered).  Works on ints, strings, bools, floats, tuples, records, ADT variants, lists, and arbitrary nestings.  Failure messages render both sides via `to_string`.  `Test.assert_ne` counterpart.  The existing type-specific `assert_eq_int` / `assert_eq_str` / `assert_eq_bool` remain — `assert_eq` is additive.  9 new tests in `test/stdlib/test_test.march` (the first tests for the `Test` module itself).
- **Logger v2 — structured fields, tracing, pluggable appenders.** Replaces the v1 `println`-with-severity logger.  Backward-compatible: existing `Logger.info`, `Logger.with_context`, `Logger.set_level` keep working unchanged.
  - **Structured fields**: new `LogValue = LStr | LInt | LFloat | LBool | LAtom | LNull` ADT and `LogField` record.  Convenience constructors `Logger.s/i/f/b/a` so `Logger.with_field("user_id", Logger.i(42))` reads cleanly.  Formatters preserve types instead of stringifying — JSON renders `"user_id":42`, not `"user_id":"42"`.
  - **Scoped context**: `with_field`, `with_fields`, `current_fields`, `field_count`, and `with_scope(fields, thunk)` for auto-cleanup.  `with_scope` is implemented via the runtime's `try_finally` primitive, so fields pop even when the thunk panics.
  - **Pluggable appenders**: `Appender = Appender(name, LogEntry -> Unit)`.  `add_appender / remove_appender / clear_appenders / list_appenders`.  Built-ins: `appender_stderr`, `appender_stdout`, `appender_file(path, formatter)`, `appender_callback(name, on_entry)` for OTLP exporters or test harnesses.  Multiple appenders fire in registration order; one appender failing doesn't stop the others (errors logged to stderr).  Empty registry falls back to v1 stderr text format so the logger remains useful out of the box.
  - **Built-in formatters**: `format_text` (the v1 `[LEVEL] msg {k=v}` shape), `format_logfmt` (Heroku/Grafana style with proper string quoting), `format_json` (single-line, OpenTelemetry-friendly: reserved keys `trace_id`, `span_id`, `parent_span_id` go to the top level of the JSON object; everything else under `attributes`).
  - **Per-module level overrides**: `set_module_level("MyApp.Worker", Debug)` enables debug for one module while the global level stays at Info.  `level_for(module)` returns the effective level.  `log_in(module, level, msg, fields)` consults the per-module level and tags the entry's `source` field with the module name.
  - **Tracing helpers**: `with_trace_context(trace_id, span_id, parent)`, `current_trace_id`, `current_span_id`, `current_parent_span_id`.  `with_span(name, thunk)` generates a fresh span_id (via the new CSPRNG-backed `Crypto.random_hex`), inherits the current trace_id (or starts a new one), demotes the previous span_id to `parent_span_id`, and pops everything on return or panic.  Nested `with_span` calls produce correctly-parented entries with one trace_id per call tree.
  - **Field deduplication**: when nested scopes push the same key, formatters render only the most-recent push (head of stack).  Prevents the "outer + inner span both appear in JSON" artifact that would otherwise confuse log shippers.
  - **Runtime side**: `lib/eval/eval.ml` adds `log_value` ADT, `logger_fields` field stack, `logger_appenders` registry, `logger_module_levels` Hashtbl, and 11 new builtins (`logger_add_field`, `logger_field_count`, `logger_pop_to_depth`, `logger_get_fields`, `logger_register_appender`, `logger_remove_appender`, `logger_clear_appenders`, `logger_appender_names`, `logger_dispatch`, `logger_set_module_level`, `logger_clear_module_level`, `logger_module_level`).  The dispatch builtin invokes appender callbacks via `apply_hook` and never lets one appender's failure stop another.
  - **Spec**: `specs/2026-04-14-logger-v2-design.md`.  **Phase 5 plan**: `specs/2026-04-14-stdlib-phase5-plan.md`.
  - **Tests**: 4 new (typed `with_field`, `with_scope` clean exit, `with_scope` clean panic exit, module-level override).  `test_march` 1311 → 1315.  All other suites green.
- **Stdlib hardening Phase 4 — medium polish.** Twelve fixes addressing API ergonomics, missing functionality, and one security bug discovered along the way.
  - **Option/Result panic messages**: `Option.expect` and `Result.expect` now prefix the panic with the module name; `Result.expect` includes the underlying `Err` value via `to_string`, so debugging panics shows *why* the assumption was violated, not just that it was.
  - **String docs**: `String.codepoint_count` added as the canonical name (matches what the function actually counts); `grapheme_count` retained as a documented legacy alias.  Header comment about graphemes corrected.  Stale `${n}` interpolation reference removed from `from_int`.
  - **Option.zip param shadowing**: parameters renamed `oa`, `ob` so they no longer shadow the type variables `a`, `b`.
  - **Map default comparators**: added `Map.int_cmp`, `Map.str_cmp`, `Map.from_list_int`, `Map.from_list_string` so callers don't need to write `fn a -> fn b -> a < b` at every Map.* call site.
  - **Json.get_path + Json.get_at**: new `JsonPath = Key(String) | Index(Int)` ADT and `Json.get_path(jv, path)` for traversal through arrays as well as objects (`jv.items[0].name` style).  `Json.get_at(arr, i)` returns the i-th array element as `Option(JsonValue)`.  `get_in` retained for object-only paths.
  - **CSV CRLF documentation**: `Csv` module header now explicitly documents that the parser accepts CRLF, LF, and bare CR as record separators interchangeably; trailing CR/LF stripped; quoted fields preserve embedded CR/LF.  Verified with a mixed-terminator round-trip test.
  - **Regex options**: new `RegexOpts = { case_insensitive: Bool, multiline: Bool }` record, `Regex.default_opts()`, `Regex.matches_opts(pattern, string, opts)`.  `case_insensitive` is implemented (ASCII fold of both pattern and input); `multiline` reserved for future work and currently a no-op.
  - **Strict Base64 decoding**: `base64_decode` runtime builtin rewritten to enforce RFC 4648 strictness — input length must be a multiple of 4, padding must be 0/1/2 trailing `=`, all other characters must be in the alphabet.  The previous implementation crashed on invalid bytes (`Char.chr` exception from negative arithmetic) and accepted ill-formed input.
  - **Uri.merge_strict**: new strict variant of `merge` that returns `Err("...")` if dot-segment resolution would pop above the base (would otherwise be silently absorbed per RFC 3986 §5.2.4).  Module name normalised from `URI` to `Uri` so the all-caps acronym doesn't trip the camel-to-snake filename mapping.  `bin/main.ml` `stdlib_file_list` extended to actually load `uri.march`, `base64.march`, `native_array.march`, `task.march`, `forge_nb.march` (all five had been silently absent from the load order).
  - **CSPRNG fix (security)**: `random_bytes` and `stdlib_random_bytes` runtime builtins were using OCaml's `Random.int 256` — a non-cryptographic PRNG — despite the `Crypto` module documenting them as suitable for keys/nonces/salts/tokens.  Both now read from `/dev/urandom` for true OS CSPRNG output; negative lengths panic with a clear message.  This is the single highest-severity Phase 4 finding.
  - **Sort consolidation**: `Sort.sort(xs, cmp)` added as the canonical entrypoint, dispatching on input size — `sort_small_by` for n < 16, `insertion_sort_by` for n < 64, `timsort_by` for n ≥ 64.  Existing entrypoints (`mergesort_by`, `timsort_by`, `heapsort_by`, `introsort_by`, `sort_small_by`, `insertion_sort_by`) retained for callers that want a specific algorithm.  `Enum.sort_by` now delegates to `Sort.sort` (was hardcoded to `Sort.timsort_by`).
  - **Prelude name audit**: documented in the prelude header that the auto-imported `head`, `tail`, `length`, `reverse`, `fold_left`, `filter`, `map` are all List-typed (no `String.length` exists, so no silent type confusion); `unwrap` / `unwrap_or` operate on `Option`.  No actual collisions were found — the duplication is intentional convenience.
- **Stdlib hardening Phase 2 — convention migrations across the stdlib.** Four mechanical sweeps to bring every module in line with the project's coding conventions.
  - **2a. Indentation sweep** (54 files): `mod Foo do ... end` bodies now indent two spaces.  Of 66 stdlib modules, 54 were 0-space-indented and got reformatted; the 12 already-2-space modules were left alone.  A small Python script (`/tmp/reformat_mod2.py`) handled the bulk transform; `system.march` and four others used the `end -- mod Foo` trailing-comment style and needed an extension to the script.  All test suites still green after the sweep.
  - **2b. Doctest format conversion**: every fenced ` ```march ` doctest block in stdlib doc strings converted to the `march>` prompt style — the only canonical form per the project policy.  Only `list.march` had fenced blocks (11 of them); all other modules were already using `march>`.  Conversion preserves multi-expression blocks as multiple prompt/expected pairs.  Inline `Func(arg)  -- result` illustrations in doc bodies were left as-is — they're prose examples, not runnable doctests.
  - **2c. Error-signaling policy migration**: stdlib was already largely policy-compliant after Phase 1 (file errors structured, datetime parser returning Result, etc.).  Added `Char.digit_value_opt` and `Char.hex_digit_value_opt` returning `Option(Int)` for the lookup-miss arm of the policy; the panicking `digit_value` / `hex_digit_value` remain for callers asserting "I know this is a digit".
  - **2d. Uncurried-collection callbacks**: every `fold` / `fold_left` / `scan_left` in the stdlib migrated to the `(collection, init, f)` argument order with uncurried 2-arg callbacks.  Affected definitions: `List.fold_left`, `List.fold_right`, `List.scan_left`, `Array.fold_left`, `Prelude.fold_left`, `Enum.fold`, `Iterable.fold`, `Map.fold`, `Set.fold`, `Hamt.fold`, `NativeArray.fold_int`, `NativeArray.fold_float`.  All ~30 stdlib call sites updated via a paren-balancing Python script (`/tmp/swap_fold_args.py`); test sites in `test_march.ml` and `test/stdlib/test_*.march` updated.  Map/Set/Hamt `fold` keep the curried per-element callback (`f(acc)(elem) = new_acc`) because internal HAMT helpers expect it; only the OUTER argument order moved.  `OrderedMap.fold`, `SortedSet.fold`, `Seq.fold` were already collection-first.
  - All test suites still green: `test_march` 1311, `test_stdlib_march` 15, `test_properties` 36.
- **Stdlib hardening Phase 3 — numerical stability, quadratic-operation fixes, and concurrency hardening.** Builds on Phase 1 with algorithmic and performance improvements across numerical, collection, and concurrency code.
  - **Numerical**: `Stats.variance` / `variance_pop` rewritten to Welford's online algorithm (shared `welford` helper); numerically stable for near-constant data where the naive two-pass sum-of-squares suffered catastrophic cancellation. Verified: `variance([1e9+1, 1e9+2, 1e9+3]) == 1.0`. `DateTime.parse` now validates calendar ranges via a new `validate_datetime` helper — rejects month 13, day 32, hour 24, second 60, and leap-year-aware day bounds (Feb 29 OK in 2024, rejected in 2023). `BigInt.to_string` switched from O(k²) repeated `++` to reverse-accumulate-then-`string_join`, making it O(k) in limb count; base-10000 per-limb representation was already correct (review overstated as "repeated div 10"). `Decimal.div_round` added for half-up rounding; the existing truncating `div` now documents the convention.
  - **Numerical findings reclassified**: `stats.mode` tie-break is already deterministic (returns smallest value by sorted traversal); `random.shuffle` is a correct inside-out Fisher-Yates (no off-by-one); `random.sample` is shuffle+take which is correct, just O(n²) on lists; `BigInt.div/mod` single-limb path was already correct for negatives (truncating toward zero; remainder same sign as dividend; verified `a == q*b+r` identity), multi-limb panics with improved message documenting the 10^9 bound.
  - **Quadratic fixes**: `Base64.to_mime_lines` rewritten to `string_join` on a reverse-accumulated list of chunks (was O(n²) via `result ++ "\r\n" ++ chunk` in the inner loop). `Enum.uniq_ord(xs, cmp)` added as an O(n log n) variant backed by `Set.contains` / `insert` — opt-in for users whose element type has a natural ordering; the existing `uniq` / `uniq_by` remain for typed-equality-only cases. `List.concat` verified O(n) (review overstated); `Map.from_list` verified O(n log n) via HAMT inserts.
  - **Concurrency**: `HttpServer.send_resp` now panics when called on an already-halted conn — double-response is always a handler bug and the previous silent-overwrite behaviour masked it. `PubSub.topic_matches` made segment-aware: `"user*"` no longer matches `"users"` or `"user_other"` (the char beyond the pattern prefix must be a segment separator `.`/`:` or end-of-string); retains wildcard behaviour for `"room:*"` matching any `"room:..."` topic, and bare `"*"` still matches everything. 12-case regression test passes.
  - **Resource handling**: `File.read` / `write` / `append` / `delete` now classify errors into `NotFound(path)`, `Permission(path)`, `IsDirectory(path)`, `NotEmpty(path)`, or `IoError(msg)` instead of collapsing everything into `IoError`. Two new OCaml helpers in `lib/eval/eval.ml` — `file_error_of_unix` maps `Unix.error` codes (ENOENT, EACCES/EPERM, EISDIR, ENOTEMPTY), `file_error_of_sys` does best-effort substring matching on `Sys_error` messages as a fallback. Verified: `File.read("/nonexistent")` → `NotFound`, `File.read("/tmp")` → `IsDirectory`.
  - All test suites green: `test_march` 1311, `test_stdlib_march` 15, `test_properties` 36.
- **Stdlib hardening Phase 1 — security and correctness blockers resolved.** Seven security fixes and nine correctness fixes landed atop the Phase 0 cleanup.
  - **Crypto security**: `crypto.secure_compare` rewritten to walk both strings to `max(|a|, |b|)` without early-exiting on length mismatch (closes length leak; algorithmic constant-time). `crypto.hash_password` PBKDF2 iteration default raised from 100 000 → 600 000 (OWASP 2023 SHA-256 floor); existing hashes remain verifiable because the count is stored in the hash string.
  - **HTTP security**: `Http.set_header` now sanitizes CR/LF/NUL from both header name and value (blocks CRLF-injection / response-splitting / request-smuggling). `HttpClient` redirects strip `Authorization`/`Cookie`/`Proxy-Authorization` when the target origin (scheme+host+port, case-insensitive) differs from the request origin. `HttpTransport` refuses `https://` requests with a new `SchemeNotSupported` error variant rather than silently downgrading to plaintext TCP.
  - **SVG XSS**: `Plot.svg_text_el` and the y-axis rotated-label emitter now run user strings through a new `svg_escape` helper (`<`, `>`, `&`, `"`, `'`) before embedding in SVG. Prevents XSS when plot output is served to a browser.
  - **JSON**: header comment type declaration reformatted (one variant per line) — actual `type JsonValue` declaration was already correct; the review finding had overstated this.
  - **List correctness**: `List.dedup` now uses structural equality (`h == prev`) instead of `to_string`-based comparison, which was wrong for tuples/records/floats. `List.member` docstring corrected (had been copy-pasted from `dedup`). `List.flat_map` rewritten to prepend-reverse, O(n) in total output size (was O(n²) via repeated `append`).
  - **UUID v5**: two bugs fixed. (a) `bytes_to_uuid_str` dash-position off-by-one produced `6-4-4-4-14` instead of `8-4-4-4-12`. (b) The SHA-1 input construction routed namespace bytes through `char_from_int`, which returns `""` for values > 0x7F — corrupting every v5 output. The fix builds a `Bytes(...)` containing `ns_bytes ++ name_bytes` and passes it directly to `sha1_bytes`. v5 now matches the RFC 4122 DNS and URL reference vectors (verified against Python `uuid.uuid5`).
  - **File resource safety**: `File.with_lines` and `File.with_chunks` now close the fd even if the callback panics, via a new `try_finally(action, cleanup)` builtin (registered in both `lib/eval/eval.ml` and `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`; signature `∀a b. (_ -> a) -> (_ -> b) -> a`; OCaml impl runs cleanup in a try/except then re-raises). Regression test added.
  - **BigInt clarifications**: single-limb `div`/`mod` were already correct (truncating toward zero, remainder-same-sign-as-dividend); verified with `div(-17,3) == -5`, `mod_(-17,3) == -2`, identity `a == q*b+r` holds. Multi-limb divisors panic with an improved message documenting the 10^9 limb bound. Separate `BigInt.compare` stack-overflow on trivial inputs was discovered and spawned as a side task (pre-existing, not introduced by this work).
  - **Bytes**: `hex_digit`'s two branches both return String already — the review finding was incorrect; roundtrip `Bytes.to_hex(Bytes.from_hex("ff007f"))` works.
  - **DataFrame**: `make_df` now panics on ragged column lists (programmer-error → panic per policy). `from_columns` remains as the `Result`-returning safe variant for caller-controlled input.
  - **Channel findings spawned as side tasks**: `channel_socket` registering `transport_pid` as both channel and transport owner is either an intentional "transport handles channels" design or a missing per-channel-actor implementation; requires design clarification. `channel_server` dispatch atoms (`:presence_diff` etc.) were a false positive — those atoms don't appear in the stdlib.
- **Structured concurrency — cancellation tokens + Task.race/any/all_settled/scope.** Signal-based preemption daemon (`SIGUSR1` via `pthread_kill`, 1 ms quantum) in `runtime/march_scheduler.{h,c}`.  Ref-counted `march_cancel_token` struct with `march_sched_spawn_with_cancel`.  Eval: `VCancelToken of bool ref` value variant, `te_cancelled` flag on `task_entry`, five new builtins (`task_cancel_token_new`, `task_cancel`, `task_is_cancelled`, `task_spawn_with_cancel`, `task_cancel_by_id`).  Typecheck: `CancelToken` type, `task_cancel_by_id` poly1 signature.  Stdlib `Task`: `Task.race` (first-in-list wins, losers marked cancelled), `Task.any` (first Ok wins; all-Err → `Err(reasons_list)`), `Task.all_settled` (collect all; no short-circuit), `Task.scope` (Phase 1 pass-through).  24/24 tests in `test/stdlib/test_task.march` pass.  10 new alcotest cases; `test_march` 1320 → 1330.
- **`forge refactor` — project-wide refactoring CLI.** New parser-based engine `lib/refactor/` (library `march_refactor`) + `forge refactor` subcommands (`forge/lib/cmd_refactor.ml`). Discovers every project `.march` file, parses the raw AST (edits real constructs, never strings/comments), applies byte-offset edits in descending order, and re-parses each edited file before writing (never emits unparseable source). A `site`-tagged name visitor powers `--kind` filtering. Commands: **rename** (project-wide incl. qualified `Mod.x`; `--pattern` regex; `--dry-run`), **fix** (camelCase functions → snake_case project-wide via the lint naming helpers), **move** (relocate a top-level declaration to another file, module-wrapping a new dest), **replace** (structural `$metavariable` codemod over call-shaped expressions), and **bundle** (introduce a parameter object: bundle a function's params into a generated record and rewrite the signature, body, and all call sites project-wide). 9-case test suite `test/refactor/test_refactor.ml`. Limitations: rename is name-based not scope-resolved; bundle requires single-clause annotated functions (recovers call-arg source by splitting paren text on top-level commas, since string-literal spans only cover the opening quote).
- **`forge lint` + `forge check` — static analysis and fast type-check.** New `march_lint` library (`lib/lint/`) with 21 rules across 5 categories (naming, style, safety, dead-code, actors). `forge lint --strict --all --json` invoked via `forge/lib/cmd_lint.ml`; reads per-project `.march-lint.toml`. `forge new` scaffold creates `.march-lint.toml` template. LSP diagnostic codes migrated to descriptive slug format (`dead-code/unreachable-after-diverge`, `dead-code/unused-private-fn`, `perf/non-tail-call`, `perf/closure-capture`, `perf/actor-send-copy`, `perf/stack-promoted`, `perf/fbip-reuse`, `perf/indirect-call`). Machine-readable language spec at `docs/coding-standards.md`. `forge check` (`forge/lib/cmd_check.ml`) runs parse → desugar → typecheck only (no codegen) for fast CI iteration; `--check` flag exposed on the march binary itself. `test_lsp.exe` 147 tests all passing after slug migration.
- **Bug fixes #35, #36 — ADT structural equality + multi-module test discovery.** (1) `llvm_emit.ml`: `==`/`!=` on heap-allocated ADT values was emitting `icmp eq ptr` (pointer comparison) instead of structural comparison. Added `ensure_adt_eq_fn` which generates per-concrete-type structural equality LLVM functions using `ctor_info` field layout; `is_int_cmp` handler now detects TCon pointer values for `==`/`!=` and emits calls to the generated functions. Handles tag comparison + per-field comparison (Int/Bool/Unit: icmp eq i64, Float: fcmp oeq, String: march_string_eq, nested ADTs: recursive). Polymorphic types resolved via `apply_ty_subst`. (2) `lower.ml`: `collect_tests` silently skipped `DMod` entries, so tests in MARCH_LIB_PATH modules were never discovered when a different file was the entry point. Added `| Ast.DMod (_, _, inner_decls, _) -> collect_tests prefix inner_decls`. Two regression tests added (`adversarial-regressions` group): `#35 ADT == emits structural eq fn not icmp eq ptr` and `#36 collect_tests descends into DMod`.
- **Test count**: `test_march.exe` 1409 (+8: #35/#36 regressions +2; Phase 5.1 Logger v2 +4; Phase 5.4 UUID v7 +5; structured concurrency +10; Phase 5.6 DateTime tz +11; Perceus scrut-escape rewrite regression +1; uniform int tagging +2 llvm_emit IR tests; Perceus/@__/let-generalization regressions +5; Compress stdlib +24; Show/println generic dispatch +7). No new tests for the `println(42)` JIT segfault fix (covered by existing `repl_compiler_parity` and `repl_jit_regression` suites).  `test_stdlib_march.exe` 25 (Phase 5: +4 for stats, random, test, seq; +6 serialization: xml, toml, msgpack, yaml, socket, dns. test_seq.march itself grew 101 → 122 with the Phase 5.7 Seq source constructors; yaml 36 tests, xml 40 tests, socket 9 tests, dns 11 tests, toml 157 tests, msgpack 195 tests). `test_lsp.exe` 147 (slug migration). `test_properties.exe` 36.  All green.
- **Compress stdlib** — `stdlib/compress.march`: gzip, deflate, zstd, and brotli sub-modules with one-shot encode/decode and streaming variants. Compression levels (`BestSpeed`, `Default`, `BestCompression`, `Custom(Int)` for gzip; `Fast(Int)`, `Default`, `Best` for zstd; `Level(Int)` 0–11 for brotli). HTTP helpers `accept_encoding/1` and `best_encoding/1`. C FFI via `lib/eval/compress_stubs.c` (OCaml CAMLprim stubs, zlib always-on, zstd/brotli behind `#ifdef MARCH_HAVE_ZSTD`/`MARCH_HAVE_BROTLI`) and `runtime/march_compress.c` (native compiler shims). 24 alcotest tests including round-trip property tests with `Gen.list(Gen.int(0, 255))`.
- **Serialization stdlib** — six new stdlib modules spanning binary, text, and network serialization: `stdlib/msgpack.march` (MessagePack binary encoding/decoding, ~195 tests), `stdlib/toml.march` (TOML v1.0 subset parser, ~157 tests), `stdlib/xml.march` (non-validating XML parser + emitter with `~xml` sigil, ~40 tests), `stdlib/yaml.march` (pure-March YAML 1.2 subset parser with `~yaml` sigil, ~36 tests), `stdlib/socket.march` (TCP socket wrapper over `tcp_connect`/`tcp_send_all`/`tcp_recv_all`/`tcp_close` builtins, ~9 tests), `stdlib/dns.march` (DNS resolver wrapper over new `dns_resolve` builtin using `Unix.getaddrinfo`, ~11 tests). All 6 modules wired into `bin/main.ml`, `test/test_stdlib_march.ml`, and the `~xml`/`~yaml` sigils into `stdlib/sigil.march`.
- **Stdlib Bastion-removal cleanup (Phase 0)** — `stdlib/correlation.march` removed (it was a Bastion request-correlation middleware, not language-level). `bin/main.ml` `stdlib_module_names` registry pruned of stale entries (`Islands`, `BastionDev`, `BastionCookies`, `BastionRoutes`, `BastionPubSub`, `BastionCSP`) that had been left behind when the Bastion modules themselves moved out of `stdlib/`. Stale comments about "multi-file Bastion projects" and "Islands from bastion/lib/islands.march" updated to neutral language. `test/test_stdlib_march.ml` updated: removed `correlation.march` from the `all_stdlib_decls` eager-parse list and dropped the CorrelationId test case. `test/stdlib/test_correlation.march` deleted. `html.march` and `sigil.march` kept — they are dual-use (HTML escape is needed by Plot/SVG, sigils are a language feature). Stdlib now sits at 65 modules.
- **Import deduplication in `resolve_imports`** — `bin/main.ml`: after loading explicit imports (step 1), all resolved modules and their nested transitive deps are now marked as "already-emitted" in the `resolved` cache (set to `[]`). Previously, every conduit submodule that did `import Conduit` would embed the entire Conduit DMod (~80 declarations) inside itself as a nested DMod. The typechecker re-processed those 80 declarations inside EVERY submodule, causing exponential cost: each successive module took 2x longer to typecheck, hanging on module #102 after 17+ minutes. The fix is a 12-line `mark_emitted_decls` recursive pass after `explicit_decls`. Combined with removing the `import Conduit` self-import from all 37 conduit submodule files, the conduit test app now compiles in **0.3 seconds** (down from 17+ minutes, ~3400x speedup). Also removed debug instrumentation (timing prints, step counters) added during profiling.
- **O(log n) typecheck environment** — `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`: replaced all four `env` association-list fields (`vars : (string * scheme) list`, `types : (string * int) list`, `ctors : (string * ctor_info) list`, `records : (string * ...) list`) with `Map.Make(String).t` (module `StrMap`). Previously each lookup was O(n) with n = total declarations loaded (stdlib 57 modules + all transitive deps); with conduit → depot chains this produced pathological O(n²) compile times (17+ minutes for a single-function app). After the fix, lookups are O(log n) and the same app typechecks in under a second. `env.ctors` uses `ctor_info list StrMap.t` to preserve ambiguity detection when two types define a constructor with the same name. Updated: `lib/repl/repl.ml`, `lsp/lib/analysis.ml`, `test/test_march.ml`. Three new tests in `typecheck perf` group guard against regression: `large env lookup O(log n)` (500-let module < 2s), `many ctors no quadratic` (200-variant type < 2s), `deep nested modules` (20×20 submodule matrix < 2s).

- **Full pipeline working**: `dune exec march -- file.march` parses → desugars → typechecks → runs `main()` if present
- **Match syntax**: `match expr do Pat -> body end` — no leading `|` on arms; NL separates arms inside match body; multi-statement arms use `-> do ... end` wrapper (2026-03-26 syntax overhaul)
- **String interpolation**: `${}` syntax fully implemented — `INTERP_START`/`INTERP_MID`/`INTERP_END` tokens in lexer; desugars to `++`/`to_string` chains (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml`)

- **JIT codegen: bitwise builtins** — `int_and/int_or/int_xor/int_not/int_shl/int_shr/int_popcount` now work in JIT; fixed missing `builtin_names` entry in `defun.ml` and added inline `EApp` cases in `llvm_emit.ml`
